# pathos.cloud Dashboard UI Reference

> Canonical reference for the dashboard layer. For *how* the dashboard mirrors the API + which architectural role it plays, see [docs/architecture/dashboard.md](../../docs/architecture/dashboard.md). For design rationale, see [docs/concepts/](../../docs/concepts/).

Quasar v2 (Vue 3) | Javascript | SCSS | Webpack | Axios

## Setup (after `npm init quasar`)

When running `npm init quasar` from project root choose:
- App with Quasar CLI
- Project folder: `dashboard`
- Quasar version: Quasar v2 (Vue 3)
- Script type: **Javascript**
- Quasar App CLI variant: **Webpack**
- CSS preprocessor: **Sass with SCSS syntax**
- Features: **Axios, Vue-Router, Pinia, ESLint**
- ESLint preset: Standard

Then replace/add the pre-built files in `src/`.

## Service mirror (src/services/)

Each frontend service is a thin axios wrapper around one backend resource. Conventions:
- Return the raw API payload (`{ success, ...payload }` or `{ success: false, error }`).
- Components handle both shapes inline.
- The axios instance lives in `services/api.js` with a request-interceptor that injects the JWT and a 401 interceptor that clears auth + redirects to `/auth`.

| File | Backs |
|------|-------|
| `api.js` | the axios instance |
| `auth.service.js` | `/api/identity/*` |
| `node.service.js` | `/api/nodes/*` |
| `label.service.js` | `/api/labels/*` |
| `post.service.js` | `/api/posts/*` — **canonical post API client** |
| `nodeInteraction.service.js` | legacy `/api/nodes/:id/{vote,interactions,...}` |
| `skeleton.service.js` | `/api/skeletons/*` — includes `items(id)` for the spine-pairs walk |
| `ref.service.js` | `/api/refs/*` — `summary`, `search`, and `surround(ref, limit)` (the one call behind `ElementSurround`) |
| `verify.service.js` | The truth spine's read surface (Thread B, 2026-07-30): `verify(ref)` → `GET /api/verify/<kind>/<hash>` report for `ProvenanceBadge`, `downloadBundle(ref)` → the export bundle handed to the browser as a JSON download (offline-verifiable via the api repo's `scripts/verify-bundle.mjs`) |
| `entity.service.js` additions (2026-07-30) | `createAlterEgo` grew `inherit {photo, bio}` + `discloseOrigin`; `origin(id)` → the lineage chain (disclosure-gated); `setOriginDisclosure(id, bool)` — the owner's switch |
| `feed.service.js` | `/api/feed/*` |
| `components/shared/GlobalSearch.vue` | **DELETED 2026-08-23** (user ask: "remove the search button and its functionalities"). It was **Thread E's face** (2026-07-30): the NavigationBar search button + top-anchored dialog over `GET /api/search` — content hits (snippet rows → node viewer) + by-name chips (entities/labels/posts → their viewers). Debounced 350ms; empty state names the access doctrine so "nothing" reads as privacy working. ⚠ **`GET /api/search` IS NOT DELETED** — `dashboard/NodeSearchInput.vue` still rides the same FULLTEXT shadow, so the ask removed a face, not a capability; nothing on the server moved. The bar was this component's only mount, so the deletion is the whole removal |
| `components/entities/OriginTree.vue` | **The ORIGIN CONSTELLATION** (2026-07-30): an entity's lineage as a night sky — three drifting CSS starfield layers over deep ink, the chain rendered BOTTOM-UP (you at the bottom, the thread unraveling through alter-egos to the root, then the invite chain climbing to the PIONEER's gold-gradient star). The thread is one repeated SVG bezier whose stroke DRAWS upward (dashoffset animation) staggered per node — the unravel IS the animation; edges captioned "unravels to" (teal) / "invited by" (gold). A concealed edge renders as FOG ("the thread fades here") — the server already refused, the sky just says so. Owners of an alter-ego get the amber disclosure toggle ON the sky. Opened by the "Origin constellation" button on `EntityProfilePage`'s side column |
| `components/entities/AlterEgoPanel.vue` (2026-07-30 window) | The inline creation form became a DIALOG (`.ego-create`): name + bio + **inherit from root** (photo / bio checkboxes) + the **origin disclosure toggle** (amber; default private — the label states the consequence in plain words, both ways). The choice lives as the profile's ORIGIN record; flippable later from the constellation |
| `nav.service.js` | `/api/nav/*` |
| `pin.service.js` | `/api/pins/*` |
| `access.service.js` | `/api/access/*` — `status`, `grant`, `publish`, `tree` (the pre-publish reference tree), `request` (locked chip → owner poll; ships with chats). NOTE: `api.js` is a **default** export — `import api from './api'`, not `{ api }` |
| `chat.service.js` | `/api/chats/*` — `list`, `open(memberIds)` (find-or-create), `feed(chatId)`, `send(chatId, content, shareRefs)` |
| `poll.service.js` | `/api/polls/*` — `vote(pollId, 'yes'\|'no')` (decider-only, final) |
| `claim.service.js` | `/api/claims/*` (Thread D reader surface, 2026-07-31) — `get`, `list`, `vote(id, {stance, ref, note})` (dispute ref mandatory, `<kind>/<hash>` form), `retract`, `create` |
| `embed.service.js` | `/api/embeds/*` — `resolve(url)` (a web link → the `EmbedFrame` descriptor, `embed: null` when no rule matches) and `rules()`. Only for URLs that are not yet nodes: a node read already carries `node.embed` |

## Stores (src/stores/)

| Store | What it holds |
|-------|---------------|
| `auth.js` | `token`, `user`, `systemStatus`. Boot calls `/api/identity/verify`; on 401 the store is cleared and the router redirects to `/auth`. |
| `navigation.js` | Persistent navigation history + checkpoints + the **StateHolder map** (`states`, keyed by route: per-element viewer state persisted via `/api/nav/state`) + back/forward **return-marking** (`lastReturn`, consumed by viewers to halo the element you came back from). Driven by the bottom `NavigationBar`. See [docs/concepts/navigation.md](../../docs/concepts/navigation.md). |
| `maker.js` / `uploader.js` | The two docks' content state: draft/upload tabs (localStorage-persisted) + own window flags (`isOpen`/`isMinimized`/`isMaximized`). Each exports the shared minitab label helper (`draftLabel` / `uploadLabel`). |
| `labelMaker.js` | The label maker dock's window flags + the selected label (session-only — label mutations are single round-trips, nothing to draft). `openAt(label)` opens focused on a given label. |
| `chat.js` | The chat dock's window flags (maker-pattern) + `activeChatId` + per-conversation composer drafts — localStorage `pathos_chat`. |
| `mediaViewers.js` | **RETIRED 2026-08-17 — fused + renamed into `flyoutViewers.js`** (below): the media viewer records grew a TARGET (node / post / ref) and the whole element-flyout family moved in. Same geometry fields, same fit/gesture contracts, `flyout:<id>` window keys. |
| `dashboard.js` | The **dashboard dock's** window flags (2026-08-10), the `labelMaker` pattern: `isOpen`/`isMinimized`/`isMaximized` and nothing else — the panel is empty by design, so there is nothing to draft and nothing to persist. z-order under the `dashboard` key in windows.js, which is what puts it in the same stack as the other five creation docks. `close()` deliberately KEEPS `isMaximized`, the way every dock does: a window you left maximized should come back that way. |
| `flyoutViewers.js` | **The flyout viewer windows** (2026-08-04 as `mediaViewers.js`; **fused + renamed 2026-08-17**, absorbing `flyouts.js` — the single flyout's park-flag store, 2026-08-10→08-17, died in the fusion). N viewers, each `{ id, target ({kind:'node'\|'post'\|'ref'} with the enriched node / feed item / bare ref), rect, natural, minimized, maximized, label, icon }`. `spawnNode`/`spawnPost`/`spawnRef` dedupe per element identity and FRONT an existing window instead of stacking a twin; `retarget` lets a ref window become what it resolved into (same window, same rect, new self); `describe` mirrors the window's resolved name+glyph down for the parked tab; `openPostIds` is what lights the feed cards' triggers. Geometry field names kept from the media era ON PURPOSE — `utils/mediaFit` and `useMediaWindowGestures` are written against exactly that shape. Z-order through `windows.js` via key-agnostic `flyout:<id>` keys; nothing persisted — a viewer window is session furniture. |
| `geo.js` | Location-sharing consent (2026-07-19). `maybeAsk()` (MainLayout onMounted) shows the one-time "Anchor your moments?" dialog; agreeing triggers the browser geolocation prompt and `capture()` stores the coords **city-rounded to 1 decimal** as localStorage `pathos_geo` (`'lat,lon'`; choice remembered in `pathos_geo_choice`). `services/api.js` attaches it to every request as `X-Pathos-Geo`, so the API stamps every moment the user mints. Precise coordinates never leave the browser; the driver pre-declines so scripted flows never see the dialog. |
| `windows.js` | **Window manager** for the bottom-docked windows (`maker`, `uploader`, `skeletonBuilder`, `labelMaker`, `chat`, `dashboard` since 2026-08-10, `stack`, `pins`): z-order stack (`focus`/`release`, `zOf(key)` → 2400+) so a newly opened window covers the previous one and closing/minimizing it reveals that one again. Also owns the stack/pins window flags (`panels.*`, localStorage-persisted, + `openPanel`/`closePanel`/`minimizePanel`/`restorePanel`/`togglePanel`), the **side-column geometry**: `railKeys` (open stack/pins), `railWidth` (the one-column width, kept as a **permanent reserve** — page content pads right by it and nothing reflows when a panel expands; each side bar is ONE element whose parked presentation is the SAME panel narrowed to a thin icon column at the same screen edge, `right: 0` — `is-parked`, 2026-07-24 third pass, the separate rail tree + `railOffsetOf` are gone) and `dockRight` (every OTHER expanded window shifts left by this so it renders *beside* the parked column, via the `--dock-right` CSS var on `.dock-window`), plus `footerPanelInset` (how far the minitab strip shifts left to clear the pins widget — `RAIL_W` when parked, `PANEL_W` when the panel is expanded at the screen edge), and the transient **`split`** state (skeleton builder left + companion maker/uploader right, 50vw each via `.is-split-left`/`.is-split-right`) — **dormant** since the July 2026 schema/instance split (nothing calls `enterSplit` now that instance population moved to the SkeletonPage viewer; the docks just float over the page). |

## Composables (src/composables/)

| Composable | Role |
|------------|------|
| `useStateHolder.js` | ⭐ The per-viewer StateHolder seam: persisted reactive `state` (debounced + flushed on leave), approximate scroll restore (window and/or tracked containers, proportional + height-settled), the luminous-yellow return halo on `[data-nav-focus]` elements, `describe()` (feeds real titles/hashes to the stack panel), `recordInteraction()` (extends the element's pathchain interaction path). Wired into: LabelExplorerPage (dug-open square set), PostViewerPage, NodeDetailPage, LabelDetailPage, SkeletonPage, FileTreePage (re-reveals last address), `posts/FeedStream.vue` (the feed's holder moved out of the page into the stream COMPONENT at the end of 2026-07-25 — `trackScroll: false` + `trackContainer(wellEl, 'feed')`, since on `/feed` the scroller is the container's well and the page never scrolls; `onBeforeRouteLeave` still fires from a child of the routed component — and since 2026-08-06 `state.headY`, where the draggable head box was left standing: an ARRANGEMENT of the surface, not a lens you look through, so coming back from a post to find the head snapped back to the top would read as the drag having been undone. Written on pointer RELEASE, one row per gesture), FeedPageLegacy. |
| `useElementSurround.js` | The surround seam (2026-07-14): one `refService.surround(ref, limit)` call resolving an element's skeleton walk + surround sections (author / createdAt / forkedFrom / labels / versions / forks / comments / scores) for `shared/ElementSurround.vue`. Used by LinkViewerPage, PathViewerPage, LabelDetailPage sidebars. |
| `useMediaArena.js` | **The media viewers' two regions** (2026-08-04; split **2026-08-05** on a user ask) — one module-singleton measurement set (viewport + the real `.media-tabs`/`.nav-footer` boxes, one resize listener — it read `.frieze-header` until that element was deleted on 2026-08-12, and the ceiling moved to the silver rail's `rect.bottom` with it) behind two computeds. `arena {x,y,w,h}` = where a window is **born**: desktop = the RIGHT HALF (below the crown, above the nav footer, left of the stack/pins rail, 14px inset); `windows.isMobile` portrait = the TOP HALF (over the content by design, 8px inset); landscape phone = the whole viewport between the bands — caught by a **height ≤ 500px gate**, because the store's width-only 600px gate reads a rotated phone as desktop. `roam` = where it may **go**: the WHOLE viewport, no inset and no band subtraction. The arena's ceiling is the top chrome's measured `getBoundingClientRect().bottom`, NOT its height (2026-08-05; that chrome is the media-tabs rail since 2026-08-12, the crown strip before it): with the media tabs band parked above it the strip no longer starts at 0, while `friezeH` — which feeds `chromeOf`, i.e. the band drawn INSIDE a viewer — still has to be the height. Spawn fit asks the arena; drag clamps, resize maxima and the stale-rect re-clamp ask the roam. They were ONE region until 08-05, which made the right half a fence — the window could not be pulled over the page it came from, and every rail/orientation change hauled it back. The bands are not carved out of the roam because neither hides what matters: the crown strip paints UNDER a viewer (3000 vs 3010+) and the nav footer can only cover its bottom, so the header stays visible and grabbable — and `clampRect` keeps the whole rect on screen. |
| `useMediaWindowGestures.js` | The media window's drag + resize (2026-08-04) — one Pointer-Events listener set (mouse + touch): bar `pointerdown` (non-button, `closest('button')` guard) = **move**, eight rim handles = **aspect-locked resize** (media ratio from `viewer.natural`; anchor = opposite edge/corner; floor = `minSize()` — the header shrink limit; ceilings = anchor-relative **roam** distances). Every move commits through `store.setRect` → `clampRect` (drag past the boundary pins at the farthest legal spot). The composable **never sees the arena** since 2026-08-05: both gestures clamp to the whole viewport, and they must use the SAME region — an arena-clamped resize of a window parked outside the arena computes a negative ceiling and `clampRect` then teleports it back into the right half. BottomSplitter's hygiene: body userSelect/cursor parked during, listeners torn down on release AND unmount, `is-grabbed`/`is-resizing` kill transitions and light the edges (`--teal-13` ring). Gestures disabled while maximized; a stale rect re-clamps at gesture start. |

## Layouts

| Layout | Used by | What it provides |
|--------|---------|------------------|
| `AuthLayout` | `/auth` | Centered, glass-style auth surface, crowned since 2026-08-12 by the media-tabs SILVER RAIL alone — `FriezeHeader` is deleted from both layouts (user ask) and the page container pads down by `--media-tabs-h`. Formerly: crowned by the fixed `FriezeHeader` (page container padded down by `--frieze-h`) |
| `MainLayout` | every authenticated page | **NO CROWN STRIP SINCE 2026-08-12** (user ask: "remove the brown frieze header from the top of the screen and also the one from the bottom of the screen, below the footer bar") — `FriezeHeader` is deleted, the drawer's `.drawer-frieze--top` and `.drawer-floor-frieze` went with it (they only continued the two screen-edge bands across this column), and the page container pads down by **`--media-tabs-h` alone**, the silver rail being the whole top chrome now. ⭐ **THE DRAWER'S OWN ASKS, 2026-08-18** — (1) `aside.q-drawer` is back on **`top: 0`** (as is `.stack-window` at the other edge), and the silver rail was then raised to **3125** so it draws on top of BOTH columns, partially covering their frieze bands — everything else top-anchored still steps down by `--media-tabs-h`; (2) it **casts no shadow at all** (`box-shadow: none` — the old `6px 0 22px` washed the nav bar, the rail's underside and the page it now lies over, and its `--grey-6 border-right` states the edge alone); (3) the FIRST `.drawer-frieze` is **flush with the window's top edge** (2026-08-18), which took zeroing both Quasar's `.q-list--padding` top and the band's own margin. The footer block's burger is a **rounded square filling its slot** (100% × 100% inside a 4px pad, `--radius-sm`) in a slot painted **transparent**, so the button sits on the drawer's own coat instead of in a darker rail block. Historically it was: fixed `FriezeHeader` crown strip over everything + page container (pads right by `windows.railWidth` so the parked side columns never cover content, and down by `--frieze-h` so content clears the frieze; the **drawer does NOT** — since 2026-07-24 it runs from the screen's top-left corner OVER the crown strip, and **since 2026-08-02 it runs to the WINDOW FLOOR too** (user ask): `bottom: 0` at **`z 3120`**, so the column owns the full window height and LIES OVER the nav bar (3110) instead of stopping at its top edge. That bar was raised above everything on the same day precisely so nothing could paint on it; the drawer is the ONE deliberate exception and it covers no control — the bar's left cluster is empty while the drawer stands (see `.drawer-footer` below) and even expanded the drawer is 220px, far short of the right cluster. It covers the bar's left end as well (and covered the LEFT end of the frieze footer band until that band's 2026-08-04 retirement). Its last `--nav-bar-h` is therefore the bar's own row, and **`.drawer-footer` rebuilds that row inside the drawer** (with a `.drawer-floor-frieze` **FriezeBar under it** until 2026-08-12, un-flipped like the drawer's own section dividers, continuing the floor band across this column — in the crown strip's pale palette since 2026-08-04; it left with the band itself, so the drawer's last pixels are this row's): brown-1 plaque (**the bar's own plaque left `--brown-1` on 2026-08-17 for a `--light-cream` sheet under a 30% `--grey-3` veil, and its top lip left brown-3 for `--grey-6`; this block did NOT follow either — the drawer is one material top to bottom, so the rebuilt row now matches the DRAWER rather than the bar it stands on, and the two `border-top`s landing on the same pixel are two different inks. The geometry below is unchanged**), a brown-3 `border-top` landing on the same pixel as the bar's, and the 41px brown-4 `.drawer-burger-slot` + 1px hairline at its left end holding the 24px inverted-brown chip (28px before the bar shortened) — NavigationBar's `.burger-slot` to the pixel (verified: the chip's box is x 7…35 whichever surface draws it). **THE BURGER LIVES HERE NOW**, and its only job is to CLOSE (`drawer = false`): while the drawer stands there is nothing to open, and when it goes the bar grows its own burger back in exactly that spot. Mini state: the content box is 41px, so the slot's own hairline falls outside it and the drawer's brown-3 `border-right` stands in for it — same ink, same pixel (`overflow: hidden` on the block does the clipping). Quasar's modal `.q-drawer__backdrop` was bumped 2999 → **3115** the same day: under the drawer (3120) but OVER the bar, so on a phone the bar dims with the page instead of poking out of a modal, lit and still clickable. **Collapsed width = `--dock-rail-w` (42px) since 2026-07-31** — it mirrors the stack/pins parked column so the two screen edges wear one rail width each, and the mini rail runs unbroken from the crown strip to the bottom of the screen (its own footer block continuing it now, the nav bar's block only when the drawer is gone). The hard-coded `:mini-width="42"` became **`:mini-width="drawerRailW"`** that day — the token read once in `setup()` (`getComputedStyle(document.documentElement).getPropertyValue('--dock-rail-w')`, fallback 42), because three surfaces now depend on that one number and a drift shows as a step at the bottom-left corner. Note the local `drawerRailW` is NOT `windows.railWidth` (the page container's right reserve for the side widgets); the list is densified to make the 32px chip work: item margins `2px 5px`, `min-height: 40px`, frieze dividers `6px 0`, section headers `6px 14px 3px`. **The IDENTITY section is `profile → Organizations → Log out`, and the profile row is a DENSE IDENTITY BLOCK since 2026-08-02** (`.drawer-profile-item`), not the word "Profile": the feed card's author section — `EntityAvatar` at 28px, display name over `@handle`, the same 1.15 leading that makes two lines read as one stamp, the same `display_name || username || entity #id` fall-back chain — re-coated for the drawer in `--brown-8` ink on the brown-1 plaque (`.drawer-profile-name` 0.92em/700, `.drawer-profile-handle` 0.78em mono at brown-8/.62). It is where the acting identity lives now that the nav bar carries none. `EntityAvatar` gets the auth store's `user` object, which has no `photo` key, so it resolves the face itself through the session-cached `entitySummary` — one request, shared with every other chip for that entity. The **MASK CHIP** (`.drawer-profile-mask` — inverted-brown `theater_comedy` in a 15px circle with a brown-1 ring) floats on the AVATAR's bottom-right corner rather than beside the name, because Quasar's mini drawer keeps only the `avatar` section: acting as an alter-ego has to stay visible in the 42px rail state, which is the state the drawer is in most of the time. `Log out` (`.drawer-logout-item`) came off the nav bar with it and CLOSES the section below the destinations, drawn muted — one loud affordance per drawer, and that one is Back) + bottom `NavigationBar` (the `FriezeFooter` band that stood on its top edge from 2026-07-25 was retired 2026-08-04 — the floor frieze band inside the footer wears its pale palette instead) + the six always-mounted docked windows (`MakerDock`, `UploaderDock`, `SkeletonBuilderDock`, `LabelMakerDock`, `StackPanel`, `PinsDrawer`), z-ordered by `stores/windows.js` + `media/ElementFlyoutHost.vue` (2026-08-04 as MediaViewerHost — the flyout viewer windows and their minimize sliver, media faces included since the 2026-08-17 fusion; see the shared/ rows below). Routes the pin refresh key both ways (tack ↔ drawer), and routes maker/uploader `created` events into an active SkeletonPage populate session's fresh tray (`skeletonBuilder.deliverFresh`) before falling back to post/node navigation. **Mobile pass (Thread H item 1, 2026-07-31, one `600px` breakpoint kept in sync with `MOBILE_MQ` in `stores/windows.js`)**: the drawer model **starts closed under 1024px** — below `show-if-above`'s breakpoint the q-drawer is an OVERLAY, and a `true` model at boot drops its fullscreen `.q-drawer__backdrop` over the page, killing every tap (the menu button toggles it as an overlay instead); `windows.initViewportWatch()` (called here on mount) keeps `windows.isMobile` live, which zeroes `railWidth`/`footerPanelInset` while the CSS hides the stack/pins widgets — so under 600px the layout is **one surface at a time**: full-width feed container (95% of the track) with the flyout overlaying it (2.5% margins), full-SCREEN creation docks (`left/right: 0`, `top: var(--frieze-h)` in `_components.scss` — change request #675), full-width chat dock (its scoped block — a global override loses to scoped specificity) with the conversations column narrowed to `minmax(110px, 34%)`, and the instance editor's field/Populate grid stacked.  **`<router-view @pins-changed="pinsRefreshKey++">`** (2026-08-07) — a page can pin too now (the feed card's cap), and the pins widget that has to reload is a sibling of the page container, not of the page. Vue Router forwards attrs, listeners included, to the matched component, so any page that DECLARES the emit is heard here and the rest are unaffected — same destination as `ElementFlyoutHost`'s tack and the nav bar's. The declaration is load-bearing: an undeclared emit falls through to the page's root element as a DOM listener, where a custom event name never fires (see specs/gotchas.md). **THE DASHBOARD PANEL IS A DOCKED WINDOW HERE (2026-08-10)** — `<DashboardDock />` beside the other seven, and nothing else. It spent one pass as a floating `.dashboard-flyout` slot owned by this file (fixed, `left: 52.5%`, z 3060, two plain refs and an Escape handler of its own); the second ask that day moved it onto the bar with the creative windows, so its flags live in `stores/dashboard.js` like every other dock's — which is what lets the nav bar draw both its button and its minitab without this layout passing anything down. The slot, the refs, the Escape listener, the `dashboard-open` prop and this file's only `@media (max-width: 600px)` block went with it. |

## Pages

| Route | Page | What it does |
|-------|------|-------------|
| `/auth` | `AuthPage` | One frieze-crowned brown plaque (2026-07-29 redesign): metallic wordmark + two-temperature slogan ("mind at rest" mint / "heart ablaze" ember), enter\|join halves side by side (no tabs; hairline divide goes horizontal on wrap), "I've got a secret" invite well w/ mint aurora, mint/ember CTAs, brown-2 field wells. The *enter* half toggles into a **recover** form (Thread H, 2026-07-31 — the `.auth-toggle` dotted-underline text control under the CTA): recovery secret in the same aurora well (`restart_alt` icon, "A vouch from your inviter") + username + new password (min 8) → `authStore.recover` (`POST /identity/recovery/reset`), a success routing straight to `/feed` logged in; head retitles enter→recover, epigraph hides while recovering |
| `/landing` | *(static, not a Vue route)* | The pre-2026-07-29 production landing preserved verbatim under `public/landing/` — history-mode router base + Vite assets base both patched to `/landing/` in the minified bundle, `tomb.png` path fixed; allegue's whim, served beside the SPA. **Its favicon set is the SPA's favicon too since 2026-08-03**: the pathos mark (turbine rosette in a cyan ring) copied byte-identical from `landing/` up to `public/{favicon.ico, icons/favicon-16·32·96·128.png}` and declared in `src/index.template.html` (the template linked `icons/favicon-16/32` for months while `public/icons/` didn't exist — tabs were blank). The landing copy stays byte-frozen; the root set is the app's own |
| `/feed` | `FeedPage` | ⭐⚠ **READ FIRST — THE EDGES ARE THE HEADER RAIL'S COMPOSITION SINCE 2026-08-27** (the ⭐ block at this row's end has the recipe; every bar palette named between here and there is the 08-24 record it superseded). **THE 2026-08-24 PASS INVERTED THIS SURFACE.** Two asks: **(a) the two vertical frieze bars INVERTED** ("let's invert the color pallette: use the background color on the light-cream frieze bar, make the background light-cream, and for the other svg and the borders. use indigo-8") — a **`--light-cream` plate** under **`--indigo-8` on BOTH waves and BOTH edges** (`--frieze-bar-v-lip` too, so each bar is one indigo drawing on a cream strip rather than a plate with a frame of its own), all dialled at `.feed-container__edge`. ⚠ **The CARVE had to go off** (`--frieze-bar-v-carve: none`): FriezeBar's groove reads as relief only when the ink is LIGHTER than its plate, so an inverted band wearing it is lit from the wrong side — the same law the nav bar's trail hit the day it spent an hour inverted, and a LAW here, not a preference. Two notes on that pair of changes: `justify-content: flex-start` is also the **correct anchoring for a horizontal scroller** — under `center`, a content run wider than the track is clipped at the START and unreachable, since `scrollLeft` cannot go negative; and the gap is carried as the ITEM's `margin-left` rather than the track's `padding-left`, because a percentage flex-basis resolves against the flex container's CONTENT box — padding would quietly make the box a percentage of (track − gap) instead of a true track share (verified across widths: 535.2px = 40.00% of a 1338px track, unchanged when the gap went 8px → 10%; 669px = 50.00% with the gap at 5%; measured 40.00% with the 2.50% gap after 2026-07-27's resizes; as a margin both percentages share that one basis and simply add, whereas as padding a 5% gap would shrink the box below its stated share). The whole construction — the tablets/marbles walk, the 0.8px `--key-ring` inset-shadow rim, the board-riding `top`, the glyph-swap pairs — is in git under 2026-08-21, and the lesson is the reason not to regrow controls here: a ~15px frieze has no room to carry anything but its own motif. **THE HORIZONTAL RESIZE survives whole** (a cursor, a title and the rail's silhouette `--indigo-11` glow — it costs the motif nothing): a rail drag moves its own wall and leaves the far one — LEFT rail, right edge fixed, `l` the free variable; RIGHT rail, left edge fixed, `w` the free one. Three clamps: `GAP` 8px of daylight at each end of the TRACK, which is the whole of *"never touch the left drawer / the stack and pin sidebars"* because `q-page-container` already pads the track by both (nothing here reads a drawer or rail width, and nothing should start to); **`min-width: 25vw`** in the stylesheet as the guarantee (15% for the first hours of 2026-08-18) with the same number clamped in JS so the gesture STOPS at the floor; and the slot's own width as the ceiling. **THE UNITS ARE FRACTIONS OF THE TRACK, never px** — `widthPct`/`leftPct`, `null` until someone drags, which leaves the 45%/2.5% defaults in charge — so the box follows the slot when a dock opens or the window resizes exactly as those two defaults always have; a `ResizeObserver` on the track re-clamps only what is NOT a percentage (the 8px gaps and the 25vw floor). Persisted in `localStorage` under `pathos_feed_geometry` — NOT the StateHolder that carries `headY`, and deliberately: a holder is keyed by route, this page and `FeedStream` are the SAME route, and two holders on one key are two hands on one dial. ⭐ **2026-08-27 — THE EDGES ARE THE HEADER RAIL'S COMPOSITION** (user ask: "take the top header frieze bar and the whole header structure and … replace the friezes from the main feed container with the same kind of composed component"): each `.feed-container__rail` is now the COAT-BAR (`--plaque-coat` face, `padding: 0 1px` — the header's lead/pad turned, so the band is INLAID with coat showing both sides — and a 1px `--grey-6` rim on the INNER-facing edge, mirrored by modifier), and the `FriezeBarVertical`/`…B` inside is only the BAND: plate **`--indigo-7`**, motif **`--plaque-flat`** on both waves — FLAT, no gradient (the header band's teal→indigo ramp deliberately not carried over; the flat token because a wave fill is a `<color>` slot where `--plaque-coat`'s layer list is illegal), two 1px rules in **`--grey-6`** (edge + lip — the band framed in its host's ink, three long lines one ink per rail). The A/B horizontally-mirrored masks and ALL of the 08-24 geometry survive (15px band = 1+1+11+1+1, `13px auto` fit, carve off); the rail is content-sized to 18px (lead + band + pad + rim). GONE: the cream lip and the 1px `--indigo-7` box-shadow hairline + z-lift (the rim states the inner seam from inside the rail's own box; the indigo-7 moved INTO the bar as its plate). ⭐ **THREE MORE ASKS THE SAME SITTING**: the PATTERN EXCHANGE ("the thin one on the feed and the thick one on the header", colorings staying put) made these bands **`slim`** — one wave, mask `b`, the A/B mirror pair intact by construction — while the header band took the full two-wave pattern; then the THINNING ask ("svg slightly smaller … bar thinner … make the inner and outer light-creams the same size") adopted the board posts' SOFT recipe whole: `--feed-edge-w` **13px** (band = 1 rule + 1 pad + **9px layer** + 1 pad + 1 rule), the fixed `13px auto` fit REMOVED so slim's own `117%` squeezes the meander (~0.81px a column, deliberately soft — restoring pixel-drawn is `13px auto` back + 15px, one edit), and the rails' coat went **2px a side** (`padding: 0 2px` — the two strips were already equal at 1px; what read as thinner inside was device-pixel rounding on a fractionally-positioned box, and 2px makes the rounding a fraction of the strip instead of all of it). Rail total still 18px: 2 + 13 + 2 + 1. Host restates slim's two casualties — width via `--frieze-bar-v-slim-w`, the 1px rules over slim's zero (page styles inject after component styles; the board's posts ride the same fact). Witness: `flow-feed-cream.mjs`. |
| — | `FeedPageLegacy.vue` | Unrouted. The previous `/feed` as a FULL-PAGE surface: post-square stream (select-a-card) beside a `FeedPostPanel` detail column, `useStateHolder`-backed. Kept **verbatim** so the old surface can be restored by pointing the `/feed` route back at it (note it is no longer `FeedPostPanel`'s only live consumer — `skeletons/SkeletonFlyout.vue` mounts the same panel since 2026-07-26) — which is why `components/posts/FeedStream.vue` is a refit rather than an extraction: the card styles are duplicated on purpose, and this file is the frozen restore point, not a live dependency. Edit FeedStream for anything you actually want on `/feed` |
| `/mercury-playground` | `MercuryPlaygroundPage` | Design sandbox (left drawer hidden via `meta: { hideDrawer: true }`): gradient builder over the ServiceConsole palette (radial/linear, position/angle sliders, editable stops, click-palette-to-add-stop, copyable CSS), live previews on switchable canvas colors — boxes in the repo's `rounded-*` utilities plus a q-btn variant row (unelevated/rounded/push/glossy/round/lg) — and 13 clickable presets lifted from the ServiceConsole stylesheet that load into the builder. Also a **pixel grid lab**: rows × cols of 3-layer "pixels" (offset shadow / main / translucent glass w/ backdrop blur), one global panel drives color+alpha+radius per layer for all pixels, and a mini control grid below toggles single pixels on/off or opens a per-pixel override editor (per-layer colors, keyed `row-col`); built to later take one background painted across the whole grid. A **wave frieze** toggle constrains the grid to a 22-row × 20-col-period stepped-fret band (Mixtec greca, frieze group 2, bar style at 2px/design-unit): wave A stands ON a continuous 2-row `=` bar (10×10 head, 1px square-spiral groove entering from the left, 4-step staircase right), wave B is A's exact 180° rotation hanging upside-down below with its head under A's staircase; loops via `col % 20`, A/B colorways swap each period (defaults `#4b4b4b`/`#b3b3b3`) while the bar always renders the B color unswapped. Defaults to 3 periods (22×60, size 9, gap 0) so the mosaic repetition reads; cols snap by 20 / rows by 22 while on; mini-grid cells auto-shrink (14→10→8px past 32/48 cols); per-pixel overrides still win. The **tile board** is the page's default configuration (full-width card, on by default; the gradient builder + boxes/buttons/presets sit in a row below it, and only the heavy 3-layer pixel grid stays unmounted until the board is toggled off). Above everything, a **frieze header**: a thin full-bleed metallic strip at the very top (negative margins cancel the page padding so it touches the screen edges) — import two SVGs (or hit "use board waves" to pull the board's A/B masks directly), each becomes a `mask-image` on a stacked layer with its own palette-pickable color, `mask-repeat: repeat-x` repeats the frieze side-to-side, and the surface is a brushed-metal sheen gradient over a chooseable base color. Three geometry sliders: **height** 20-120 (thin by default, 44px) + top/bottom **padding** 0-24 + **frieze size** 15-100% — the motif's height as a % of the strip (`mask-size: auto <scale>%`), so shrinking it tiles MORE repetitions to complete the band. A **glass overlayer** (`.mercury-frieze-glass`, `glassStyle`) is a direct child of the header (not the padded inner), so it spans the FULL strip height — over the friezes AND the padded metal margins — a palette-pickable tint at a 0-0.85 **opacity** slider (`hexToRgba`) under a fixed diagonal white sheen gradient, with an optional **glass edge** toggle adding a tinted 1px border + bevelled inset highlight/shadow box-shadow. A **carve** control (`carve` = depth/light/shadow) applies stacked `drop-shadow` filters to each masked silhouette — a `dark` edge up-left + a `light` edge down-right, each doubled (full + half offset) so the coloured edge fills a solid band rather than a hairline (depth 0-5px) — so the two frieze patterns read as needle-cut into the glass-metallic plaque (defaults depth 1.5, light `#ffffff`, shadow `#0b0c10`); light/shadow are palette-pickable. The board itself is a single-layer two-color drawing strip: one editable tile (chooseable dims, 4-40×4-32, LED-style flat cells on dark) rendered 3× side by side from the same `row-col` map via `col % cols` — painting any repetition (wave A / wave B / erase tools + drag strokes, two neon color swatches) updates all three live, and strokes wrap across the faint seam guides, for testing horizontal tile continuity; while it's on the 3-layer pixel grid, boxes, buttons and presets cards are unmounted to save resources. Tiles export/import as plain text (`MERCURY-TILE v1` header, `cols/rows/size/a/b` settings, then `map:` + one line per row with `.`/`a`/`b`): the **save** button copies the text to the clipboard (mirrored into the textarea as fallback) for keeping in notes, and **interpret** parses pasted text back — resizing the board, restoring both colors and relighting the matrix (tolerant parser: trims blanks, clamps dims to slider ranges, case-insensitive); round-trips exactly. A radius slider (0-50% of cell size) rounds the board pixels and rides along in the text format (`radius:` line, optional on import); SVG downloads come **one pattern per wave**: **svg A** / **svg B** save `mercury-wave-a/b.svg` with only that wave's cells on a TRANSPARENT canvas filled PURE WHITE `#ffffff` (the neutral mask base — use as CSS `mask-image` and paint the element any color, or tint via blend modes/filters), **svg both** saves `mercury-tile.svg` with the dark `#0b0c10` bg baked in; all three share the identical cols×rows canvas so they tile seamlessly and layer in perfect registration. Board cells render with NO gap and SVGs draw gapless un-rounded rects under `shape-rendering="crispEdges"`, so exported drawings are continuous figures with no pixel seams (the radius slider now only rounds the on-screen cells). Self-contained page; palette hexes are local constants, not app SCSS vars |
| `/nodes` | `NodeExplorerPage` | Node grid + search |
| `/nodes/:id` | `NodeDetailPage` | Read-only node viewer with provenance + labels |
| `/nodes/:id/edit` | `NodeEditPage` | Legacy raw-node editor (kept; most edits should flow through `PostViewerPage`) |
| `/nodes/path/:path` | `NodeDetailPage` | Look up a node by its pathchain hash |
| `/labels` | `LabelExplorerPage` | Root labels + tree viewer |
| `/labels/:id` | `LabelDetailPage` | Single label + ancestry + holders |
| `/moments/:id` | `MomentViewerPage` | Time/space-anchor viewer (subject-panel chrome): hero datetime + place line, facts (`time_utc`, pathchain time, coordinates + OSM link) with the **tiny world map** (`moments/MomentWorldMap.vue`) to their right when the moment carries coordinates, decoded on-disk protobuf rows, and the "minted at this moment" Micro-chip rail. Also resolves `/moments/<hash>` |
| `/paths` | — | Redirects to `/files` (the on-disk path/link files are browsed there). |
| `/posts/:id` | `PostViewerPage` | ⭐ Main viewer — the `.md` body node rendered by `MarkdownBody` (`ref-display="auto"` since 2026-07-31, was `mini`: bare node refs to embeddable URLs / media files bloom into `NodeMini`, other bare refs read as micro chips; `![[…]]` block embeds and `-[[…]]` forced micros overrule per ref — `docs/concepts/references.md`) + BottomSplitter comments / forks panes (walk-backed `children`/`forks` endpoints) + a versions sidebar. The comments pane embeds `PostMakerSurface` as its composer (draft parented to the post; closing parks it as a dock tab). `?unravel=<id,…>` deep-links open the comments pane, expand the chain and halo the target comment (reloading once when the target is newer than the loaded list). Attachments and the `contentElements` machinery were removed 2026-07-14 (refs live inline in the markdown). The old `PostDetailPage.vue` was deleted — the router has used `PostViewerPage` since |
| `/skeletons` | `SkeletonPage` (list) | ⭐ Skeleton explorer — one dig-in square per **origin** skeleton (`skeletons/SkeletonSquares.vue`); the dug pit lists instantiations as identifying rows (`skeletons/SkeletonInstanceList.vue`: title/compacted name, author, age, excerpt, `SkeletonRefLinks`, per-row dig for instances-of-instances, "+ N more…" pagination). Selecting a square or row feeds the right-hand Structure panel (`skeletons/SkeletonUsages.vue`). |
| `/skeletons/:id` | `SkeletonPage` (detail) | ⭐ Spine-pairs navigator. Drives the **pathos-docs viewer** when the skeleton is `PATHOS_DOCS`. The header shows the skeleton's role as its real classification label chip (`SCHEMA` / `INSTANTIATION` in the labels band — the grey mode badges are gone; the name is the title: data, not a label). Schemas get **Instantiate** + **Fork** buttons (Fork opens the builder dock's fork draft via `skeletonBuilder.openForkDraft` — works on your own schemas too); non-POST instances get a **Fork** button (`POST /skeletons/:id/fork` → navigates to the fork). A **Forks band** under the subject panel lists direct forks (`GET /skeletons/:id/forks`) — on `/skeletons/5` (ELEMENT) that's the whole per-kind schema family. `ElementActions` hides its own fork entry here (`hide-fork`) so there's exactly one affordance. An **Export dropdown** (2026-08-01, both header branches) offers Bundle (3 layers deep w/ chain evidence) and Rows (instances as tabular data) — `skeletonService.exportDeep` → `GET /skeletons/:id/export` → JSON blob download named `pathos-<name>-<id>-<format>.json`; a 403 root stays with the AccessDeniedBanner path. **CLAIM instances lead with the claim band** (2026-07-31, `claims/ClaimBand.vue` — mounted in BOTH branches: above the read-only structure panel, and between header and editor for the owned author view).  **Both header branches wear `sym_o_orthopedics`** (2026-08-07, user ask) — the Material Symbols glyph, at 20px, replacing the owned-editor's hard-coded `schema` and the read-only branch's `iconForSkeleton(skeleton)`. That helper answers with the per-KIND icon (a POST reads `article`), which states what the instance is ABOUT where this line is the VIEWER's own title; it still dresses the usage and fork row lists below, where per-kind is exactly what tells the rows apart. The feed card's cap uses the same glyph for its way in here, so the door and the room wear one sign. |
| `/files` | `FileTreePage` | ⭐ Whole-platform pathchain browser — shared registries + every owner subtree. See [File tree viewer](#file-tree-viewer) below. |
| `/files/:ownerHash` | `FileTreePage` | Focused subtree for one owner + ancestor-lineage breadcrumb. `?reveal=<address>` deep-links to a specific element. |
| `/entities/:id` | `EntityProfilePage` | Entity viewer (subject-panel chrome + Anchors rail). On your own profile the Anchors rail adds a **wallet block** (DOG Coin C1, 2026-08-01: balance headline + the 5 latest movements with direction arrows and memo lines, each row's receipt an `InfoChip` into the payer-signed TRANSACTION skeleton; `wallet.service.js` → `GET /api/wallet`), the invite-secret generator (since 2026-08-01 it wears the **doubling-wait curve**: a status line off `GET /identity/invite-status` — "Invitation #11 · unlocks in 2m", inherited handicap called out — the button disabled while locked, and a 429 from generate surfaced as "Not yet — invitation #N unlocks in …"), a **"your secrets"** list (status chip `used`/`unused`; used rows link to the receiving entity via `GET /api/secrets/mine`; `reset`-type rows read `reset · <status>` with "recovers @user" — receiver bound at mint), a **sessions** block (Thread H, 2026-07-31: `GET /identity/sessions` → device rows with a glance label parsed from the user agent — "Chrome · Mac" — "this device" chip on the current row, revoke buttons on the rest, "last seen" times), and a big **Log out** button below the panel (`authStore.logout()` → best-effort `POST /identity/logout` server-side revoke, then clears localStorage → `/#/auth`). Viewing a person **you invited** (their `ancestor_id` = your root, type USER) shows a **"password recovery"** block in the Anchors rail: mint button → `POST /identity/recovery/mint` → the one-time reset secret hash to hand over out of band. When the viewed entity is in *your identity tree* (your login or any of its alter-egos), an **Alter-egos panel** (`entities/AlterEgoPanel.vue`) joins the rail: acting-as banner, "return to root identity" button, the entity's nested sub-entity tree (per-node internal log-in buttons via `authStore.switchIdentity`), and the alter-ego creation module (name + optional bio → `POST /api/entities/:id/alter-egos`). Below the grid, a full-width **contributions band** (`entities/EntityContributions.vue`): three panels — **Instantiations** (every owned template instance via `GET /entities/:id/skeletons`, schema link per row, "plumbing" toggle for ELEMENT/PATH/PIN/NAVIGATION), **Posts** (`GET /feed?ownerId=`, title + excerpt rows), **Content** (`GET /nodes/uploads?ownerId=`, thumbnail mini-gallery — images inline, other kinds as icon+ext tiles), each paginated with a load-more. Own profiles also get the **"My organizations" band** (`entities/EntityOrganizations.vue`, above My polls): each membership cell shows the org, role title, admin chip and the MASK with its put-on-the-mask switch — masks live in the ORG's folder, so this band (not the alter-ego tree) is where they surface. Org entities (type 3) hide the AlterEgoPanel — masks mint only through the join seam |
| `/organizations` | `OrganizationsPage` | **Organizations home** (orgs feature 2026-07, `docs/concepts/organizations.md`): my memberships grouped per org (name, role title, admin chip, MASK row with acting chip / **Put on the mask** → `authStore.switchIdentity`), pending-invitations band (accept/decline), found-an-organization form (name + your role title + bio → `POST /api/organizations`), and the join-with-a-secret redeem form (`POST /api/organizations/join`) |
| `/organizations/:id` | `OrgDetailPage` | Org detail — `:id` is the organization ROW id. Public header always (org entity profile: name, bio, member count, parent link, my seat + mask switch). Members see: the **decision-making tree** (recursive `organizations/OrgMemberTreeNode.vue`, nested by REPORTS_TO, per-seat switch on `is_me`), **sub-organizations** band (+ admin create form w/ lead-mask select), **rules** band, **resource tree** band (ElementMinis **keyed by acting id** — wearing the mask remounts them so grants re-resolve; a `SlotRefPicker` share panel feeds `POST /:id/share`), and the admin **invite panel** (entity search + role title + reports-to mask + admin toggle + message). Outsiders get the locked panel (membership travels by invitation) |
| `/*` (catchAll) | `ErrorNotFound` | The 404 — **Talavero's page** (2026-07-31): the same picture-banner pattern as `AccessDeniedBanner` (landscape shot full-bleed on desktop, portrait in flow on mobile, copy on the slate field) with the `404` code and "Path not found in the pathchain". Root is a **plain div on purpose**: this route mounts OUTSIDE any QLayout, and a `q-page` without a layout renders nothing (the pre-Talavero page had exactly that bug — the 404 was blank) |

## Key components

| Component | Role |
|-----------|------|
| `layout/FriezeHeader.vue` | **DELETED 2026-08-12** (user ask: "remove the brown frieze header from the top of the screen and also the one from the bottom of the screen, below the footer bar"). It was the platform's **crown strip** — a thin `--frieze-h` band fixed edge-to-edge at the top of every page, mounted by BOTH layouts, brown-1 plaque under two mercury-wave masks with a carve filter, z 3000, pointer-events none. THE WINDOW'S TOP CHROME IS NOW `MediaTabsBar`'s silver rail alone (`--media-tabs-h` — grey-4 face, indigo-4 rim), and the bottom chrome is the nav bar's row alone. Removed with it, because each existed only to continue one of the two screen-edge bands across a column that owned those pixels: `.drawer-frieze--top` (MainLayout), `.nav-floor-frieze` (NavigationBar), `.drawer-floor-frieze`, `.pins-floor-frieze`. KEPT: every band drawn INSIDE a window — the drawer's section dividers, the stack's two, the pins' one, each dock's own — and `--frieze-h` itself, which is still the frieze family's thickness (`FriezeBar`, the vertical pair, `--media-tabs-band` = a fifth of it). ⚠ It is no longer a TOP-CHROME offset: `--frieze-h` in a `top`/`padding-top` calc now opens a band-height gap under a band that is not there. Its palette lineage survives in the rows below ("the crown strip's tone mapping" = base -1, waves -3/-4) — those describe a recipe, not a live element. |
| `layout/FriezeFooter.vue` | **RETIRED 2026-08-04** (user ask: make the floor band "like the clear one on top of the footer nav bar, and remove that one"). Was the crown strip's bookend at the bottom of the window (2026-07-25 → 2026-08-04): `FriezeHeader` with the waves MIRRORED VERTICALLY — `assets/frieze/mercury-wave-{a,b}-vmirror.svg`, the originals wrapped in a `translate(0 143) scale(1 -1)` group baked into the assets (a CSS `scaleY(-1)` would have mirrored the drop-shadow carve too; the assets stay in the tree, unused) — parked ON the nav bar's top edge at the crown strip's own z 3000, with the header's brown-1/brown-3/brown-4 palette, its carve, and its lip turned into a `border-top`. Deleted because the window-floor **FriezeBar floor band** (NavigationBar's `.nav-floor-frieze`, continued by the drawer's `.drawer-floor-frieze` and the pins column's `.pins-floor-frieze`) wears the crown strip's pale palette itself now — through the `--frieze-bar-base`/`--frieze-bar-wave-{one,two}` dials, the same seam the chat dock's green band uses — so the page is closed by ONE band and nothing rides the bar's top edge. **`--dock-bottom` (`_tokens.scss`) went with it**: the token once stood docks one `--frieze-h` higher to clear this opaque band, and every dock now anchors to `bottom: var(--nav-footer-h)` (the base `.dock-window` states that value as its default). |
| `layout/FriezeBar.vue` | The crown strip's **box-insertable sibling** (2026-07-24): same geometry as `FriezeHeader` — height `var(--frieze-h)`, 1px vertical pad, `mask-repeat: repeat-x` at `mask-size: auto 99%`, the identical paired-drop-shadow carve — but a normal in-flow block (`width: 100%`, no fixed positioning, no z-index, `pointer-events: none`) meant to sit INSIDE other boxes. Uses the **horizontally mirrored** mask exports `assets/frieze/mercury-wave-a/b-mirror.svg` (the originals wrapped in a `translate(231 0) scale(-1 1)` group) and the **inverted palette**: flat brown-4 (`--brown-4`) plaque base with brown-2 (`--brown-2`) + brown-1 (`--brown-1`) waves — the negative of the header's brown-1 base / brown-3+4 waves. **ALL THREE TONES are dials** — `var(--frieze-bar-base, var(--brown-4))` (2026-07-26) plus `var(--frieze-bar-wave-one, var(--brown-2))` / `var(--frieze-bar-wave-two, var(--brown-1))` (2026-08-03) — so a host box outside the brown colorway sets them on the element it mounts the bar on and the band follows; ⚠ since 2026-08-17 (user ask) most placements do NOT: the **SIDE CHROME TRIO** rule in `_components.scss` (one grouped `.drawer-frieze, .stack-frieze, .pins-frieze` selector — ⚠ **plus `.nav-frieze` for half of 2026-08-23, a QUARTET that reverted when the nav trail was INVERTED to a `--light-cream` plate with `--grey-3`/`--grey-4` waves; a band stating its own three paints has nothing left to take from a palette rule**) dials the drawer's four dividers and the stack/pins' three bands at once — base `--grey-8` (one mid-sitting ask visited `--grey-7` and came back) and, **since 2026-08-21's from-scratch asks, THREE PAINTS AND NOTHING ELSE, PIXEL-DRAWN**: wave two `--grey-2` (mask `b`, the thick wave carrying the middle line), wave one `--grey-4`, NO rims, `--frieze-bar-carve: none`, and a fixed fit — `--frieze-bar-h: 15px` + `--frieze-bar-fit: auto 13px`, the masks' 13-row grid at its natural 1px per row (a 21×13 tile inside the component's 1px pads), nothing squeezed, every stroke on the pixel grid; resize only in 13px mask steps (next clean size `auto 26px` in a 28px band). (The 2026-08-17 era between: 1px `--grey-6` rims on every band, a 0.96 × `--frieze-h` trim walk, and two SMOOTH warm-first `--orange-11`/`--teal-11` gradient ramps through the `-paint` dials — the full construction is in git; `--orange-11` was minted for it and deleted with it.) The component's default browns survive on the AUTH page's two bars alone. Consumers: `skeletons/ElementFlyout.vue` fed the base `--grey-9` in its single-box era (the dashboard dock keeps that dress; the fused window feeds `--grey-8`), and `chat/ChatDock.vue` (2026-08-05, hue-swapped from a full-height green band to `slim` lime — see the `slim` prop below) dials base `--lime-10` with a `--brown-1` wave two. The wave dials REVERSE a note that stood here — that re-toning them "would be a different bar rather than this one dressed" — and what settled it is that the platform already HAS a band in a second family: `FriezeBarVertical` is FriezeHeader's tone mapping in indigo, so a whole colorway of this band is a thing the design does, not a costume. What must NOT move with the tones is the RECIPE (masks, fit, carve — though the fit and the carve are DIALS themselves since 2026-08-21: `--frieze-bar-fit` for a pixel-exact mask, `--frieze-bar-carve: none` for flat shapes) — and the waves must stay a step or two ABOVE their base, since the carve lights them from the top left and reads as a groove only when the ink is lighter than the plaque it is cut into. One interaction: `slim` drops layer one, so a host dialling only `--frieze-bar-wave-one` dresses a band that isn't drawn. **`flip` prop** (2026-08-02, user ask) — the band with its meander running the OTHER WAY, for the three bands in the stack/pins right-edge column, so that column reflects the left drawer's bands instead of repeating them. It swaps the two layers' masks for the **un-mirrored `mercury-wave-{a,b}.svg`**, which by construction ARE this bar's masks flipped (each `-mirror` file is its original wrapped in `translate(231 0) scale(-1 1)` on the same canvas), so the flip costs no asset. Masks ONLY — plaque, tones and carve stay put, and pointedly **not** `transform: scaleX(-1)`, which mirrors the drop-shadow carve along with the motif and lights that band from the wrong side (the same trap `FriezeBarVerticalB` ships baked mirror assets to avoid). **`slim` prop** (2026-07-26) — the HALF-HEIGHT variant, and a whole recipe rather than a knob, because at `calc(var(--frieze-h) / 2)` (**9.45px** at a 900px viewport, inner 7.45px, since the 2026-08-02 thinning — 13.5px/11.5px before it) four things have to move together or the motif goes sub-pixel: **(1) height** halves, the 1px vertical pad stays (the only margin left at that size); **(2) ONE WAVE** — layer one (brown-2, mask `a`) is `v-if`'d OUT, not hidden, leaving the brown-1 `b` mask alone. The two masks are the SAME meander offset by half a tile — `a` carries one upper + one lower spiral, `b` carries the other pair PLUS the **full-width centre rule** — so `b` alone still tiles as a frieze, at half the spiral density and with the rule running unbroken through the middle (the one mark that survives at any size); two interlocking waves in 11px would have read as a texture, not a pattern; **(3) MASK FIT** — both masks are a 13-row grid (231×143, 11px cells) whose **top and bottom rows are EMPTY** (ink runs y 11→132 = 84.6% of the file), dead margin that reads as breathing room at full height and costs a sixth of the band when halved, so the slim bar scales the mask PAST its box at **`auto 117%`** and lets the blank rows fall outside — `0.846 × 117% ≈ 99%`, so the INK fills 99% of the inner height, exactly the fraction the full bar gives the whole FILE (same 1% breathing, moved where it can be seen; nothing is clipped). Worth ~**0.67px per motif row** against the ~0.57px a plain `99%` leaves, vs ~1.3px on a full-height bar (1.04/0.88/1.9 before the 2026-08-02 thinning — both slim figures are sub-pixel now, and the `117%` is what keeps the centre rule reading as a line rather than a haze); **(4) CARVE** — the drop-shadow offsets halve with the box (**0.5/0.3**, half of the full bar's post-thinning 1.05/0.5; it was 0.75/0.4 under the 1.5/0.75 original), since a groove WIDER than the ~1px stroke it is carving and the band would read as a black-and-white smear with a brown tint. The TONES are not part of the recipe — those are the three `--frieze-bar-*` dials, orthogonal and set by the host. ⭐ **THE FIT AND CARVE REACH THROUGH `slim` SINCE 2026-08-30** (the top rail's ask: its band went back to slim while keeping the pixel-drawn `auto 13px`/`carve: none` its 08-27 height paid for): slim's `auto 117%` and halved carve became the DEFAULTS of `--frieze-bar-fit`/`--frieze-bar-carve` reads rather than hardcoded values, so a dial-less slim host renders to the pixel as before and a dialing one gets under slim exactly what it would on a full bar. Consumers: `skeletons/ElementFlyout.vue` (its grey-9 era), since 2026-08-05 `chat/ChatDock.vue`'s header band (base `--lime-10`, wave two `--brown-1` — no wave-one dial, since slim never renders that layer), and since 2026-08-30 BOTH horizontal chrome edges: the top rail's `.media-tabs__frieze` AND the nav bar's `.nav-frieze` trail (each grey-8 plaque, the cyan→indigo ramp on wave two, pixel-drawn through the dials — see `media/MediaTabsBar.vue` / `layout/NavigationBar.vue`). First placement: MainLayout's left drawer, where **FOUR** of them are the section dividers (above Back, after Back, before the EXPLORE header, before the IDENTITY header — they replaced the spaced q-separators). ⚠ **The count went 4 → 3 → 4**, and the two "top" bands are NOT the same object: the original `.drawer-frieze--top` was hoisted out of the scroll area to REPLACE the crown strip across this column and went with that strip on 2026-08-12, while the one above Back since **2026-08-17** (user ask) is an ordinary inner divider — inside the `q-scroll-area`, in the list's own padding, same `.drawer-frieze` class, same rhythm, same recipe (and the side-chrome trio palette since later that day — see the dials ⚠ above) — so it brackets the Back button into its own section instead of continuing a screen-edge band. (`.drawer-frieze` — `.drawer-frieze` — `margin: 8px 0`, NO horizontal margin so each runs the full drawer width edge to edge in both mini and expanded states; the q-list only pads vertically, so nothing insets them). The **top one is a special case** (2026-07-24): the drawer now overlaps the crown strip, so `.drawer-frieze--top` **replaces** it across the drawer's column rather than sitting under it — it is hoisted OUT of the `q-scroll-area` to be the drawer content's pinned first flex child (`flex: 0 0 auto`, `margin: 0`; the old `margin-top: -8px` q-list-padding hack is gone) so it can never scroll away from the corner, and it wears `FriezeHeader`'s **exact box**: `height: var(--frieze-h)` plus the same `border-bottom: 1px solid #BCAAA4` brown-3 lip — which, border-box, also eats the same 1px off the carve area, so both motifs are drawn at an identical size and the band steps nowhere at the drawer's right edge (verified: both boxes measure `top 0, h 27, border-bottom 1px, padding-top 1px`). Only the **palette** differs by design — the drawer's band keeps FriezeBar's inverted brown-4 base. Second placement (2026-07-24): the stack/pins side widgets carry **three between them**, full widget width in both presentations — the stack's **top edge** (`.stack-frieze`), the stack's **inner** band between its well and its bottom header/head glyph (`.stack-frieze--inner`), and the pins' single band between its **top header/pin glyph and its well** (`.pins-frieze`). Only the stack's top band is the **same special case as the drawer's**: that widget sits at `top: 0` over the crown strip, so its band **replaces** the strip across its column and is likewise matched to `FriezeHeader`'s exact box (`height: var(--frieze-h)` + the 1px brown-3 bottom lip, with the widget's own `border-top` dropped). The two INNER bands stand in for nothing, so they take the plain `height: var(--frieze-h)` with **no lip** — border-box resolves all of them to the same 27px box either way (verified in place: bands at `top 0` / `top 509` on the stack, `top 640` on the pins, each `h 27`). The pins widget's old **bottom-edge** band (against the nav footer) was removed the same day — that seam would cut the pinned column off from the tack in the bar, which should read as one strip. **ONE TILING, always** — the two layers span the box (`inset: 0`) and the masks anchor `left center`, so the motif runs one way across the whole band, every placement. A **`mirrored` prop lived here for a single day** (2026-07-27, added and removed) for the flyout's inner band: the band cut in half about its centre, each half an absolutely-positioned tiling running OUTWARD from the seam — the right the usual `-mirror` masks from the centre rightward, the left the **un-mirrored `mercury-wave-{a,b}.svg`** (by construction the exact horizontal mirror of the `-mirror` pair — each `-mirror` file is its original wrapped in `translate(231 0) scale(-1 1)` on the same 231×143 canvas, so the mirror cost no new asset) at `mask-position: right`. It came out because the centre SEAM is a visible event — two motifs meeting nose to nose in the middle of an 11px strip, in the one spot the eye lands — where the flyout's band is there to be a quiet rule between the head and the well; the right half simply extends across the box instead. Kept here in case a centred band is ever wanted again, with the one trap: **NOT `transform: scaleX(-1)`** — the carve is a `drop-shadow` filter and a mirrored element mirrors its filter with it, putting the dark edge up-RIGHT on one half and up-LEFT on the other, two light sources in one band (`FriezeBarVerticalB` ships baked mirror assets for exactly this reason), and no `overflow: hidden` on the halves, since the carve paints up to 1.5px outside the ink and clipping shears the groove off both sides of the seam. **THREE ADDITIONS ON 2026-08-07** (user ask, for the post card's frieze pair — `posts/FeedStream.vue`, the platform's fourth `FriezeBar` consumer and the first to mount TWO). (1) **`vflip`** turns the meander round the HORIZONTAL axis, the counterpart to `flip`'s vertical one, and by the same mechanism — an ASSET SWAP, never a `transform: scaleY(-1)`, which would take the drop-shadow carve with it and light one band from below. The masks are now the four corners of one square: plain (`flip`), `-mirror` (the default), `-vmirror` (`flip vflip`) and the new `-mirror-vmirror` pair — the originals wrapped in `translate(231 143) scale(-1 -1)`, i.e. turned 180° — which `vflip` alone selects; the compound rule `.frieze-bar--flip.frieze-bar--vflip` must stay MORE SPECIFIC than the single-class ones or the cascade answers a `flip vflip` bar by source order. It is NOT the removed `mirrored` variant described below (that one cut ONE band about its centre; this turns a WHOLE band over so two of them can bracket something as a reflected pair). (2) **The wave dials gained an IMAGE form** — `--frieze-bar-wave-{one,two}-paint`, `none` unless set, laid over the flat tone: the layers are a mask over a painted box, so a gradient fills the motif exactly as a colour does and the plaque underneath (a different element's background) is untouched. One ramp spans the WHOLE bar however many times the 231px mask repeats, since gradients have no intrinsic size, and the carve reads ALPHA, which paint does not touch. (3) **The height is a dial** — `--frieze-bar-h`, defaulting to `--frieze-h` (`slim`: half it). The mask fit follows the box for free (its `99%`/`117%` are fractions of it); the carve does NOT, its offsets being absolute px, which is both deliberate (a groove is a fixed width) and the floor on the dial — trim far enough and the 0.5px groove is wider than the strokes it cuts. |
| `layout/FriezeBarVertical.vue` | **The crown strip turned 90° CLOCKWISE, in the INDIGO colorway** (2026-07-25). Everything that made `FriezeHeader` is rotated with it, so the two read as the same object seen on its side: thickness is `width: var(--frieze-bar-v-w, var(--frieze-h))` (the header's height, same token, now measured across — a **published dial** since the `slim` variant, which is how `FeedPage` runs the feed container's two edges at `0.8 ×` it — 2026-08-07's "a little thinner" number, BACK since 2026-08-21 ("thinner … not so thin they lose detail"; a 2026-08-18 "a little thicker" ask held them at `0.95 ×` in between) — still short of the strip's own full thickness so the bars stay THINNER than it, and how `slim` states its own half; it drives the `flex-basis` too, so a host never restates the geometry); the motif is the SAME mercury waves exported **pre-rotated** — `assets/frieze/mercury-wave-{a,b}-rot90.svg`, the originals wrapped in a `translate(143 0) rotate(90)` group with the canvas swapped 231×143 → 143×231 (the same asset trick as the `-mirror` pair FriezeBar uses) — tiled `mask-repeat: repeat-y` at `mask-size: var(--frieze-bar-v-fit, 99% auto)` — **fit and carve are DIALS since 2026-08-21** (`--frieze-bar-v-fit`, `--frieze-bar-v-carve`; slim's `117%` reads the same fit var — the feed head box's posts state `13px auto` + `none` to draw the motif pixel-exact) — (the header's `auto 99%` on its side), `mask-position: center top`; padding is `0 1px` (the header's `1px 0`); and **the carve rotates too** — a vector `(dx,dy)` turned 90° CW becomes `(-dy,dx)`, so the header's dark up-left + light down-right read here as dark up-RIGHT + light down-LEFT, making the render pixel-identical to physically rotating the strip. The **lip** follows the same turn: the header's `border-bottom` lands on the LEFT edge, which is the default `lip="left"`; `lip="right"` mirrors it so a PAIR of bars can face their lips inward around a box. The one thing that is NOT a rotation is the **palette** — and as of the end of 2026-07-25 it is not even a variation: the bar wears **`FriezeHeader`'s OWN tone mapping in Quasar's indigos**, so it is a true colorway of the crown strip. **INVERTED on 2026-08-07** (user ask): base **`--indigo-8`** under wave layer one **`--indigo-1`** (the lit face) and wave layer two **`--indigo-3`** (the dark) — a DEEP plate under PALE waves, where the crown strip and every setting of this bar since 2026-07-25 ran a pale plate under deeper ones. The four-role MAPPING is untouched and wave one is still the lit face; only its DIRECTION flipped, and the pair kept its **TWO-LEVEL SPREAD** through the flip, because the spread is the carve and not the depth. Two things change ends with it and must be re-read: the **headroom** (a pale pair is capped by the family's lightest step, `-1`, so open the spread DOWNWARD — take wave two to `-4`, never lift wave one) and the **carve's white flank**, which now falls on a near-white wave, so the lit side of the groove reads much softer than the dark side (the same asymmetry raking light gives, and the reason a pale wave must not be pushed paler even if a step existed — at pure white the groove would have one visible flank). The plaque also brings the bar LEVEL WITH the feed head box's two inner posts, `--indigo-8` since 08-06, so all four frieze edges on that surface are one object in four places. The plaque's own walk: `--indigo-1` (⇔ brown-1, 2026-07-25) → `--grey-4` (2026-08-05, the floating media windows' coat) → here; and before the inversion the waves were **`--indigo-4`** (lit) / **`--indigo-6`** (dark) — **TWO LEVELS APART**, where every setting from 2026-07-25 on had them one apart. That pair is the end of a four-step walk on 2026-08-05: `-3/-4` → `-4/-5` (*"stronger by 1"*) → `-5/-6` (*"one more tone"*) → **`-4/-6`** (the light one back up alone), which required **adding `$indigo-5` (#5c6bc0) and `$indigo-6` (#3f51b5)** to a family that had stopped at -4. The first two moves are the tail of the same day's swap to a neutral plate: these tones were chosen to carve a groove on `--indigo-1`, where wave one sat at 1.19:1 on its own coat, and `--grey-4` is a whole hue away and reads flatter under them. **The third move is the one to learn from** — stepping both waves together held them one level apart the whole way, so all three settings had the same internal contrast and only got darker as a block (more ink on the plate, not more relief); opening the pair to two levels is what let the motif read in DEPTH. A frieze is two waves interleaved, not one wave in two shades, so **the SPREAD is the carve** and the spread is the dial to reach for. `-6` is the family's Material 500, its pure hue and the darkest thing on the plate short of the ink at `-8` — which is also the wall, since at `-7` wave and ink are neighbours and wave two starts reading as a written line. All of this costs the level-for-level tie to the crown strip's browns (which stop at -4), and that is the right thing to give up: the bar is a colorway of `FriezeHeader` in its **tone MAPPING** — plaque / wave one / wave two / lip — and every one of those four has left the browns' indices now. **Keep the mapping in step, not the numbers.** One relation genuinely differs as a result: the crown strip's waves are one level apart and these are two, so the vertical bars carve deeper than the horizontal ones by design. And the **lip = THE PLAQUE'S OWN TONE, so the bar draws NO inward line at all** (2026-08-06, user ask, last of four settings that day: `--indigo-6` → -5 → -4 → `--grey-4`) — and it TRAVELS with the plaque, so it is `--indigo-8` since 2026-08-07. Its inner edge is where the plate stops against the box. A `3px` sliver came back a moment later ("just a little little") and `8px` on 2026-08-07, and the lip stayed plaque-toned through both: that gap does the separating. This note used to end *"if the cards are ever pushed further off the bars, the lip wants `--indigo-4`"* — they were, and it does NOT, because the plaque went `--indigo-8` in the same pass and a deep plate stopping against a pale field is the strongest edge on the surface. The lip's job comes back only if the plaque goes pale again. The history: it was `--indigo-3`, the wave-ONE tone, for the bar's whole life, then one afternoon at `--grey-6` and one evening at `--indigo-6`, wave TWO: when the plate went neutral that morning the lip went neutral with it, on the argument that a coloured hairline down the INSIDE of a piece of metal reads as trim rather than as where the metal stops. That argument was really about a lip left behind at -3 while the plate moved, and the wave walk answered it — with the waves at `-4`/`-6` the colorway's dark end is a tone of the plate's OWN motif rather than a leftover from the old coat, so the lip could come home, first onto the dark wave and the next day onto the lit one. What the round trip settled is the WEIGHT: a full-height hairline down BOTH sides of the box reads at -6 as a frame drawn around the feed and at -4 as where the bar's own material stops. The motif survives every plaque swap on the same terms it always had — the waves need only sit a step or two off their plaque, in EITHER direction, for the carve to read as a groove — but "clears it" and "carves on it" turned out to be different bars, which is exactly what the wave step above went on to fix. That lip draws the feed container's SIDE BORDERS. The **container's background used to be the same plaque coat**, so box and frieze edges read as one continuous material — `--indigo-1` on both from 2026-07-25, `--grey-4` on both from 2026-08-05, when the bar went neutral in the morning (user ask) and the box, its head band and its scroll bed followed the same evening (second ask); for the length of one pass between those asks the rule was simply FALSE on screen, a neutral band edging an indigo box. **That rule is RETIRED as of 2026-08-07**: the plaque left a second time and the box did NOT follow, so the surface is `--indigo-8` bars around a `--grey-4` field. Two rolled edges on a 19px strip with the motif running between them reads as a **CYLINDER**, a rod standing beside the feed rather than a band lying on it. Both `::after` blocks are deleted (A's and B's), along with the `--lip-*::after` placement rules and `slim`'s `content: none` cancellation. What it was really buying, SOFTNESS against the near-black page canvas (hence a silhouette running almost to black), the `--indigo-8` plaque of the same ask now gives for free; a pale plate is what needed the dissolve. The profile, for the record: an `::after` 8px wide on the edge **opposite the lip** — keyed off the same `--lip-*` class, so the roll is always on the bar's free side wherever it is placed — carrying two overlaid gradients and **no border at all**. A bullnose seen orthographically from above does not end in a line; the last pixels of its face are the surface turning away, and what the eye reads there is a run of tone. The profile is deliberately ASYMMETRIC, which is what separates it from a stripe: the fall OUTWARD from the crest is short and steep (~4.6px, `rgba(11,12,16,.9)` at the silhouette down to 0), the rise INWARD is long and gentle (a `rgba(255,255,255,.68)` crest at 4.6px settling to 0 at 8px). The `--lip-*` classes restate ONE side after the base rule drew both, winning on source order at equal specificity — which decided nothing for the day and a half the two tones matched. In order: the inversion (base `--indigo-8`, waves indigo-1/-3) → lit wave to `--grey-4` → base to `--indigo-9` + dark wave to `--brown-1`, meant to put the feed container's bars on exactly the plate and warm motif the head box's inner posts already wore → wave one to `--grey-5` → **base BACK to `--indigo-8`**. The round trip settled the point: the argument for -9 was to match those posts, and THE POSTS ARE `--indigo-8`, so -8 is the level that lands all four frieze plates together and -9 was a step away from what it was chosen to match. **That last step turned the mapping over**: Quasar's `brown-1` (#efebe9) is lighter than `grey-4` (#e0e0e0), so wave TWO had silently become the lit face and the pair had collapsed to ~1.1:1 with its names inverted; a step down restores ~1.6:1 and states it honestly. | `layout/FriezeBarVerticalB.vue` | **Variant B of the vertical frieze bar** (end of 2026-07-25) — `FriezeBarVertical` with the wave pattern **MIRRORED HORIZONTALLY** and *nothing else changed*, so a pair framing a box reflect each other across its centre line instead of repeating. Two deliberate NON-changes: (1) **the carve does not flip** — B keeps A's dark up-right / light down-left drop-shadows, because a scene has one light source and mirroring it would read as a second sun on the box's right-hand side; this is the same call `FriezeBar` makes, masking with the `-mirror` waves while keeping `FriezeHeader`'s own carve. (2) **the mirror is baked into the ASSETS, not a CSS transform** — `assets/frieze/mercury-wave-{a,b}-rot90-mirror.svg`, the rot90 exports wrapped in one more `translate(143 0) scale(-1 1)` group (composite: source `(x,y) → (y,x)`, canvas unchanged at 143×231). A `transform: scaleX(-1)` on the layer would have mirrored the drop-shadow carve along with the mask, which is exactly what (1) rules out — and it is why the repo already keeps a `-mirror` pair for the horizontal bar. **B also carries A's outer-edge ROLL** (2026-08-05) — the same 8px `::after`, the same stops, keyed off the same `--lip-*` class, so on B's usual `lip="left"` it lands on the box's right-hand OUTSIDE and the pair reads as one plaque turning over at both long edges. That element **is** mirrored with `transform: scaleX(-1)`, and it is the ONE place in the frieze family where that is allowed: the rule in (2) exists because a `scaleX` flips a layer's drop-shadow carve, and the roll has no drop-shadow — only two gradients, whose direction *should* flip, because the edge they describe has flipped. Re-hueing B is part of the same two-file edit as A. |
| `layout/RgbHairline.vue` | ⭐ **The RGB hairline — a divider drawn as a VERTICAL SANDWICH** (2026-08-07, user ask): a thick grey rule, a gradient band, a thick grey rule, `2px + 2px + 2px = 6px` in all. It stands BESIDE the frieze family rather than inside it, and the distinction is the reason it exists: a frieze band is a MOTIF carved into a plaque and needs height to read (~0.8px per motif row is the floor — see `FriezeBar`'s `slim` and [specs/gotchas.md](../../specs/gotchas.md)), while three flat lines hold their reading at any size, so the divider closest to a reading area can be the thin one. The filling is the only HUE in the rule — a `--cyan-11` → `--indigo-11` ramp running DOWN it, so the divider states a direction as well as a division; both are their family's **A100**, the third accent at that index in service (with `--teal-11`), because a mark made of two hues has to be built from ONE index across two families or the ramp reads as bright fading to dull. The bread is `--grey-7`, a step UNDER the host's ordinary hairlines (a compound rule is read as one object, so its own lines should not be mistaken for two of the host's) — on the feed it sits one step above the frieze band's `--grey-8` plaque and one under the `--grey-6` every LINE on that card is drawn in — the card's two dividers are a plate group of their own, which is what lets both stand out of a box whose lines never move. **Four dials**, in the frieze family's idiom (`var(--x, default)` inline, never a default declared on the element — a host class and a component rule on the SAME element are settled by source order, which is not a contract): `--rgb-hairline-edge` / `-edge-h` / `--rgb-hairline-fill` / `-fill-h`. Reversing the ramp is a re-stated `--rgb-hairline-fill` (CSS cannot read a gradient backwards, so there is nothing to flip). Two implementation notes worth keeping: **`background-clip: padding-box`** is load-bearing — a background paints under the BORDER box by default, so the ramp would run over all 6px and the filling would show only its middle slice, two muddy blues instead of cyan → indigo; and **`flex: 0 0 auto` lives in the component**, because every host is a column with a ceiling somewhere and the failure is silent (the rule survives as a coloured line and nobody reads it as squashed). Consumer: the post card's pit↔foot divider (`posts/FeedStream.vue`), which dials nothing — the defaults ARE that card's. ⚠ **It is a ONE-INSTANCE consumer again since 2026-08-10**: the card carried three for part of that day (a thin `--rail` pair bracketing the label strip, the lower one `--flip`ped) and the day's last ask deleted the pair with both modifiers when the strip took a rim of its own. Nothing on the platform sets `--rgb-hairline-edge-h` or restates `--rgb-hairline-fill` today — the dials are still the contract, they just have no live user. |
