The budget effect: `CAP` 24 → **26**, and the resting constant back on **276** by three moves that cancel (−2 the band's rules, −`0.55 × --frieze-h` its plate, +2 this border), making `--media-max-h` **px-only on the resting card** for the first time since 2026-08-07; expanded **302** + the well's own `1 × --frieze-h`. ONE rgb band — the label strip bracketed by two plain `--grey-6` `border-bottom`s since the day's closing asks (the byline's, and the rail strip's riding its `v-if`), which is what those seams wore before the frieze ever took them. Plain rules could come back where the rgb pair could not: a flat line beside a rimmed box states a seam, a 4px sandwich pressed against one states it twice. The pair went because the label strip took a RIM of its own (below): a rimmed box pressed between two full-bleed rules is the same seam stated twice, and one line replacing two bands is fewer lines, not more. The FOOT's band never belonged to the bracket — it closes the reading area and is half of nothing, the standing the cap's band had before the frieze took that seam. **THE LABEL RAIL STOPPED BEING A TRAY, THEN BECAME A PANEL AGAIN** (2026-08-10, six asks in a run). It first lost everything that made it a box: `.post-square__rail-strip` lost its `6px 7px` padding on both axes, `.post-square__rail` lost its vertical padding (the chips' own rims are the lane; the 6px side lead stays as the scroller's run-in/run-out), its `7px` radius, and its uneven `1px/2px-top` rim (`border: 0`) — and its floor walked `--grey-4` → -6 → -7 → -6 → -8 → -6 → -4 (a full circle closed the same day) → **`--grey-5`**, the one level the walk had never tried, where it rests. The walk tried every level a full-bleed figure could stand on: -7 is the divider plate (the RgbHairline's bread), -8 the frieze band's plaque, and both read as CHROME — right for a plate the card is BUILT from, wrong for one it CLASSIFIES itself on, where the strip stopped being part of the card and became a fourth divider with chips lying on it. -6 is the card's own LINE ink, so the band was that rule given height and filled. **The round trip is the finding**: every step of it argued DEPTH, and depth was the wrong axis — -6/-7/-8 differ only in how much like chrome the band reads, and this row is the one thing on the card that is neither chrome nor content but CLASSIFICATION. At the shallow end it is the shallowest figure on the square, lighter than the card's own line ink instead of equal to it, so the strip stopped competing with the two rgb rules then bracketing it and read as a shelf the chips stand on (the rules are gone now — the level survived them, and with the rim back the floor is a step under its own edge, which is what a shelf is). ⚠ **-4 was one step too far, and for a reason worth keeping**: that level is the **feed BED's** tone, so card and field met at exactly one place and that place sat between two rgb rules — the band read as a window through the card to the surface under it, a real device but not what a classification strip is. **`--grey-5`** keeps everything the shallow end bought and takes the coincidence back; it belongs to no other figure on this surface. What separates the tiers now is HUE (`--indigo-2` plate, `--deep-purple-2` members), not the floor. ⚠ **THEN IT WENT BACK TO BEING AN INSET PANEL** (the day's last rail ask, and the same ask that deleted the rgb pair above — the two are ONE decision): `.post-square__rail-strip` takes `padding: 4px 7px` again and `.post-square__rail` a **`6px` radius** and an **EVEN `1px` `--grey-6` rim**. Three dials, one reading — the rail is a box set INTO the card, not a band spanning it, and it has to stand off the card's edges or its corners curve against them. The rim is EVEN this time, where the old one was `1px/2px-top` to mirror the card's own uneven border (that weight said "something sits down onto this"; nothing sits on a free-standing panel). The vertical air is on the STRIP, not the band — it is card showing around the panel, and it replaces the lane the two rgb bands used to hold; the band keeps its own thin `2px` inside the rim for the chips. ⚠ The floor was tried at **`--grey-4`** once more and came straight back to `--grey-5` on the next ask. Worth recording because the try was principled: -4's objection (it is the feed BED's tone, so a full-bleed band read as a WINDOW through the card) had genuinely EXPIRED with the inset — a rimmed plate standing on card material all round reads as a plate, not a hole. It came off anyway, so the level is settled by EXPERIMENT and not by that argument: -5 has been chosen over -4 twice now, under two different classifications of the row. Full walk: -4 → -6 → -7 → -6 → -8 → -6 → -4 → -5 → -4 → **-5**. Height 42 → 21 → 34 → **31px** (strip padding, rim, then the padding tightening to 2px against the new hairline's 1px). The rail's own SIDE padding came down `6px` → **`4px`** in the same run — the run-in before the first bundle plate — which may not reach 0: a scroller with no side lead reads as content already cut off at rest. ⚠ **THE THREE RAIL RADII ARE ONE NESTED FAMILY** since that run's last ask: rail **10px** › `.post-square__bundle` **7px** › `.post-square__bundle-item` **5px**, each inner corner roughly its parent minus the lane between them. The rail now OUT-ROUNDS the pit (7px), reversing the rule its original 6px was chosen under ("a hair under the pit's") — move one and move all three, or concentric boxes read pinched at the corners. **A `new_label` BUTTON closes the strip's right end** (`.post-square__rail-add`, wearing `.post-square__cap-act` so the card keeps ONE button language across cap, foot and rail). It sits OUTSIDE the rail, in the strip's flex row, because the rail is a horizontal SCROLLER — a control laid inside it slides off exactly on the cards carrying the most classification; the rail takes the slack (`flex: 1 1 auto` + `min-width: 0`) and the button stays put. ⚠ **It has no handler yet, on purpose**: attaching a label is `POST /skeletons/:id/labels {labelId}` behind `maker/LabelPicker.vue` (compact mode, `picked`, `excludeIds`) and is owner-gated, while a feed is mostly other people's posts — what the control should do on a foreign card is a product decision, and wiring the owner path alone would put a button on every card that 403s on most, which takes back the 8px the deleted bands paid for: the pit's media constant moved 276 → 278 → **276** again, the day's closing asks putting it back where it started by a different route. **THE CHIPS WERE REBUILT, THEN BUNDLED.** The rebuild first: they had carried a heavier foot (1px box, 2px bottom) since the rail was born; that foot became an `--indigo-11` → `--deep-purple-11` **gradient**, then grew `--grey-7` bread above and below it — the RgbHairline's sandwich at chip scale, 4px of edge under an 18px pill — and then **the whole construction came off**. What it was solving ("this chip sits ON something") is a DEPTH problem, and it was answering with colour and weight on a card that already runs three coloured devices down its length. A `0 1px 1px` shadow replaced it for two minutes and came off too. **BUNDLED BY ROOT (2026-08-10, user ask)** — the rail holds one plate per label TREE, not per label PATH. A post classified twice under one root printed that root twice; on a plumbing post the rail read `PATHCHAIN › … PATHCHAIN › …`, the same word at the head of every plate, and the root is the least informative step in a path besides. Now: a bundle reads `[ 🪐 :: ]` then one member plate per label of that tree. `labelBundles(item)` groups `labelPaths(item)` by `names[0]` into `{root, mark, items}`, each member spelling only the TAIL of its chain (`lp.path` rides along unsliced, so the tooltip is still the whole classification). Grouping only, never filtering — every label keeps its id, its link and its funnel; a bare-ROOT label states its own name in the member cell rather than rendering an empty one; order is `labelPaths`' order held by Map insertion, so user classification still sorts ahead of the PATHCHAIN families. Verified on a full feed page: 72 label paths → 42 bundles (30 marked PATHCHAIN, 12 name-only CLAUDE), 30 of them merging two members each. **The chrome did not change, it MOVED**, from `.post-square__label` to `.post-square__bundle`: coat `--grey-3` (a day's walk: `--grey-3` → `--deep-purple-1` → `--indigo-1` → `--light-cream` → back. The cream's argument was that the plate IS the card's own material lying in a band cut through the card; what it cost was legibility of the TIERS, since the members are greys and a warm sheet under cool tokens states hue where the arrangement wants depth. At -3 the plate is one step off its members' -2 and three off the band's -6 — band, sheet, token, three tones of one material), rim **`--indigo-2`** at `1px` (tone taken off `--indigo-1` on 2026-08-10, one step deeper because the weight had already left the highlight register; 0.5 → 0.75 → 1 across two asks, so the plate's edge is twice its members' 0.5px rims — the DIFFERENCE is the point, not the weight; ⚠ a full pixel leaves the sub-pixel register the rest of the rail is drawn in, and indigo-50 was the palest thing that level could do rather than a tone picked for a full pixel — which is what the move to -2 fixed), **plus a SECOND rim outside it since 2026-08-10** (user ask): a `box-shadow: 0 0 0 0.5px var(--grey-7)` ring. The two do different jobs, which is what makes two lines on a small plate legitimate — the indigo is a HIGHLIGHT (a lit lip against the plate's own coat) and never could state the plate against the BAND, since the band is grey and the lip near-white; with the rail floor up at `--grey-4` it has even less to push against. The grey ring is the OUTLINE the highlight cannot be, at the divider-plate tone, well under both band and coat. Read outward: coat → lit lip → drawn edge → band. ⚠ A RING, not a border or an `outline` — it costs no layout (a border would grow the plate and the rail's measured 24px with it), it follows the `4px` radius exactly, and it leaves `outline` free for focus; the plate's own `overflow: hidden` does not clip it, since overflow clips CONTENT, never a shadow outside the border box. `4px` corners, `overflow: hidden`. The chip's old rule was chip-on-tray restating card-on-bed one level down; it broke when the tray became a mid-grey band and the step widened from one to three levels, and what replaces it is stronger — the plate is not a step off its tray, it IS the card's material, and the strip is a hole cut through the card. Warmth is what makes that read: band, lines, pit and foot are all neutral, so these are the rail's one warm object and they are warm *because the card is*. The rim landed on indigo-50 after a walk that ran the greys all the way up (-7 → -6 → -5 → -4 → -3 → -2) and then stepped off them — at -6 it was exactly the band's own tone and did nothing; by -2 it had become a HIGHLIGHT rather than an outline; indigo-50 keeps that register and adds a COOL edge on a warm sheet, so the outline is stated by hue as well as by lightness. Sub-pixel because 1px is not the thinnest register and the platform already writes in that one. **The members are plates of their own** (`.post-square__bundle-item`): `--grey-2` on a `--grey-5` rim after a three-ask walk (-2 on -4 → -4 on -1 → here) that tried the pairing both ways up and settled on the conventional one — a pale coat with a darker rim. The inverted setting (mid coat, lit lip, echoing the plate's own highlight) is a real device and is why the walk happened, but it made every member as heavy as the sheet holding it. **Each member also carries an INNER RING since 2026-08-10** (user ask): `box-shadow: inset 0 0 0 0.5px var(--deep-purple-2)`, a level **minted for this one line** (Material 100, beside the family's two hover tones — deep purple's first RESTING role on the rail). With it the row has **one colour per TIER** — `--indigo-2` states the plate, `--deep-purple-2` states its members — which is what finally tells the two nested boxes apart after every edge here had been a grey separated only by depth, a job six steps at 0.5px could not do. Reading outward from a member: ring → member rim `--grey-5` → plate coat `--grey-3` → plate rim `--indigo-2` → plate ring `--grey-7` → band `--grey-4`. ⚠ **INSET, where the plate's ring is outset** — members sit flush against each other inside the plate, so an outward ring would land on the next token's and read as one doubled line down the row. That two-TIER reading is the whole point of bundling — a warm sheet per tree, cool tokens lying on it — and it is why the members carry no hairline between them the way the CAP's and the FOOT's cells do: a rule and a rim inside the same three pixels would be one seam drawn twice. The label itself is now bare TYPE inside its member; `--indigo-9` ink on hover. **The `::` seam** (`.post-square__bundle-sep`) stands between the tree and its labels — the card's own separator for "this term qualifies the next", as in the CAP's origin clause and the FOOT's `post :: skeleton :: <hash>`. It takes the MARK's tone rather than the labels' ink — both `--grey-9`, and the seam BOLD (four dots of ink have to hold their own between a 26px drawing and a bordered plate; weight costs no space where a size bump would push both neighbours apart) — because mark and seam are one utterance and the members answer it; `margin: 0 -2px` halves the plate's 4px gap around it, since punctuation is a joint between two objects and not a third one. **The plate's HOVER** is two dials, and the ask was precise about what not to do: coat → `--grey-2` (*not* the `--deep-purple-1` it took while this chrome belonged to a single chip — a plate that turns lilac states a SELECTION, and this one is a container, so what it should say is "you are in here", a step of tone rather than a change of hue), rim → `--deep-purple-11`. ⚠ At `--grey-2` the coat meets its members' own tone and the two flatten into one field, the `--grey-5` rims still telling them apart — that is the ask's arithmetic, not an oversight. The root's side answers together: mark and seam both → `--indigo-8` (walked `--indigo-11` → `--indigo-2` → here; pale accents looked like a wash on a drawing whose mass is stroke). **The funnel takes no width while hidden.** It was `opacity: 0` alone, which hides a control without UNPLACING it — 11px of icon plus its gap sat inside every member as dead space that looked like slack padding. It collapses to `width: 0` now and opens on `.post-square__rail:hover`, the same trigger as before, so the strip shifts once as a whole rather than each member twitching. ⚠ Its `display: inline-flex; line-height: 1` is load-bearing, not tidiness: as a default `inline-block` button the glyph sat on a text baseline and measured ~22px for an 11px icon, which cost nothing while it stood outside the chip and drove the rail 21 → 28px the moment bundling moved it inside a member. Hover ink `--indigo-8`, replacing the hard-coded `#00829c` teal — the last of that generic interactive tone left in this rail. Rail geometry after all of it: bundle 20.03px, rail 24px (the band's own vertical padding came back at a thin `2px` — ⚠ on `.post-square__rail`, *not* on `.post-square__rail-strip`, which would float the band in a gap between the two `RgbHairline`s instead of giving the plates room inside it). Card chrome 148px; media budget **276 / expanded twin 302**, the resting one now **px-only** (the frieze band was its lone viewport-relative term and left the card on 2026-08-22; the expanded twin keeps a `1 × --frieze-h`, but that one is the WELL's padding). ⚠ 310 → 302 also un-lags the expanded copy: the same day's density pass took the well's paddings 22/12 → 18/8 and this constant kept charging the old 34 (280/314 until 2026-08-10: +2 for the frieze band's new grey-6 rules, −6 for the pit's halved margin. An inner pit HEADER lived between those two edits — a bold post name over a grey-6 hairline, which put the pair at 295/329 — and was **removed the same day at the user's word**; nothing of it survives but the sticky lesson in `specs/gotchas.md`). Two things the gradient pass is worth remembering for: a gradient **cannot** be painted on one border edge as a `bottom / 100% Npx` strip — `border-radius` clips the rectangle and the colour dies before the corners, which is exactly how that foot looked wrong; the way that works is a full **border-box** background layer with the coat clipped to **padding-box**, so what shows of the gradient is the border RING and it follows the radius like a real border. And ⚠ `background-origin` must be restated after every `background` shorthand — the shorthand resets it, and the `:hover` rule silently moved the foot 2px up the chip until it was. Both are in [gotchas](../../specs/gotchas.md). The strip ELEMENT survives its padding: a divider on this band has to run lip to lip, which means the full-width strip and never the inset rail. `FeedPostPanel.vue` now has TWO live consumers: `FeedPageLegacy` (the restore point) and the flyout. The **container's box is the frame, so the scroller lives here, not on the window**: a `.feed-stream__head` band on top (padded `calc(var(--frieze-h) + 8px)` so the title clears the crown strip the container runs up over) — **moved INSIDE the well and made `position: sticky; top: 0` at the end of 2026-07-25**, which is the whole reason the cards can travel behind it: as a sibling ABOVE the well it was a separate box, the well clipped its own content, and nothing could ever pass under the band (a translucent coat there would have shown only the container's flat plaque). It **was GLASS** for one day — `rgba(var(--indigo-3-rgb), 0.82)` over `backdrop-filter: blur(5px)` (`-webkit-` prefixed too, Safari still needs it), retired for a flat `--indigo-1` plaque at the end of 2026-07-25, see **(E)** below — with a `2px solid var(--indigo-3)` bottom edge — the band's own tone at full opacity, so the border reads as the solid edge of a translucent band rather than a separate rule laid under it (it was a generic `rgba(var(--ink-rgb), .14)` hairline, then briefly `--indigo-2`). That was the **one deliberate exception to the surface's flat-plaque rule**, which bans sheen and glass everywhere else — the rule is universal here again since (E). The alpha rose from `.72` when the blur arrived: at `.72` with no blur the text travelling under the band still read as *text*, competing with the title; the blur softens it to movement and the extra opacity settles the rest. The band is also **short** — `padding: 8px 12px`, symmetric. It used to carry `calc(var(--frieze-h) + 8px)` on top to clear the crown strip the container runs up over; now that it is its own surface it **stands in for the strip** across the container's width instead of sitting below it, the same move the drawer and the stack widget already make where they meet the top edge (band height 82.6px → 55.6px). `margin: 0 -5px 10px` seats it — side negatives cancel the well's side padding so the band stays full-bleed frieze lip to frieze lip (both were `10px` until 2026-08-06, when the padding halved and this had to halve with it), the bottom margin is the 10px gap to the first card. **The margin-top is `0` and the well's `padding-top` went to `0` with it, NOT cancelled by a negative margin** — sticky constrains the margin box, so `-10px` there is undone by `top: 0` and drops the band 10px below the container's top edge while swallowing the card gap; see [gotchas.md](../../specs/gotchas.md). Verified: band top − well top = `0` at rest AND after scrolling, gap to card₁ = 10px, band width = well width, 56px of card₁ behind the band at `scrollTop: 260`. **THE BAND IS RETIRED — 2026-08-06, user ask**: the head is a draggable BOX now, `posts/FeedHeadBox.vue` (its own row above), placed over the well instead of pinned inside it, and `.feed-stream__head` is gone with all four of the rules this paragraph exists to explain — the sticky pinning, the `margin: 0 -3px 10px`, the `--grey-4` coat and the `2px --indigo-3` edge. Each was an answer to *a plate living at the top of a padded scroller*, and the head does not live there any more: it spans the field by construction, hides the cards by being opaque rather than by being pinned, and draws its own two edges. What the stream KEPT is the band's right-hand end — the sub-line and the lens cluster, handed to the box through its `controls` slot, still styled here, now stacked under a new `.feed-stream__controls` (a centred flex row that SCROLLS sideways rather than wrapping — a wrap would silently make the whole head taller) because the slot is half a box wide instead of a whole band. **THE CLUSTER IS FOUR DROPDOWNS, A FUNNEL AND THE COUNT (2026-08-07, three user asks)** — each dropdown one 21px `.feed-stream__label-open` plate wearing a glyph and, beside it, a `.feed-stream__lens-n` MARK that states what the lens is set to. ⚠ **That shape lasted a day**: later on 2026-08-07 the count moved to the box's header, SORT BY followed it, the label funnel became a row of its own, and the three that were left went into a `QBtnGroup` — so `.feed-stream__label-open` no longer exists and the cluster is TWO BARS, not one row. The per-lens notes below are still current; see the `FeedHeadBox.vue — the 2026-08-07 board pass` row for the arrangement. The lenses were: **TRUST** (`.feed-stream__hops`, `connect_without_contact` + the radius — `∞` for the open feed, else 1/2/3 → `?maxHops`; it was a four-plate segmented control, ~100px spent naming the three radii you are NOT under, and folding it left the row 9px narrower even after two lenses were added beside it), **DATE** (`.feed-stream__when`, **`calendar_month`** + a short word → `?from=&to=` — WHICH DAYS. It was the "time lens" and owned the clock too until **2026-08-08, when the user asked for the pair to be split**: one control answering both questions answered neither fully — you could say "since Tuesday 09:00" but never "mornings". Menu = six presets over a custom-range drawer of two native **`date`** fields, either end optional, plus APPLY. ⚠ The split trades away an instant-precise window FROM THIS DRAWER; `?from=`/`?to=` still take full ISO and the spoken lens still resolves them, so the capability is in the platform, not in this tray. It builds the same `when` object the spoken lens's clause does, so `resolveWhenLocal` stays the one resolver and "today" is the VIEWER's today), **TIME** (`.feed-stream__clock`, `schedule` + `a.m.`/`p.m.`/`eve`/`night`/`9–5` or the hand-typed pair itself → **`?timeFrom=&timeTo=&tzOffset=`**, new with the 2026-08-08 split) — WHAT HOURS, on whatever days the calendar beside it admits. It is the question a window cannot ask: a window is two instants on the line, "mornings" is a pair of clock hands repeating on every day inside it, and the two COMPOSE (measured: open feed 115 → date=7d 7 → +morning 4; clearing the clock returns to 7 with only `from`/`to` on the wire). Menu = five presets over two native `time` fields. `tzOffset` is `getTimezoneOffset()` verbatim so morning means the READER's morning — the same promise `resolveWhenLocal` makes for "today" — and a wrapping pair (22:00 → 06:00) is nights, written server-side as two ranges since one BETWEEN matches nothing. ⚠ NOT part of the spoken `FilterSpec` (the seat has a `when` clause and no clock), so unlike the date and identity buttons this lens has no twin to drop and nothing arrives here from a lens reply — if the seat is ever taught hours, this is the state it would write. State: `TOD_OPTS`/`todWin`/`todKey`/`todMark`/`todTitle`/`setTodWin`/`applyTodCustom`; the date lens's own were renamed `DATE_OPTS`/`dateWin`/… in the same pass. The bundle is FOUR buttons now (measured 42/42/42/40 in its row), calendar to the LEFT of the clock as asked), **IDENTITY** (`.feed-stream__who`, `person_search` + the count of picked seats → `?authors=<csv>`; menu = a debounced `refs/search?kind=entities` field over the picked chips — faces, click to drop — over the results as EntityAvatar + name + @handle. Picks union and the menu stays open for several; it filters the EXACT seats picked, never their masks, per the 2026-08-04 seat-strict doctrine) and **SORT BY**. The time and identity buttons write the same parameters the spoken lens's `when`/`authors` clauses do, so each drops its spoken twin when used and yields to an arriving spoken one — one clause, one place, and the box never states a filter the stream is not under. Menu content is TELEPORTED to `<body>`, so every rule inside these plaques states raw `--indigo-*` tokens: the head box's `--fhead-ink`/`--fhead-rule` dials do not reach outside the box (see [gotchas.md](../../specs/gotchas.md)). `.feed-stream__heading` is deleted; the name is the box's bar title. **The well changed with it in two places:** `.feed-stream-pane` took `position: relative` (the box's containing block — and deliberately NO z-index, or the pane becomes a stacking context and the flyout's 3002 starts ranking against it as a unit, the trap `.feed-page` already documents), and the well's `padding-top` came BACK as `calc(var(--fhead-h, 118px) + 20px)` — the box's measured height (published down from `setup()` as a px custom property, same reasoning as `--post-square-max`) plus its resting offset plus the gap to card₁, so at rest the stream begins exactly where it began under the band. ⚠ **THE TWO CONSTANTS CAME DOWN IN THE 2026-08-22 DENSITY PASS** (user ask, "reduce the overall padding around them to make the feed fit denser"): the well's top term is `--fhead-h + 18px` (12px HOME offset, which clears the head box's corner sweeps, + the stream's new **6px** flex gap, from 10) and its bottom `--frieze-h + 8px` (from 12), with the sides at **3px** (from 8 → 5 the same day). Both top and bottom are RESTATED in the expanded card's height subtraction and `fsck --static`'s `expanded-card` witness fails the build if the pair drifts — grep `KEEP THE SUBTRACTION IN STEP`, there are exactly two sites. (Historical measurement, at the retired 10px gap and a 117px box: box top = 10px inside the pane, well padding-top 137, gap box→card₁ = 10.) It does NOT follow the box down: the reserve is the head's HOME SLOT, and a stream that reflowed while you dragged would turn the drag into a resize — drag the head away and the reveal it leaves is where it came from) and one `.feed-stream__well` (`overflow-y: auto`, bottom padding `calc(var(--frieze-h) + 12px)` — sized to clear the frieze footer band the container hovered over until that band's 2026-08-04 retirement; the breathing room at the window floor stays, and — since 2026-07-25 — the SCROLL BED: **`background: var(--indigo-2)`** then **`var(--grey-4)` on 2026-08-05** (user ask), flush with the container and its two frieze edges, and — after an afternoon of walking on 2026-08-06 (`--grey-5`, a sunk tray; `--grey-3`, a lit page) — **back on `var(--grey-4)`, the plaque, in the ask that also removed the well's side padding and took the frieze lips to the same tone**. Those three go together: bars, container, head band and bed are ONE material with no edge anywhere between them, so the box is continuous lip to lip. The step did not vanish, it MOVED — the last ask of the day put the CARD at `--grey-3`, one above the bed — and that is the right way up: a lighter bed makes the box a page with darker sheets on it, a lighter card keeps the box reading as one material with its own frieze edges. The bed is the only part of the field a reader ever actually SEES — it covers the container edge to edge — so leaving it indigo while the box went neutral would have made that change true in the stylesheet and invisible on screen. The tonal step it used to hold under the cards is not lost, it changed sign: the `--indigo-1` cards separated by HUE and by their own outline rather than by sitting a shade above their bed — and after 2026-08-06 the step is back and pointing the other way: a pale card on a `--grey-4` bed. Both parts moved again on 2026-08-07 (two user asks): the LINES went `--grey-6` ("from indigo to grey") and then the COAT went warm — `--brown-1` on "just the background", and an hour later the minted **`--light-cream` (#FCF3E0)** — so the card is a cream sheet on a cool plate, edged in neutral. **It became THREE LAYERS in the last ask of that day**: the cream coat + its `--grey-5` border (2026-08-22 — **the card's whole line system moved to `--grey-5`**, all eleven declarations, from the `--grey-6` it used since 2026-08-07: the outer edge first as a repair when the bed briefly stood at -6 and swallowed it, then the ten inner hairlines as a choice. The one-ink rule is whole at the new level — don't move one line back), then a VEIL over them (`.post-square::before` — **`--grey-3` at 75%** since 2026-08-22, "one tone darker of grey and 10% less transparent"; it walked `--grey-3` 70% → 60% → `--grey-2` 80% → 65% on 2026-08-07). **That wash is the token `--card-veil` since 2026-08-23** and this rule reads it: a NodeMini quoted into a post had to wear this same coat (user ask), and rather than paste the `rgba()` a second time the wash was tokenised and `--card-coat` composes it over `--light-cream` as one `background` layer list for whoever needs the sandwich whole — one declaration of the wash, one of the sheet. The veil stays a LAYER rather than folding into the fill because it is inset to the border, which is what keeps the card's own outer line undimmed with `backdrop-filter: blur(2px)` and a **2px `--grey-1` border at full strength** of its own, filling the padding box so the card's line keeps its own tone, turning its OWN 7px corner (= the card's 8px radius − its 1px border — a clip cannot bend a border), `pointer-events: none` so it eats no clicks), then the CONTENT above both (`.post-square > *` at `position: relative; z-index: 1`, with `isolation: isolate` on the card so that pair of z-indexes cannot mean anything outside it). **Measured** off the rendered card, left edge inward: `x=1` `rgb(158,158,158)` the card's `--grey-6` line, `x=2` `rgb(248,247,246)` the veil's own hairline, `x=3+` `rgb(242,239,234)` the veiled field, against a bed of `rgb(245,245,245)` outside. ⚠ So **card and field are only THREE levels apart and the card is the DARKER of the two** — inverted from the arrangement held since 2026-08-06 (darker sheets on a light page, not pale sheets on a grey plate) and the thinnest margin this pair has run at. The card is stated by its outline rather than by tone: **TWO LINES, dark outside and light inside**, a bevel whose hairline is the brightest thing on the surface. Legitimate as a very quiet register; the dial that widens it is the FIELD, not the wash (`--grey-4` held the wash for one setting at 13 levels). The veil's border shows anything at all only because `background-clip` defaults to `border-box`: the wash paints under it, so the border tone composites over its own washed self. It went `6px --light-cream` at 50% (*"thick transparent borders"*) → `1px --grey-2` at 80% (*"waay thinner"*) → **`1px --grey-1` at 80%**, and the first swap is a TONE lesson rather than a width one — a border in the COAT's own tone is capped by how far the wash has moved the coat, since it can only give some of it back, while a border off the card's axis states whatever value it likes. One pixel of a free tone says more than six of a capped one. ⚠ **THE ONE RULE TO KEEP FROM THE WASH'S WALK.** It ran `--grey-3` 30% → 50% → `--grey-2` 50% → `--grey-3` 70% → `--grey-4` → back to -3 → -4 → **-3 again**, and the repeated `--grey-4` settings are not dithering — they were measured against **different beds**. The first time the bed was `--grey-4` too, so the card's coat converged on the field it lies on (`rgb(232,229,224)` against `rgb(224,224,224)` — eight levels of red, NONE of blue), the step vanished and the card's shape fell entirely to its outline. Then the FIELD moved (`--grey-5`, then `--grey-2`), and against a `--grey-2` bed the same wash read 13 levels darker: a clear object again, just inverted. **A wash may not be, or approach, the bed's tone — the rule is the relation, not the token.** Measure after each change, the dials interact. At 70% the coat is doing HUE and the veil is doing VALUE. The veil tints only the card's OWN exposed coat, since every inner panel is content and sits above it (the pit keeps its flat `--grey-1`, the title plate its `--grey-1`, the rail its `--grey-4` tray), so the veil reads in the gutters BETWEEN panels, which is where a card's coat is visible at all. The blur is nearly a no-op by construction and that is understood, not overlooked: a backdrop filter samples what is painted behind it, and behind this one is a flat fill, so it shows only as a faint perimeter haze where the border's tone is pulled a pixel or two inward — raising the radius widens that edge and does nothing to the middle. Both traps (paint order putting an `::after`/`::before` veil over sibling TEXT, and flat-backdrop blur) are in [gotchas](../../specs/gotchas.md) — hue is doing work again, but carried by the OBJECT this time rather than by the field. For the one day between those asks the coat and the line were both neutral and the line alone was load-bearing; do not thin it without moving one of the two tones. It **walked the whole colorway in one day**: -4 (deep, when the cards were full-bleed and the bed alone had to separate them), then -3, then -2 once the card gained its own `--indigo-4` outline — the outline took over the separating, so a dark field underneath was doing that work twice. -2 was also the one step NOT in the frieze bars' tone mapping of the day (1/3/4), so the bed read as its own quiet layer rather than echoing an edge — an argument that has expired several times over since 2026-08-05: the bed is neutral (`--grey-4`, briefly `--grey-5` and `--grey-3`, back to `--grey-4`) and the bars kept no indigo but their WAVES for two days — the lip walked -6 → -5 → -4 → `--grey-4` on 2026-08-06 and draws nothing at all. **2026-08-07 reversed that**: the bars are `--indigo-8` plaques under `-1`/`-3` waves, with the lip following the plaque, so the indigo on this surface is now the FRAME (four frieze edges + the head box) and the bed and cards are what carry no colorway. The head band above wears the container's coat, whatever it is — `--indigo-1` from 2026-07-25, `--grey-4` since 2026-08-05, following by construction, since the band's whole definition is *the container showing through the stream* (its `--indigo-3` bottom edge stays: that line states where the band ends and belongs to the colorway, not to the plate) (it was transparent over `.feed-container` before the glass, and states that same tone explicitly since (E)), so the box reads as chrome over its field, and the cards are light plaques lying ON that field. What SEPARATES one card from the next has moved with the bed, and the cards cast no shadow, so this is worth stating exactly: it was a tonal step while the bed was `--indigo-2` (cards a shade above their well), it became hue plus the outline when the bed went `--grey-4` under `--indigo-1` cards, and since the end of 2026-08-06 it is a tonal step PLUS a line — a `--grey-3` card on the `--grey-4` plaque, edged all round in `--indigo-4`; 2026-08-07 then took the line to **`--grey-6`** and the coat to **`--light-cream`** (`#FCF3E0`, via one hour on `--brown-1`), so it is a tonal step plus a line plus a HUE the object carries. **The well also carries SIDE and TOP padding** (end of 2026-07-25 — it had neither for most of that day, when the cards were full-bleed): the side padding is the gap between the frieze bars and the cards — `10px`, then `5px`, then `0` ("remove completely"), then `3px` (all four on 2026-08-06, the last "just a little little"), and **`8px` since 2026-08-07** (*"a little more padding on the sides from the frieze bars"*). Three pixels was a SLIVER, not a gap: the smallest reveal that keeps the card's own border from LANDING ON the frieze bar, which was the whole job while the bar was `--grey-4` and the card's edge was the only line at the seam. What changed the job is the same day's other ask — the bars went `--indigo-8`, and two objects that far apart in tone need SPACE rather than a hairline of daylight; at 3px the pale card looked stuck to the dark bar. The `0` pass is why the bar's lip draws nothing — at zero the card's edge and the bar's met as one line at the seam — and the lip stayed plaque-toned through every later setting, so the gap does the separating. At `8px` against the `10px` vertical rhythm the reveal is now NEARLY EVEN where it was deliberately uneven; the last 2px are not obviously an improvement, since the sides are read against a hard vertical edge and the top/bottom against another card. Note this is no longer 2026-07-25's full-bleed arrangement either way: that pass zeroed the card's side borders and radius *because* they would collide with the lip, and the card has been a complete four-edged box throughout this one. The top padding is the gap under the head band, which the first card sat flush against until it was added. **The head band's negative side margins are exactly `-1 x` that padding** (`margin: 0 -3px 10px`) — they cancel it so the BAND stays full-bleed lip to lip while the CARDS keep the sliver; at `0` they must be `0` too, or a negative margin with nothing to cancel pushes the band out over the frieze bars. The top padding can only ever affect that first card — every card below it is spaced by the stream's flex `gap` — and `10px` matches that `gap`, so the bed's reveal was one uniform margin on all four sides of every card (measured: head-band→card₁ = 10px, card₁→card₂ = 10px). **The sides broke that symmetry deliberately on 2026-08-06**: 5px at the lips against 10px vertically, which is the one asymmetry on this surface and the one to restate if the padding is ever touched again. The bottom padding is the exception and has a job of its own: it cleared the frieze footer band the container hovered over (band retired 2026-08-04; the clearance stays as the last card's breathing room at the window floor). It narrows `.feed-stream`, which is the element the square ceiling is measured from (615 → 595px at a 1440 viewport) — the `ResizeObserver` picks that up on its own and `--post-square-max` follows, so nothing else needed touching) — so the page itself never scrolls (verified: `documentElement.scrollHeight` stays 900 at 900px tall with 30 cards / 4854px of stream in a 770px well). `useStateHolder` runs with **`trackScroll: false`** + `trackContainer(wellEl, 'feed')` for that reason, and remembers the unfolded card in `selectedKey`; unlike the legacy page it **pre-selects nothing** — with the panel inline there is no empty side column to fill and an auto-unfolded first card would only push the stream down. `min-width: 0` on the pane, the well and `.post-square__name` is **load-bearing geometry, not tidiness** — see the `min-width: auto` note on the `/feed` row. **The cards stopped being the legacy ones on 2026-07-25** (they were verbatim copies until then) — two changes, shape and body: **(A) FULL-BLEED (then GAPPED), SQUARE-CEILINGED squares.** A card's horizontal extension is **100% of the column** — `.feed-stream__well` lost its `12px` side padding (`padding: 0 0 calc(var(--frieze-h) + 12px)`) and `.post-square` its `border-left`/`border-right` and `border-radius`, so the card ran frieze lip to frieze lip and the body got the container's whole width to portray itself in. **The well took side padding back at the end of that day** — `10px`, halved to `5px` and then removed entirely on 2026-08-06 (so the cards are full-bleed once more, but drawn as complete boxes), to open a gap between the frieze bars and the cards (see the well's own note above) — so the cards are no longer literally full-bleed, though they still fill 100% of the column *inside* that padding. **The card was then drawn as a complete box again**: a border on all four edges plus `border-radius: var(--radius-md)` (11.9px as computed; **a flat `4px` since 2026-08-06 — two user asks that day, `--radius-sm` (7px, the step the card's inner boxes turn) and then "even less"; flat px because there is no platform step below -sm and minting `--radius-xs` for one box would state a rhythm that does not exist**), and the side borders run within `3px` of the frieze lips, the well's side padding having gone to `0` and back to a sliver that same day, replacing the `border-left/right: 0` + `border-radius: 0` + `rgba(var(--ink-rgb), 0.16)` of the full-bleed pass. The side border had been zeroed *because* it would have collided with the frieze lip it touched; the well's padding removed that constraint, and with it the reason the card drew only two of its four edges. It landed as `1px solid var(--indigo-4)` — one step DARKER than the bed, so the outline read rather than dissolved — and **settled on `--indigo-3`** at the end of the day. Lighter *and* thicker is one move rather than two opposing ones: a hairline needs contrast to register, a wider band does not, so the outline could drop to the colorway's lip tone and still read — as a drawn EDGE rather than a dark line. That also puts the card's outer border on the same ink as its inner one (the pit's) and as the frieze lips, which was the division of labour on this surface until 2026-08-05/06 — **-1 coats container and card, -2 is the bed, -3 draws EVERY line, -4 is left to the frieze bars' wave layer** — and every one of those four steps has since moved (grey-4 coat, grey-3 bed, -4 lines, -4 also the lip); what survived is the SHAPE of the rule — one coat, one bed, one line ink — including the last of those, which came within one ask of being two (a bevel: colorway on bottom + left + inside, neutral on top + right) before the user put all four edges back on `--indigo-4`. The weight is **EVEN on all four edges since 2026-08-06** and, after two asks on 2026-08-07 ("slightly thicker" → "a little thinner"), **1.5px** — exact at 2×, floored to 1px at 1×, and a real position rather than a compromise: the inner lines are 1px, so the edge has to outweigh them to read as the card's outline, and 2px made it the heaviest line on the card by double once the dividers inside it thinned. Its INK spent an hour of that day out of `--grey-6` — to `--grey-7`, following the dividers, then `--grey-8` (a "one tone down" read as darker) — and came home on the third ask ("clearer"). **So the card's oldest rule is intact on every LINE it draws** (inner and outer alike, `--grey-6`, only the weight telling them apart) and what the day actually added is a second group that is not lines at all: the two DIVIDER PLATES, `--grey-7` for the rgb hairline's rules and `--grey-8` for the frieze band's plaque. An edge a step under its own inner lines read as a box around a box. Move the lines together; the plates move on their own. It was UNEVEN from the end of 2026-07-25 (`border-bottom-width: 2px`, a hairline box with a heavier FOOT; 2px all round before that read heavy, the border being the card's only line). What retired the foot is that the bed stopped being something to sit ON: at `--grey-3` it is LIGHTER than the card, so a weighted base under a box resting on nothing darker read as a shadow with no caster. The old rationale, kept because it is the reason the shape existed: one weighted base was enough to seat the card on a darker bed, written as a 1px box plus a single overridden edge rather than a four-value `border-width`, because that is what the intent says. Verified across the stream: `1px` top/left/right, `2px` bottom, one distinct border color `rgb(176,190,197)`, 0 cards deviating. Its **coat is the CONTAINER's coat** (2026-07-25): `background: var(--indigo-1)`, the same plaque tone the feed box and its frieze lips wear — NOT the generic white `--paper-card` every other square on the platform (`.label-square`, the legacy feed) uses. A post square was a piece of this container, cut from the container's material, so card + bed + edges made three tones of ONE colorway (`--indigo-1` card ⇔ `--indigo-4` well ⇔ the frieze bars' `1/3/4` wave stack). **Since 2026-08-05 the card is the only one of the three left in it** — bed and plate are `--grey-4`, the bars' waves settled at `-4`/`-6` — so the card reads as an object ON the plate rather than as more of it, which is the arrangement that ask was after. Its height is **`min(content, width, 60vh)`**: the square is a **CEILING, not a fixed shape** — a short post stays a short band whose height simply follows its content, a long one grows down until it is exactly as tall as it is wide *or 40% of the window, whichever comes first,* and stops. That is `max-height: min(var(--post-square-max, 100cqw), 60vh)`. **The `60vh` is the absolute cap (2026-07-26 — the number is a DIAL and has been turned three times: `30vh` on 2026-07-25's fifth pass, opened to `40vh` the same day)** and it is there because *a square is a ratio, not a size*: the width ceiling alone says nothing about how much SCREEN a card may take, so on a wide column (or a short window) a square card is simply a tall card — at 615px the cards were 615px tall against a 852px slot, one card per screen, and the stream stopped reading as a stream. `min()` is the operator, not a second `max-height` declaration beneath the first (which would replace it, not narrow it). Nothing else in the card had to change: both limits bear on the same flexible pit, so whichever bites trims the body and spares the foot. Measured at 900px tall: every card `max-height: 360px`, `360 / 900 = 40.0%` exactly, ~2 cards in the well. **`--post-square-max` is the column's width MEASURED in JS** (a `ResizeObserver` on `.feed-stream` in `setup()` writes `clientWidth` onto the element, live across drawer toggles and window resizes) and it comes FIRST on purpose: the pure-CSS statement of the same rule, `100cqw` against `container-type: inline-size` on `.feed-stream`, is correct but **all-or-nothing** — a container-query unit an engine does not understand takes the whole declaration down at parse time, leaving the cards with *no ceiling at all* and letting a 7 000-character post render at its full natural height. A px custom property cannot fail that way; the `cqw` fallback covers the one frame before the observer runs. Either way the width has to come from the ANCESTOR — an element cannot read its own width in its own `max-height`, and the well above is off by its padding/scrollbar. `aspect-ratio: 1` would have been the wrong tool: it *forces* height = width and would leave short posts sitting in a dead white field. The card is a flex **column** so the clip lands where it should — `.post-square__byline`, `.post-square__head` and `.post-square__foot` are `flex: 0 0 auto`, `.post-square__pit` is the one flexible track (`flex: 1 1 auto; min-height: 0; overflow: hidden`) — and, since 2026-08-23, **the publisher of `--quoted-bleed-x`** (user ask: "remove the padding between the mini node viewer's right border and the post card's content container's right border, and also the left"). The pit IS that content container, and its 10px side padding was the whole gap. It declares the inset ONCE, uses it for its own `padding: 8px var(--quoted-bleed-x)`, and a quoted panel pulls itself back out by exactly that much (`NodeMini`'s `margin-left/right: calc(-1 * var(--quoted-bleed-x, 0px))`) — the same publisher/consumer seam as `--media-max-h`, so the number is stated where it is a fact and consumed everywhere else, never mirrored. Negative margins rather than a width: a block box with `width: auto` grows into the parent's PADDING area, which is inside the padding box, so the pit's `overflow-x: hidden` has nothing to clip. ⚠ The `0px` fallback (not `0` — a unitless zero is invalid inside `calc()` and would drop the declaration) is what leaves every non-publishing host untouched; verified on `/labels/9`, where the same panel measures `0px / 0px`, so when the ceiling bites it is the **body** that gives way and the byline's author + the foot's tallies (a post's ORIGIN) are never what gets cut off. Verify by ratio, not by eye, and **measure every card, not the first few** — and check that nothing ELSE in `.feed-stream` is taller than the column is wide, which is how the unfolded panel hid in plain sight: `[...document.querySelectorAll('.feed-stream > *')].filter(el => el.getBoundingClientRect().height > colW)` must come back empty. Measured before the `30vh` cap, 30 cards at three viewports (1440/1920/1280 → columns 615/844/546): worst `height − width` = **0px**, nothing over-tall in the stream, and clicking anywhere in a card changes neither. Since the cap the width limit is the *slack* one at these columns (360px ceiling vs 615px width), so `height > colW` stays empty for free — assert on the window instead: `maxCardHeight / window.innerHeight` must read `0.40`. **(B) The body is RENDERED MARKDOWN, compressed.** Post bodies are markdown, so the pit no longer prints the raw excerpt as text — it mounts `MarkdownBody` (the platform's one pipeline, `ref-display="auto"` since 2026-07-31 — was `micro`: bare node refs to embeddable URLs / media files bloom into the teal `NodeMini` panel right on the card, everything else stays a micro chip; `![[…]]` / `-[[…]]` sigils overrule per ref, see `docs/concepts/references.md`) over `postBody(item)`. **And it is the WHOLE post, not a preview** (2026-07-25, second pass): the stream loads `feedService.getPublic({ limit: 30, body: 'full' })`, so each item carries its full markdown body and a card is an *actual post container*; `excerpt` (the 280-char slice every other feed consumer reads) stays only as the fallback for an item the server resolved no body for, and only that path wears the truncation ellipsis, appended INTO the markdown source (a separate `<span>…</span>` would render as a stray glyph after the last block). Two consequences: the pit **scrolls in place** (`overflow-y: auto`, the well's own thin rail) — which is what settles the two rules the card lives by, *no taller than it is wide* and *holds the whole post*: hidden overflow would make the square a truncation, a scroller makes it a **window** — and the pit carries `@click.stop`, so the body is a reading surface (select text, follow a link) while the head strip and foot stay the card's select-toggle. ⚠ **THE CARD CLOSED IN ON IT on 2026-08-10** (two user asks): the pit's margin — the CARD's gap around the reading area, not the pit's own breathing room — halved from `6px 7px 7px` to **`3px 4px 4px`**, and its radius went **`7px` → `3px`**. Seven was `--radius-sm`, right for a panel FLOATING on a surface and wrong for a frame set INTO one, especially once the pit sat nearly flush with the card's own `4px` corners; three is the tightest step still visibly a curve. Its `padding: 8px 10px` did NOT move — what shrank is the frame, not the sheet the prose sits on, which is why the change reads as a bigger reading area on an unchanged card. **The pit is dressed in two tones** (end of 2026-07-25): `background: var(--grey-1)` floor + `border: 1px solid var(--indigo-3)`. They pull in opposite directions on purpose — the floor is the one tone deliberately OUTSIDE the container's colorway (a tint that made the pit a darker patch OF the card, `rgba(var(--ink-rgb), 0.05)`, became a flat near-white that is a different material set INTO it), while the line is the colorway's own lip/hairline ink, the same one the frieze bars draw the container's side borders with, so the card's inner edge and the box's outer edges are one line. The border landed in the same pass the **card's drop shadow was removed** (`box-shadow: 0 1px 3px` gone from `.post-square`, and the `box-shadow` half of its `transition` with it): the card has nothing to lift off now that the well beneath it is a darker bed, the tonal step alone separates card from field, and a cast edge only muddied the 10px gap between adjacent cards — so with no shadow, the indigo-3 line is what states where the reading area begins. **The surface was then flattened outright** (end of 2026-07-25): the pit's own inset carve — the `.label-square__pit` recipe, an inset dark shadow at the top over an inset white lip at the bottom — is gone as well, so **nothing on a card casts anything**. Depth from two directions at once (a recessed pit inside a flat card on a flat bed) is what left the surface unresolved; tone and line state everything now — grey-1 floor against the indigo-1 card, indigo-3 border around it, indigo-4 around the card. Verified over the whole stream: 0 elements with a computed `box-shadow` other than `none`, across all 30 cards **and every descendant** — the assertion has to walk the subtree, since removing the card's own shadow says nothing about the pit's. **`:breaks="false"` is load-bearing.** Post bodies are hard-wrapped at ~72 chars; under the platform default every source line becomes a `<br>` and the paragraph lays out at the AUTHOR's wrap width — text that looks cut off at half the container (measured: prose filled ~50% of the pit, `br` count in the dozens; after, `br=0` and lines fill **96–99%**). Body type went `0.82em` → **`0.95em`** with `line-height: 1.5` in the same pass — a reading surface, not a preview strip; every size in the compressed tier is relative to it, so the whole markdown scale moved with it. **It settled at `0.88em` / `line-height: 1.6` at the end of 2026-07-25**: a notch smaller fits more of a post inside the card's ceiling AND gives justified prose more words per line to distribute (the narrower the measure, the uglier the gaps justification must open), while smaller type wants proportionally more leading. `.post-square__md :deep(.markdown-body)` is the **compressed tier**: every construct keeps the meaning it has in the full renderer — headings mono-bold on a tight scale (h1 `1.14em` → h4-h6 `0.94em` uppercase), `disc`/`decimal` list markers and heading weights **restated** because Quasar's reset strips them globally, inline `code` as a tinted chip, fences and tables with their **own `overflow-x` scroller** (a long line must not widen the card, which at a percentage-sized container would widen the feed box itself), thin-rule blockquotes, capped images — but the vertical rhythm is tightened and first/last margins zeroed, so a heading reads as a heading without spending a fifth of the square on its margins. **How MUCH tightened was walked back at the end of 2026-07-25**: the step was a single `0.45em`, which bought space at the cost of legibility — at that setting a heading sat almost on the paragraph above it and the blocks ran together into one undivided slab, so the reader had to parse structure the spacing should have been showing. The step is **`0.85em`** now, list items went `0.08em` → `0.3em` apart (at `0.08em` a list read as one wrapped paragraph with bullets in it), `hr` `0.5em` → `1em`, and headings carry a deliberately **ASYMMETRIC** margin — `1.15em` above, `0.35em` below (was an even-ish `0.55em`/`0.2em`). The asymmetry is the part that does the work: a heading binds to the text it introduces and separates from the text it follows, where an even margin leaves it floating between two blocks. **Prose is JUSTIFIED** in the same pass — `text-align: justify` on `p` and `li` ONLY, so the block's left and right edges both line up with the pit's border and the card reads as ordered; headings, tables and fences stay flush-left (justifying a two-word mono heading would stretch it across the whole measure). `hyphens: auto` rides along and is **not optional at this width** — justification works by opening word spaces until the line fills, so on a narrow measure without hyphenation one long word pushes a line into rivers of white. That in turn forced a **`hyphens: none` correction on `code, pre, kbd, samp`**: the property inherits, so the justified `p`/`li` handed it to every inline code span and `entity_type` broke as `entity_-` / `type` — a hyphen a reader cannot distinguish from one the token really contains. Prose may be hyphenated; anything meant to be typed back exactly may not. Verified across the live stream: all 1491 code/pre elements compute `hyphens: none`, and the pit still does not overflow horizontally (`word-break: break-word` breaks unbreakable tokens without adding a glyph). See [gotchas.md](../../specs/gotchas.md). Two rules had to GO for this: `-webkit-line-clamp: 4` (a box clamp counts LINE BOXES inside one `-webkit-box`; rendered markdown is a run of block elements it cannot measure — the square ceiling is the limit now) and `white-space: pre-wrap` (source newlines are the parser's job; preserving them would put a blank gap between every rendered block). **(C) The kind LEGEND is gone; classification is the LABEL PATH** (2026-07-25, third pass). The head strip's right-end tag (`doc` / `post` / `fork` / `comment`, `.post-square__tag` — rule deleted) and the icon's `is_doc` / parent-id branching both **asserted a category the card had derived**; every row here is a POST instance, and what kind of one it is is something the element already CARRIES. The icon is now uniform `article`. **The head strip was then dressed to match the pit** (end of 2026-07-25), so the card reads as two panels rather than a strip over a box: `.post-square__name` sits on its own **PLATE** — a `--grey-1` background rimmed `1px --indigo-3` at `--radius-sm`, i.e. literally the same floor and the same line as the markdown pit below it — with the title ink at `--indigo-8`; `.post-square__icon` is `--indigo-3` (it was `rgba(var(--ink-rgb), 0.55)`); and a **full-bleed vertical rule** `.post-square__head-rule` separates icon from title. That rule runs the strip's whole height, touching the **byline band's hairline above** (the card's top border until the author moved up there — see (F)) and the pit below: `align-self: stretch` alone stops at the strip's *padding* edge, so it also carries `margin: -7px 0` to cancel `.post-square__head`'s vertical padding — the same device as the nav bar's `.nav-divider-v`. **Keep that margin in step with the head's padding** or the line stops short of one edge (verified: rule height 37px = head height 37px, 0 overhang either end, on every card). Meeting the byline's horizontal hairline at a right angle is what ties the two strips together as one head rather than two stacked bands. Below the pit, a rigid `.post-square__labels` rail draws one chip per label the post holds, **root to leaf** (`.post-square__label-step`, steps joined by a drawn `›` inside a single `<router-link>` to the leaf label, so the chip stays one link and one tab stop; `@click.stop` keeps it off the card's select-toggle). A doc says it is a doc by carrying `PATHCHAIN › DOC` — nothing is invented. **This rail deliberately does NOT use `utils/labelChain.js#displayLabels`**: that filter drops the bare `PATHCHAIN > DOC` marker and the whole `PATHCHAIN > POST` family precisely BECAUSE a dedicated tag rendered them, so on the one surface that has no tag it would hide exactly the classification the rail exists to show. The only thing dropped is a chain that is a strict PREFIX of another present (`PATHCHAIN > POST` beside `PATHCHAIN > POST > ORIGINAL` — the longer path already draws every step of it); user classification sorts ahead of the `PATHCHAIN` plumbing families. Measured on the live feed: the doc card carries `CLAUDE › DOCUMENTATION LAYERS › CONCEPTS`, `PATHCHAIN › DOC`, `PATHCHAIN › INSTANTIATION`, `PATHCHAIN › POST › ORIGINAL`, an ordinary post the last two **(D) The author is a CONDENSED IDENTITY BLOCK** (2026-07-25; it left the foot for a band of its own the same day — see **(F)**). The foot's origin row used to run `<strong>username</strong>` beside an `EntityMicro` hash chip — three items in the same row as the post's chip and the three tallies. It is now ONE unit: `EntityAvatar` (34px face) on the left, and beside it the author's **name over their `@handle`**, the whole block a single `<router-link>` to `/entities/:id` (`@click.stop`, `.post-square__identity`). The entity hash chip went with the old run — the avatar routes to the same profile and the row already carries one address (the post's). The name is `display_name || username` at `0.78em/700` in the author purple, the handle `@username` at `0.66em` mono in dim ink, stacked at `line-height: 1.15` (any more and the two lines read as two facts rather than one block); each caps at `18ch` with an ellipsis. The block is **`flex: 0 0 auto`**, NOT shrinkable — left to the flex floor the identity collapses to `cla…` / `@cla…` on a 593px row with 300px to spare (see [gotchas.md](../../specs/gotchas.md)). It also carried **`max-width: 55%`** while it sat in the foot, because the post's adaptive `MicroChip` shared that row and a long display name would have taken the whole run; **that cap went with the move to the byline band** — the only thing beside the block there is the org badge, which the two `18ch` line caps already leave room for. The photo arrives **inline** on `item.author.photo` (the feed resolves it once per distinct author per page), so the stream costs no extra request — and so does the **ORG BADGE** (`item.author.org`, second pass the same day): when a post was published through an organization MASK, an `OrgLogoChip` follows the identity block, drawn OUTSIDE its `router-link` (a link inside a link is invalid, and the two point at different things — the person and the organization). The API now answers a mask with the PERSON's name, handle and face plus an `org` card, so the mask's flat `<person> @ <org>` display name becomes a mark you recognise instead of a string you read; `org.self` (an org publishing as itself) suppresses the chip, and `.post-square__byline .org-logo-chip { margin-left: -3px }` (`.post-square__foot …` before the move) pulls it in against the row's 8px gap so identity + badge read as one unit. **(E) The head band went FLAT** (end of 2026-07-25): `background: var(--indigo-1)` — the container's own coat, the same tone the feed box, its frieze bars' base and the post cards wear — and the heading ink is `--indigo-8` (`.feed-stream__heading`, replacing the platform-wide `text-accent` teal, which read as a foreign colour dropped onto the container; the sub-line stays the generic dim ink, since a caption sharing the heading's ink flattens the two into one block). The **glass is gone with it** — `rgba(var(--indigo-3-rgb), 0.82)` + `backdrop-filter: blur(5px)` (and its `-webkit-` twin) are deleted, and so is the now-unused `--indigo-3-rgb` token, whose only consumer this was. An opaque coat shows nothing through itself, so the blur was dead weight the compositor still paid for; the surface's ONE deliberate exception to the flat-plaque rule is retired and the rule is now universal here. The band stays `position: sticky; top: 0` — the pinning was never the glass's doing, and being opaque is what now lets it HIDE rather than soften the cards passing under it — and the `2px solid var(--indigo-3)` bottom edge is what states where it ends, doing more work now that band and cards share a tone. Verified: `backgroundColor rgb(236,239,241)`, heading `rgb(69,90,100)`, `backdropFilter: none`, band top − well top = `0` at rest. **(F) The author moved ABOVE the title — the BYLINE BAND** (end of 2026-07-25; **A SPLIT STAMP since 2026-08-09's last byline ask** — the day walked FOUR arrangements (ruled columns → two stacked dense lines → a one-line ≥1024px fold that lived hours → here, ONE layout at every width). The title plate and the open-post link are DELETED (the CAP names the card; the flyout's links are the viewer door); what stands is: `.post-square__face` (avatar 24px, its own link to the profile) beside `.post-square__byline-lines` (`flex: 0 1 auto` — not a grower) holding the `__identity` link (name + @handle INLINE, 0.72em/0.6em, both to `/entities/:id`; the NAME letters in **`--cyan-9`** and name+handle light **`--cyan-14`** together on hover — 2026-08-09 user ask, both levels minted for it: the byline is the card's one coloured voice, and one link lights as one object) + `OrgLogoChip` 14px (its own link to the org) + the heat plate, with `.post-square__byline-ago` tucked under; then the full-band SEAM RULE (`.post-square__byline-rule`, stretch + `-4px` margins, carrying the right-packing `auto` margin); then `.post-square__byline-when`, **the moment as TWO STACKED MICRO CHIPS** — the detailed datetime chip (`event` icon) over the city chip (`place` icon, only when shared), real `MicroChip`s routing to `/moments/:id` (the tiny world map's page; a moment-less legacy item degrades to the span face), compressed by `.post-square__moment-chip` (0.58em / 30ch / deep `min-width: 0`). **The whole band letters in Nasalization** (`--font-display` + 0.02em on the band; `mono` came off the handle and the plates so inheritance reaches them; the chips take the cap's named-chip deep-override). **2026-08-10 reworked the LEFT side in two asks.** (1) The HOP CHIP left the who-line and now LEADS the ago line — `[1 hop] · 3h ago`, a flex row with a `.post-square__ago-dot` separator drawn only when there is a chip to separate. It was the widest of the three qualifiers, a bordered plate standing between the name and the card's edge on the one line the name has to fit on; down here it costs the name nothing, and how FAR the author is pairs honestly with how LONG ago they published. Its `-3px` pull-in went with the move (nothing to its left to hug), and the 0.62em stayed OFF the row and on its two text children — the plate is dialled in `em` against the BAND, and a size on the row would have compounded it to 0.36em. (2) **The NAME NO LONGER TRIMS; the HANDLE does.** Both spans carried `max-width: 16ch` + default shrink, so a narrow card took them down together (`Dream Ope… @dreamo…`). Now the name is `flex-shrink: 0` with `max-width: 100%` (the line itself, so it ellipsizes only after the handle is gone) and the handle is `flex: 0 1 auto; min-width: 0` — `overflow: hidden` on `.post-square__identity` is what makes the order enforceable, since a non-shrinking name would otherwise push the badge off the card. On MOBILE (≤600px) three more rules finish it: name 0.66em / handle 0.55em / trust 0.54em, and `.post-square__byline-when` becomes `flex: 0 20 auto; min-width: 0` so the moment chips (rigid by argument on desktop — 2026-07-25's "the place string landed at a different length on every card") give first. Measured at 390px with a 28-char name: before, handle 0 and name clipped 114/160; after, handle ellipsized 81/109 and name whole at 160. Band measured 41px; the media budget constants follow, now 277 / expanded twin 311 (see the card-chrome note below). The paragraphs below describe the 2026-07-25 columns it grew out of.) The identity block of **(D)** opened the FOOT until this pass, which put the person *after* the whole post; a card reads **who → what → about → how it's doing**, so the block (avatar, name over handle, and the org badge that belongs to it) is now the card's FIRST strip, `.post-square__byline`, and the foot is left holding what the post has DONE. Card child order is `cap | frieze | byline | rgb | labels | rgb | pit | rgb | foot` since 2026-08-10 (see the divider note and the FOOT note below). The band is `flex: 0 0 auto` like the head and foot — who published a post is not something the square ceiling may trim, so only the pit gives way — with `padding: 6px 9px` and the row's `gap: 8px`. Its divider from the title strip is a **full edge-to-edge hairline**, `border-bottom: 1px solid var(--indigo-3)`, and it needs **no negative-margin trick** to reach both edges the way `.feed-stream__head` and `.post-square__head-rule` do: `.post-square` has no padding of its own, so the band already spans the card's whole content box and its border runs from one side border to the other. `--indigo-3` because that is the ink EVERY line on this surface is drawn in (the card's outer border, the pit's rim, the frieze lips, the head's vertical rule) — a divider inside the card is the same line as the ones around it, at hairline weight against the card's 2px foot and the head band's 2px edge. Verify edge-to-edge by geometry, not by eye: at a 1440 viewport the band measured `160.89 → 753.89` inside a card of `159.89 → 754.89` — inset by exactly the card's own 1px side border on each side, i.e. flush against both. **(G) The band was then DENSIFIED and given the moment** (2026-07-25, second byline pass). Three changes, one idea — the band is a single statement of provenance (*who*, then *when and where*), so it is drawn tight and ruled rather than spaced: **(i) the face shrank** `34px` → **`28px`** and the block's inner gap `7px` → `5px`, its two lines each dropping a step (`0.78em` → `0.74em` name, `0.66em` → `0.62em` handle) with their cap `18ch` → `16ch` — at 16ch the lines are near enough the same length to read as one stamp, and a name cut there was already being cut at 18. The band's own padding went `6px` → `5px` and its gap `8px` → `6px`; band height `47px` → **`41px`**, which the pit (the card's one flexible track) takes back. **(ii) A full-bleed VERTICAL hairline closes the author section** — `.post-square__byline-rule`, the same device and ink as `.post-square__head-rule` one strip below: `align-self: stretch` + `margin: -5px 0` to cancel the band's vertical padding, 1px `--indigo-3`. **Keep that margin in step with the band's padding.** It stops exactly 1px short of the band's bottom *by construction* — the band's border-box includes its own hairline, so the vertical rule meets the horizontal one instead of crossing it (measured: rule top = band top exactly, rule bottom = band bottom − 1px). **(iii) `.post-square__moment` — the TIME/SPACE CHIP** follows the rule, and **replaces the `.post-square__time` "1h ago"** that used to close the head strip (that rule is deleted; `absoluteTime` is no longer exposed to the template, `momentTitle` calls it). A post is minted at a moment and a moment is a *when* and a *where*, so it is one chip of two stacked lines, on the identity block's own `line-height: 1.15` rhythm — the band then reads as two parallel two-line facts either side of the rule. Top line is the relative time (`0.72em/700` — what you scan a feed by, carrying the weight the author's NAME carries opposite); under it, a `0.6em` mono line showing **`moment.place` when the post has one** ("Mexico City, Mexico") and **`moment.datetime` when it does not** ("Sat, 25 Jul 2026 · 11:26 PM"), with a `place` / `event` icon saying which you are looking at and the `title` carrying BOTH (a located post would otherwise have no way to tell you its date). Space wins because it is the more particular fact — but the date is the ordinary branch, since coordinates are opt-in. Both strings arrive RESOLVED from `GET /feed` (see api-reference: `moment.place` / `moment.datetime`), never formatted here — the date especially, which is built from `time_utc` in UTC server-side so every chip and viewer on the platform states one moment identically; only an item with no moment at all falls back to a local `toLocaleString`. The chip is `flex: 0 1 auto` + `min-width: 0` — **the one part of this band designed to give way** (the identity opposite must not; see (D)), so a long place ellipsizes rather than pushing the author out. Verified across the live feed: 30 cards, both branches present (`place` icon → "Mexico City, Mexico", `event` icon → the formatted date), every band `41px`, every avatar `28px`, no `.post-square__time` left. **(H) The band ABSORBED the head strip; the label rail took its place** (2026-07-25, third and fourth byline passes). The card's children are now **`byline | rail | pit | foot`** — `.post-square__head`, `.post-square__head-rule` and `.post-square__icon` are all DELETED. Everything that IDENTIFIES a post shares one ruled run, read left to right: **`[open] │ [author+badge] │ [when/where] │ [title]`**, the three `.post-square__byline-rule` hairlines dividing it. Two strips of metadata stacked over the body were saying the same kind of thing twice. `.post-square__go` LEADS the run because it is the one CONTROL among facts (at the head strip's right end it read as one more chip); `.post-square__name` CLOSES it, keeping its `--grey-1` plate (which is what still marks it as the card's subject rather than a fourth fact) at `0.74em` and **`flex: 1 1 auto; min-width: 0`** — it takes every pixel the three facts leave and ellipsizes into it, so the band is exactly as wide as the card whatever the title's length (12 of 30 ellipsize; `bandOverflow` = 0). The uniform `article` icon did not survive the move: in a band this dense it stated nothing the card does not. **THE MOMENT CHIP STOPPED SHRINKING** in the same pass — `flex: 0 1 auto` → **`flex: 0 0 auto`**, capped instead at `max-width: 32ch` on its detail line. With two shrinkable items sharing one row, flex split the overflow between them and the place string landed at a different length on every card ("Mexico City," / "Mexico City, Mexico" / "Mexico City, Mexi" down the column); exactly one item per row should absorb, and the title is the one that loses nothing by being cut. Size that `ch` cap against the WIDER branch — the 27-char date, not the 19-char place — plus the icon and gap, which sit inside the capped box: at `24ch` the date clipped on 19 of 30 cards while `innerText` still read whole, so assert on `scrollWidth > clientWidth`, never on text. See [gotchas.md](../../specs/gotchas.md) for both. **The LABEL RAIL then filled the strip the title vacated** — `.post-square__labels` (a bare wrapping run BELOW the pit) became **`.post-square__rail`**, directly under the byline: a post's classification belongs with what identifies it, not appended after the reading. It is a **rounded rectangle that scrolls HORIZONTALLY**, and the two properties are one decision: the BOX is the card's third panel on the same recipe as the pit and the title plate (`--grey-1` floor, `1px --indigo-3` rim, `7px` radius — **the floor moved to `--indigo-2` one pass later, see (I)**), so classification, subject and body are three cuts of one material; and `flex-wrap: nowrap` + `overflow-x: auto` (chips `flex: 0 0 auto`, their old `max-width: 100%` dropped so they slide past the edge instead of compressing to stubs) is what keeps it ONE line — wrapping made the rail's height a function of how many labels a post carries, four paths becoming three rows and pushing the body down on a height-capped card. Same thin scrollbar language as the pit and the well, `height: 4px` since it is horizontal. Rigid (`flex: 0 0 auto`) like the foot: the ceiling trims the body, never a card's classification. Spacing is stated ONCE per panel — rail `margin: 6px 7px 0`, pit `margin: 6px 7px 7px` (it gained the top margin the head strip's padding used to supply) — so the pit sits correctly under the band on a post with no labels, where the rail is `v-if`'d away entirely. **(I) Final band order, a two-line title, and the panels' lines** (2026-07-25, fifth pass). **The open button moved to the RIGHT END** — the band is `[author] │ [when/where] │ [title] │ [open]`: the three FACTS in the order a post is read, then the one CONTROL, standing apart instead of leading. **The title plate became a fixed TWO-LINE BOX**, denser (`0.74em` → `0.7em`, tracking `0.03` → `0.02em`, padding `2px 7px` → `2px 6px`) and built from three rules that only work together: the plate is a flex box centring its content on BOTH axes; an inner `.post-square__name-text` span does the clamping (`display: -webkit-box` + `-webkit-line-clamp: 2` + `overflow-wrap: anywhere`, the guard for a title that is one unbroken token; a titleless post reads `(untitled)` since 2026-07-27 — it no longer borrows its CONTENT node's source path); and `min-height: calc(2.6em + 6px)` (2 × line-height + padding + border — **keep all three in step**) sizes the box to two lines ALWAYS. Each does one job: a title too long for one line now WRAPS rather than being cut at the first line's end (two lines of mono uppercase is most of a sentence, one is a fragment), a short title sits CENTRED in the middle of the identical box, and because the box never changes size the band stays uniform down the column — the same card-to-card raggedness fixed for the moment chip in (H), and more visible here since the plate is a drawn box. `-webkit-line-clamp` is legitimate HERE and wrong in the pit (see gotchas.md): it counts line boxes inside one `-webkit-box`, which is exactly what a single text node is, where the pit holds a run of block elements it cannot measure. Verified: all 30 plates `31px`, 19 titles at one line / 11 at two / **0 over two**, `textAlign: center` + `justifyContent: center` + `alignItems: center`, band `41px` → `42px` and uniform. **The panels then took their own lines and tones**: the rail's floor went `--grey-1` → **`--indigo-2`** (a tray you SCAN takes the bed tone; the pit's near-white is a surface you READ, and it keeps `--grey-1`), and **the rail gained a HEAVIER TOP edge** — `border-top-width: 2px` on an otherwise 1px box, a shallow tray with a lip. The pit took the same weight in that pass and **gave it back the next**, returning to an EVEN 1px frame: it is the READING area, and a weighted edge above the text reads as a rule the prose hangs from rather than as the top of a box. The heavy-top device is the rail's alone; the pit's own uneven-border relative is the card's 2px foot, which is a different idea (seating the whole card on the bed). Finally the rail moved inside a full-width **`.post-square__rail-strip`** (`padding: 6px 7px`) carrying a `border-bottom: 1px --indigo-3` — the **edge-to-edge hairline dividing classification from content**. It must live on the strip: the rail is inset by that padding, so a border on the rail would stop 7px short of both sides. That settles the card's grammar — **DIVIDED by full-bleed lines (the byline's, the strip's), PANELLED by inset boxes (rail, pit, title plate)**, two devices that are never mixed. The pit keeps `margin: 6px 7px 7px`, so it sits correctly under whichever line precedes it — the strip's, or the byline's on a post with no labels, where the strip is `v-if`'d away entirely. The **chips inside the rail** took the other half of that step: `background: var(--indigo-1)` (they were transparent, so they wore the tray's tone and the rail read as one field with outlines drawn on it) and a heavier 2px BOTTOM — the chip-scale reading of the card's own uneven border, and deliberately the opposite edge to the rail's and pit's heavier TOP: a panel is seated by the edge where it meets what came before, a chip RESTS on the tray beneath it. Rail height `27px` → `29px` with the thicker chip foot, still uniform. Verified: chip background `rgb(236,239,241)` at "1px top / 2px bottom" across all 82 chips, rail background `rgb(207,216,220)`, rail "2px top / 1px rest" and pit an even "1px top / 1px rest", strip hairline `1px rgb(176,190,197)` spanning `161–754` inside a card of `160–755` (inset by exactly the card's own side borders). Verified after (H): card order `byline | rail-strip | pit | foot`, every rail `27px` tall with 11 of 30 actually scrolling (up to 86px of overflow), 0 stale `.post-square__labels`/`.post-square__head` elements, and the container still measuring exactly `50%` of the track. **Block embeds in a card** (2026-07-26): a `![[pathos:…]]` reference renders the full Mini panel whatever the surface's inline chip tier is, so the compressed tier restates `.element-mini { max-width: 100% }` — the Mini's own 480px cap is a prose MEASURE, and a figure is not prose — `ElementMini` drops it itself now for any media node (`.element-mini--media`), on every surface (an embedded video otherwise sits in a 480px box with dead card beside it).
