This is how a video LINK node shows as a PLAYER in the feed: post body → `MarkdownBody` → `ElementMini` → `NodeMini` → `EmbedFrame`, with the frame **bounded by the card's media budget** — `.post-square__pit` publishes `--media-max-h` (`max(120px, calc(min(var(--post-square-max, 100cqw), 60vh) - 264px))`, derived from the card's own ceiling; it was a flat `30vh` here, `20vh` under the 40vh card, until 2026-07-26) and `EmbedFrame` turns it into the width cap via `--embed-ratio` so the ratio survives exactly — measured 1.778 at 1440×900, 1600×1080 and 1280×720). The cap is about REACH, not looks: at the pit's full width a 16:9 frame is taller than the pit itself (579px wide wants 326px in a 229px window), so the card opened on the player's top third and its PLAY BUTTON, dead centre, fell below the fold — the one control an embed exists for could not be clicked without scrolling first. A video cannot be made to fit a card whose whole height is capped, so the frame gives way instead; the pit still scrolls for the rest. It only works because `GET /feed?body=full` answers with RAW markdown (corrected the same day — it used to hand over the excerpt's stripped string, in which no chip and no block embed survives). **THE CARD'S TWO INNER HAIRLINES BECAME TWO DIFFERENT DIVIDERS on 2026-08-07** (a run of user asks). It began as a PAIR of `FriezeBar slim` bands, the second `vflip`ped so the two reflected about the label lane; hours later the mirrored half was replaced by an **`RgbHairline`** (see its row above), so the card now reads byline │ **frieze band** │ rail strip │ **rgb hairline** │ pit │ foot, and the hairline is drawn only WITH the rail (a labelless card has the band alone and the pit follows it). The swap is the more interesting statement: the two full-bleed dividers stopped being the same device twice — the band is the platform's MOTIF, saying a new kind of thing starts here, and the flat rule closest to the reading area can be a third of its height. (`FriezeBar`'s `vflip` prop and the `-mirror-vmirror` masks were minted for that mirrored half and have **no consumer** now; both are kept — they are the fourth corner of a four-way asset square that was already three-quarters built.) Both `border-bottom`s went at that point — the band IS that line, given height and a motif — ⚠ **and the BYLINE's came back on 2026-08-10's last ask** (`border-bottom: 1px solid var(--grey-6)`, "a classic grey-6 hairline between the author/moment section and the label section"): the frieze had long since moved UP to the cap, and the three devices that held this seam after it (an `RgbHairline`, then half of a label bracket, then nothing at all for one pass) ended with it bare — so the plain rule is back where it started, at the card's inner 1px against its 1.5px edges. It goes on the BAND and not on the rail strip below it, because the strip is inset `7px` now and a divider has to run lip to lip; the strip's own `border-bottom` stays gone. Meanwhile the frieze's plaque walked DOWN THE GREYS one user ask at a time — `--grey-6` (the card's line ink, which is what let the hairline it replaced simply go: the band was that line with height and a carve), then `--grey-7`, then **`--grey-8`**. Every step buys the same thing, and it is the carve rather than the tone: the waves are two very light accents and a groove only reads when the plate is well under what is cut into it (1.7:1 on -6, 3.5:1 on -8). What it costs is the "the band IS the card's line, thickened" reading — two steps under that ink the band is its own object, a dark plate laid across the card, which is the relation the feed container already has with its `--indigo-8` edges one family over. The MOTIF is the one thing here not made of the card's material — an `--indigo-11` → `--teal-11` gradient down the wave through the new `--frieze-bar-wave-two-paint` dial (born `--teal-11` → `--indigo-11`, and REVERSED on the mirrored band so indigo faced the lane from both sides and mint faced out; **the ramp itself was flipped to indigo-down-to-cyan on 2026-08-10**, a user ask, once that twin was long gone). The flip runs the band AGAINST the three rgb hairlines below it, which are cyan-down — deliberately: they are different devices (a motif with height vs three flat lines), and opposing them makes the card read as a cyan low point at the reading area closed by cold indigo top and bottom. The flat `--frieze-bar-wave-two` fallback follows the ramp's TOP end and moved with it. The band stands just above `slim` (`--frieze-bar-h: calc(var(--frieze-h) * 0.55 + 2px)`, ~10.4px of plate at a 900px viewport) after **four settings in one day** — `slim` (0.5) → `0.4` ("slightly thinner") → `0.65` ("too thin, I cannot see the friezes well") → here ("thinner, but not so thin the friezes get deformed"). What the walk settled is that the number to think in is ONE MOTIF ROW of the masks' 13-row grid: ~0.54px at `0.4`, ~0.67px at slim, ~0.87px at `0.65`, ~0.80px here — **under ~0.7px a meander stops being a pattern and becomes a texture, which is what "deformed" means in numbers**. `slim` stays ON at that height (it is what drops layer one — two interlocking waves in 10px go straight back to texture). It is `flex: 0 0 auto` — the bar states a height, but in this flex column anything that MAY shrink will, the pit being the only part meant to give. **IT WEARS TWO RULES since 2026-08-10** (user ask): `border-top`/`border-bottom`, `1px solid var(--grey-6)` — the CARD'S line ink, not the plate's, at the card's lightest weight (its outer edges run 1.5px). That is the point of them: two greys down, the band had become its own dark object laid across the card, and an object wants an edge in the same ink as every other line on the square, which BINDS the plate to the card instead of leaving it floating. ⚠ **The height dial pays for them** — everything here is `border-box`, so 2px of border eats the plate and drops a motif row to ~0.65px, under the ~0.7px floor above; the `+ 2px` in `--frieze-bar-h` gives the rules their own space and leaves the motif exactly where the four asks put it (verified: outer box 12.39px, content 10.39px = the old band). Keep that term through any future move of the `0.55`. The pit's `--media-max-h` budget subtracts the band as `0.55 × --frieze-h` **plus 2px folded into its px constant (280 → 282)** and counts the hairline in that constant at a flat 6px — once, since 2026-08-10's last rail ask deleted the label bracket's thin pair; move either and move that with it. The constant stands at **276** — the rail strip's padding and the band's 1px rim against the pair's −8, plus the label strip's TWO classic hairlines (+1 each) and the padding tightening 4px → 2px (−4) that came with them — and it is a coincidence worth naming that 2026-08-22 left it there: the frieze leaving cost −2 (its rules) and −`0.55 × --frieze-h` (its plate, the whole variable term), the cap's new closing rule gave back +2. **Its expanded twin is `302`** (`276 + 26` of the well's own padding, on `.post-square.is-expanded .post-square__pit`): ONE number written twice, and the copy that lags is always the expanded one — see [specs/gotchas.md](../../specs/gotchas.md) for the grep that catches it. The card's OUTER BORDER went 1px → 2px → **1.5px** across two asks the same day (even on all four edges, same `--grey-6` ink, inner lines left at 1px). The half step is a real position, not a compromise — the outline has to outweigh the card's 1px inner lines to read as its edge, and at 2px it was the heaviest line on the card by double once the dividers inside it thinned. Measured: exact at 2× (3 device pixels), floored to 1px at 1× (the headless driver reports `1px`), so it degrades to the full step down rather than to something wrong. **THE CAP — the card's own header strip (2026-08-07, user ask).** A thin band ABOVE the byline, now the card's first child, answering what the byline never did: what this post IS and what it CAME OUT OF. Split by the byline's own vertical-rule device (`.post-square__cap-rule`). ⭐ **IT CLOSES ITSELF SINCE 2026-08-22** — a 2px `--grey-6` `border-bottom`, where for twelve days its edge was the element after it; see the card-reads line above. It was **70/30** — both cells `flex: 1 1 <pct>` + `min-width: 0`, measured 391/168 in a 559px card — and is now **FACTS / CONTROLS** (2026-08-07, user ask): the control lane is `flex: 0 0 auto` (it measures its own buttons and nothing else) and the fact cell `flex: 1 1 auto` + `min-width: 0` (it takes the remainder and is the one that gives). 30% of a feed card is far more than three 20px glyphs need, and the lane held its share whatever was in it — measured 473/82 in the same 556px cap, which is ~85 px of title handed back per card. The rule now stands where the pressing starts rather than at a fixed fraction, which is the honest place for a line dividing what you READ from what you PRESS. **RULED AT EVERY SEAM since 2026-08-09** (user ask): an EXPAND LEAD cell (`.post-square__cap-lead`, one `expand` button at the card's far LEFT edge) opens the strip behind its own cap-rule, and the same rule runs between the lane's three controls (stretch + `-2px` margin against the lane's 2px padding) — `control │ facts │ pin │ skeleton │ flyout`. The lead is the **hash lens**: press → the feed filters to this post's own chain address (`?hash=`, the same clause Talavero issues for a spoken address), the single card draws FULL-HEIGHT (`.is-expanded` — board home slot to container floor, `--feed-well-h` minus the well's own paddings, ResizeObserver-published; the fsck `--static` `expanded-card` witness holds the subtraction to the well's padding line), the head box auto-slides to its top berth, and the lane wears a `post <hash>…` chip; a second press (or the chip) releases. `is-on` keys off the ACTIVE hash — local or Talavero-spoken — prefix-matched against the card's address. The WIDE side reads `[icons] Comment on «chip» :: <name>`: the ICONS state the kind off the feed's new `origin.relation` — `sym_o_post` for an original, `sym_o_comment` for a comment, and for a fork **both** `sym_o_post` and `sym_o_alt_route`, a fork being a post that came out of a post and wearing the two marks together; the ORIGIN CLAUSES are one per pointer (comment before fork, so a post that is somehow both names both parents), each carrying its parent as a **`MicroChip`** rather than a `#41` — the parent is an element with an address, the chip is how this platform states one, and a comment's parent is as often a NODE as a post, which is why the generic chip is used and not `PostMicro`; the NAME closes the run — `item.title` or **`post #<id>`**, a fallback deliberately different from `cardName`'s (the title plate falls back THROUGH the parent because it has no room to state a relation; the cap has already stated it, so what is missing there is only the name). The other side is the CONTROL lane — three glyphs: a **pin** (`pinService`, target type `skeleton`: the same PINS skeleton the pins widget reads, with `pins-changed` bubbling FeedStream → FeedPage → MainLayout's `pinsRefreshKey` through a listener now bound on the ROUTER-VIEW); an **`sym_o_orthopedics`** link into `/skeletons/:id`, the post read as its skeleton; and **`open_in_new`** (2026-08-07, user ask), that SAME destination without the navigation — it points the right-hand flyout at this post's skeleton, so the slots open BESIDE the feed and the reader keeps their place in the column. It is a button, not a link, for the reason the title plate is one: it opens a panel on this page rather than going anywhere, and it TOGGLES (a second press closes it). FeedPage owns that state — `@open-skeleton` sets `flyoutRef` to the bare id, which is one of the two forms `SkeletonFlyout` already accepted from `?flyout=`, so the flyout learned nothing new — and hands it back as the `flyoutId` prop purely so the button can wear `is-on`. Opening it clears `selected`: the box shows one thing, so the skeleton face and the post face close each other. Pinned state is ONE `pinService.list()` per stream load into a `pinnedIds` Set — never a `pins/check` per card, which would be thirty requests to draw thirty tacks — and the tack flips optimistically with a reload-the-truth catch. All three controls are chromeless until hovered (this strip is read far more often than pressed) and the pin adds `is-on` in `--accent`. The whole band is set in **`--font-display` (Nasalization)**, the first thing on this card to wear it, at `0.62em` / `0.02em` tracking — the `.nasalization` utility's own `0.05em` is meant for headings with room — over `--grey-9`, the card's deepest ink, because this strip is the post's NAME. ⚠ **THE NAME ITSELF STEPS UP ON DESKTOP** (2026-08-10, user ask): `.post-square__cap-title` takes `font-size: 1.16em` of the band (≈`0.72em` of the card) inside `@media (min-width: 1024px)` — **FeedPage's own desktop gate**, the same number the byline reads, so the card never invents a second breakpoint. The rest of the strip stays at `0.62em`: the lift is for the one cell a reader is looking for, not for the chrome around it. Below the gate the name sits flush with the lane, where the strip is too tight for it to buy anything but an earlier ellipsis (the cap's other two cells are rigid, so every point comes out of the title's own `flex: 1 1 auto` slack). It changes TYPE, not the band — the `CAP` term in the media budget is unchanged at both widths (verified; it reads **26** since the cap took its own closing rule on 2026-08-22, 24 before that) — and ⚠ the media query is written **below** the base rule, since a query adds no specificity and would otherwise lose to source order (`specs/gotchas.md`). **THE ORIGIN CHIP NAMES ITS TARGET** (2026-08-07, second ask). It printed a hash — `mono`, capped `max-width: 13ch` because `MicroChip` is container-adaptive by design and given the room took 64 characters, leaving the post's own name as `post #…` — which made the one line that says WHICH post is being answered the one line a reader had to parse an address to follow, while the card's own name sat one span to its right in plain words. A chip whose target is a POST now carries `:display="originName(target)"` — the feed's new `origin.*.title` under exactly `capTitle`'s rule, `title || post #<id>`, so both ends of the clause are named the same way — and `:icon="'sym_o_post'"`, the glyph the cap states a post with two spans to the left (`MicroChip` defaults to `KINDS.posts.icon` = `edit_note`, the post-as-document drawing, which is a different mark). It gains `.is-named`, which lifts the character cap (`max-width: 100%`) and letters it in `--font-display` down through a `:deep(.micro-chip__hash)` — two classes + the scope attribute against the component's own single class, so no `!important` is needed — with `min-width: 0` so a long title ellipsizes instead of holding MicroChip's 6-character hash floor. The ceiling moved UP a level instead: `.post-square__cap-origin` is `max-width: 50%`, so the parent may take half the strip and the card's own name keeps the rest whatever the card's width. A chip pointing at anything that is NOT a post gets no name and keeps the hash face it always had — a NODE has no title, and a CLAIM parent is named by its STATEMENT, which the feed does not read. **The `RgbHairline` moved up with it** (third ask the same day) — it closed the CAP, dividing the header from everything under it, unconditional where it used to be `v-if`'d on the rail — went **DOWN on 2026-08-09** to stand between the PIT and the FOOT (a plain cap `border-bottom` holding the seam for hours) — **and became a PAIR on that day's last ask**: one band back under the cap with its filling FLIPPED (`--cap` modifier, `--rgb-hairline-fill` restated reversed — indigo up, cyan down), one over the foot, the two cyan ends facing the content so the pair reads as a BRACKET rather than a repeat (children: `cap | rgb-hairline--cap | byline | frieze | rail-strip | pit | rgb-hairline | foot`); the rail is closed by nothing, its strip padding and the pit's 6px top margin being the lane. The pit's media budget absorbed the cap: the px constant went 271 → **295** (the cap measures 24 and is a one-line strip by construction) and the hairline's 6px inside it is no longer conditional. **THE CARD CASTS AGAIN** (2026-08-07, user ask, "very very subtle") — flat since 2026-07-25, now a two-layer CONTACT shadow: `0 1px 2px / .045` (the dark line where sheet meets plate) over `0 2px 5px -2px / .05` (ambient). The negative spread is what keeps it a contact shadow rather than a float — reach is `blur/2 − spread + y` = 2.5px down and 0.5px sideways, so it dies inside the 10px card gap and the 8px bed reveal, which is exactly what the July shadow failed to do (the constraint was never "no shadow", it was "nothing that reaches the neighbour"). What makes it right now is the ask before it: the bed went back to `--grey-4`, so the card is the LIGHTER object again, and a pale sheet on a grey plate is the thing that HAS a contact shadow. Measured: darkest rendered pixel 212 on a 224 bed, clean bed by 3px. ⚠ **The alphas are chosen against the FIELD** — on a near-white bed they vanish, and the honest answer at `--grey-2` was no shadow at all. Same ask: rounder corners and the outline **`1.5px` → `1px`** — one reasoning for both, that the card stopped being a flat plaque held by line weight and became a sheet stated by cast and silhouette. The radius went **`4px` → `6px` → `8px`** across two asks. **`7px` is the one value to SKIP**, which is why the second step was 6 → 8: the pit, title plate and label rail all turn at exactly `--radius-sm` (7px), and a container turning at the same radius as the boxes inside it reads as a tray moulded around them rather than a sheet they are laid on — at 6 the outer corner was a pixel tighter than its contents, at 8 a pixel looser, and looser is the better reading. The **veil (`::before`) follows at `border-radius: 7px`** — card radius minus card border, the padding box it is pinned to. It had none before and got away with it because `overflow: hidden` clipped its square corners for free; a clip cannot bend the veil's own 2px light border, which thickened into the bend rather than following it. Invisible at 4px, not past it — keep the three numbers in step. **The VEIL walked four settings** the same day, and the walk matters because SWAPPING ITS TONE CHANGED WHAT THE ALPHA DIAL DOES. Measured card each time: `--grey-3` @70% rgb(242,239,234) luma 240 R−B 8 → @60% rgb(243,239,232) luma 239 R−B 11 ("like 15% less strong") → `--grey-2` @80% rgb(245,244,240) luma 244 R−B 5 → **@65% rgb(246,243,236) luma 243 R−B 10**. Read the last two rows: fifteen points of alpha moved luma by ONE level and DOUBLED the warmth — arithmetic rather than coincidence, since `--grey-2` (luma 245) is `--light-cream`'s own brightness (243) within two levels, so mixing them can only change HUE, where `--grey-3` (238) sat five levels below the cream and moved both at once. **Three dials, none a substitute for another: the TONE decides what the ALPHA is a dial FOR** (at this pairing it is very nearly pure WARMTH — go back to `--grey-3` or lower if the card should get DARKER rather than cooler)**, and the FIELD sets how far the card stands off its bed** (~19 levels here). ⚠ When re-measuring: `backdrop-filter: blur(2px)` pulls bed pixels into the composite at the card's rim (sample the middle), and a sample just ABOVE a card now lands inside the contact shadow, ~7 levels dark. | |
| `shared/ElementFlyout.vue` | ⭐ **The element flyout — the platform's ONE floating viewer window** (2026-07-26 as `posts/FeedPostFlyout.vue`; GENERIC + `skeletons/SkeletonFlyout.vue` 2026-07-31, the deferred G8 item; element viewer with the view switch, then **FUSED with `media/MediaViewerWindow.vue` — 2026-08-17, user asks**: the media window's shell and behaviors taken WHOLE per the priority list — sizing, fit-engine spawn placement, bar-drag movement, the action foot, one-window-per-element individuality, and the media aesthetic: `--grey-4` metal coat, `--grey-6` rim, 10px corners, the teal `is-grabbed`/`is-resizing` ring+bloom — see the `media/MediaViewerWindow.vue` row for the shell's full record). Props: `viewer-id` alone; everything else is the store record's TARGET (`stores/flyoutViewers.js`). The bar carries the **VIEW SWITCH** (`.element-flyout__swap`, absolute right where the media bar kept nothing — its actions live in the foot; `@pointerdown.stop` so a press never starts a drag; glyph = the face it OFFERS — `schema` while the element is up, the element kind's glyph while the skeleton is; `is-on` while the skeleton face is out; rendered only when the target HAS an element face — a bare schema is its skeleton) between the **ELEMENT view** and the **SKELETON view** (`SkeletonTable`, Field \| Type \| Data, in the `.element-flyout__generic` scroll wrapper). Per target: **node** (NodeMini's corner trigger, or a resolved nodes/ ref) → element view = `MediaViewerBody`, the per-kind MEDIA faces (embed player AND page article, image, video, audio, markdown doc, text, download card); skeleton view = the NODE'S OWN walk, pre-walked from `GET /refs/surround` LAZILY on first switch. **post** (both card triggers) → element view = **the postcard itself** via `FeedStream`'s `embed-item` mode, keyed per post (same markup, same scoped styles, none of the feed's furniture; its `select` closes THIS window — the same toggle that opened it — and `pins-changed` rides up); skeleton view = SkeletonTable on `item.skeleton_id`. **ref** (the `?flyout=` door) → skeleton view self-resolving; a ref resolving to a POST instance (`name==='POST' && !is_schema`) fetches its feed item through the hash lens (`GET /feed?hash=&body=full&limit=1`) and RETARGETS the window into a post; a `nodes/<hash>` ref fetches `GET /nodes/by-path` and retargets into a node — same window, same rect (refit once), new self. **Default view follows the KIND, not the door: posts and nodes open on the ELEMENT**, everything else on the skeleton; `userToggled` outranks the async upgrades. The header GLYPH follows what is SHOWING; the NAME is the window's stable identity (`titleOf` for nodes — `PROVIDER :: name` — the post's title for posts) and is mirrored with the element glyph into the store (`describe`) so a parked tab never renames. The FOOT answers per kind: the element's page / share / copy-path / pin (`node` / `skeleton` pin types), and the thumbs really vote (`nodeInteractionService` for nodes, `postService.vote/unvote` for posts; a bare-ref window offers the address copy alone). ⚠ async fns are defined BEFORE the immediate reset watch — it runs during setup, and a `const` after it is in TDZ on a fresh mount ("loadNode is not a function" killed the whole box on direct `?flyout=nodes/…` navigation in the single-box era; [gotchas](../../specs/gotchas.md)). CSS class walk: `.post-flyout` → `.skeleton-flyout` → `.element-flyout`; the embedded CARD is deliberately NOT re-dialled — "as it is on the feed" means its own indigo/cream self on the metal box. **The single-box era (2026-07-26 → 08-17) is RETIRED**: the `--grey-3`/brown `.flyout-window` dress (survives in `css/_components.scss` as the DASHBOARD DOCK's shell — that block, this row's git history and the plan docs keep the four-pass tone argument), the quoted-NodeMini `--node-mini-*` dial record, `FeedPostPanel`'s tenancy (survives on FeedPageLegacy), FeedPage's `.feed-flyout` slot and `stores/flyouts.js` all died with the fusion. |
| `dashboard/DashboardDock.vue` | ⭐ **The dashboard panel — the 6th window RISING FROM THE NAV BAR, and since phase 4 THE DASHBOARD SURFACE** (2026-08-10; born empty the same day, two user asks in one sitting). It was born a FLYOUT: "pop a new component we'll be working on that should follow the very same aesthetic as the current flyout skeleton viewer… and contain nothing", built as a second floating box in the feed's right-hand slot. The second ask moved it — "make it rise from the bottom, like the other creative expanded versions to create stuff that are attached to the footer nav bar" — so its two halves now come from two places, which is exactly what the dock chrome's dials are for. **FOOTPRINT + BEHAVIOUR = `.dock-window--creation`**: right half of the screen, `--dock-gap` of daylight off the crown strip and the side column, bottom edge WELDED to `var(--nav-footer-h)` (the nav bar's top edge), both top corners rounded, full width under 600px, z from `windows.zOf('dashboard')` so it interleaves with the other five — with `is-max` the ONE piece it has taken back since (2026-08-12: this dock maximizes to the chrome's rectangle, not to the screen's left edge; see MAXIMIZE below). **COAT = the FLYOUT FAMILY's**, through `.dock-window--dashboard`'s six `--dock-*` dials (`--grey-3` plaque, `--grey-4` rule + well, `--grey-5` strong, brown-8/brown-4 ink) — the chat colorway's seam, no repainting modifier — plus the family's two named pieces inside it: a `slim` FriezeBar on `--grey-9` and the sunk, bevelled `.flyout-window__well`. So it reads as the skeleton viewer's sibling and behaves as the maker's. ⚠ **THE COAT LEFT THIS COLORWAY on 2026-08-26** (user ask: the dashboards, chat, post, skeletons, labels and uploads windows should all be light-cream "with the same veil/effect as the footer navigation bar so the windows match the exact same bar color") — `.dock-window--dashboard` no longer declares `--dock-coat` and takes the bar's `--plaque-coat` from the shared footprint, keeping the flyout family's `--grey-4` lines, `--grey-4` well and `--grey-5` strong edge. Which INVERTS the note below: coat and rule sat one step apart (1.06:1), the flyout's own quiet edge rather than the brown chrome's contrast — right for a window drawn to be looked INTO rather than worked in, where the step falls on the well instead. With a cream sheet under those same grey lines the step is now warm/cool rather than a value one, so the board's edges read by HUE and the `--grey-4` well is carrying more of the depth than it was; the family's two warm marks (brown-8 head ink, brown-4 meta) turned out to be the right family for this coat four settings before it arrived, which is why no ink dial moved. **THREE traffic lights** (red close / yellow minimize / green maximize — the docks' own order, which this window is entitled to *because* it has somewhere to park). **It parks as a minitab on the footer strip** (user ask: "for the dashboard, make it a tab on the bottom nav footer bar") — one entry in NavigationBar's `parkedTabs`, `sym_o_empty_dashboard` glyph tinted `#424242` to match its button and its coat. **THE WELL FILLED ON PHASE 4** (dashboards plan, same day): under the frieze band a `.dock-tabs` STRIP — one tab per open board (label `name \|\| '(untitled)'`, ✕ per tab; the mobile dock-tab skin — well-toned tabs in rule rims — promoted to every screen here, the dials being grey already), the GHOST `.dock-tab--new` wearing **`sym_o_dashboard_customize`** (Material SYMBOLS — an unmatched ligature renders as the literal word) which MINTS an empty dashboard (`POST /api/dashboards`) and opens it in edit mode, and the PENCIL at the strip's right end flipping `isEditing`. The well mounts `DashboardGrid` for the active tab and is the ONE scroller (cells publish per-cell ceilings). Boot read = `GET /api/dashboards?ensure=1` — the default USER_HOME exists by the time the list answers; a tabless window fronts the USER_HOME board (newest otherwise). **THE BOARDS PICKER** (2026-08-11, the missing list — found the day Ramona's campaign existed server-side while a phone's strip only knew its old tabs): an `apps` button beside the ghost tab opens an IN-FLOW band under the strip listing what the acting entity OWNS (`listMine`, recent-first — name \| template \| #id); tap opens/fronts the board's tab and closes the band. Tabs are the DEVICE's memory, the picker is the SERVER's. ⚠ NEVER a floating dropdown inside `.dock-tabs` — the strip is an `overflow-x: auto` scroll container and CLIPS absolutely-positioned children on both axes (`overflow-y: visible` computes to `auto` beside a non-visible x — the first cut rendered nothing on desktop AND phone, and the text-only flow assertion missed it; assert `boundingBox()`, not `innerText`). **EDIT MODE EXPANDS the window** via `.is-edit` on `.dock-window--dashboard` (css/_components.scss): `left: 0; right: 0; top: var(--media-tabs-h, 0px); bottom: var(--nav-footer-h)` — **SCREEN EDGE TO SCREEN EDGE since 2026-08-12** (user ask: the extended board should "occupy the whole screen, except for the footer nav bar and the thin header", and display BEHIND the left drawer and the right side bars), corners squared where the box touches chrome. `right` was `var(--dock-right)` — the 46px rail reserve, the one dimension still holding daylight — and dropping it to `0` is the whole change: the top already sat on the crown strip's underside (`--frieze-h + --media-tabs-h`, the tabs band being permanent chrome ABOVE the strip) and the bottom was already welded to `--nav-footer-h`. **z UNCHANGED** (dock band 3010+, under minitabs 3045 / rails 3100 / bar 3110 — never raise a surface over the bar), which is precisely what makes the full-bleed safe: the board PASSES UNDER the drawer (3120), the stack widget (3100) and the pins widget (3120) rather than covering them, so the rails are still drawn over it and the reserve had nothing left to protect. Measured at 1440×900: board `0,24 → 1440,849` (crown bottom 24, footer top 849), with hit-tests at the left rail and both right rails answering drawer/stack/pins and the board owning everything between. **A SECOND PASS THE SAME DAY made the extended board a PAGE rather than a pane** (user ask: "remove its padding from the top and add a little padding on the left so it starts drawing horizontally the same way as the public feed container"; "remove its rounded corners") — rules in `DashboardDock.vue`'s scoped block, where the well's interior contract already lived. `padding-left` on the DOCK ITSELF puts the whole board on `.feed-container`'s own left line, computed the way the feed computes it: `var(--dock-rail-w) + 2.5%` of the track from `min-width: 1024px` (where Quasar's `show-if-above` has the drawer standing and `q-page-container` pads left by it), the bare `2.5%` of the track from `min-width: 601px` (drawer is a closed overlay, the page pads nothing, so the feed's edge is just its own margin), and nothing at all under 600px (the phone's `.is-edit` restatement already carries the mobile dock's gap daylight and there is no rail reserve down there). **Padding, not margin** — the full-bleed ask above is intact: the board's SURFACE still runs to both screen edges and passes under the drawer and rails, only its CONTENT clears them, and what shows in the inset strip is the dock's own coat (exactly what the feed shows to the left of its column: the page beneath). ⚠ **It rides the window, not the well**, because the strip above the well was being eaten too: the tab row, the Cuentista bar, and TWO OF THE THREE TRAFFIC LIGHTS — `.traffic` is `position: absolute; left: 8px` inside `.dock-bar`, so at full-bleed close (x 8…21) and minimize (25…38) sat under the 42px drawer rail, the same class of bug as the feed column burying the nav bar's burger the same day ([gotchas](../../specs/gotchas.md)). ⚠ **THE OTHER END HAD IT TOO, and worse** (2026-08-12, third pass — found by the DRIVER, not by eye): the strip's right end carries the share button and THE PENCIL THAT LEAVES EDIT MODE, and at full bleed both sat under the parked stack rail (3100 over the dock band), so "Done arranging" could not be clicked at all — a room whose door handle was on the far side of the wall. Playwright named it where a human would have seen only a dead button: 58 retries of `<section class="stack-window"> subtree intercepts pointer events`. So the dock pads RIGHT by `var(--dock-right)` in edit mode as well (`min-width: 601px`, the same reserve `q-page-container` pads by — the page reading again; the surface stays full-bleed, only the content stops). The WELL additionally drops its pane chrome in edit mode — `margin-top: 0`, `padding-top: 0`, `border-radius: 0` — because the family's `8px/8px/--radius-md` reads as a box floating in a box at desk size and the corners round nothing (the window they sit in is square-cut on all four sides). Measured at 1440×900 / 1100×800 / 900×800: board content x = **76 / 67 / 21**, each equal to `.feed-container`'s x at that width, with **0** traffic dots buried and the well flush under the strip. ⚠ The modifier is RESTATED in the ≤600px block — the two-class pair outscores the media block's bare `.dock-window--creation`, so without the restatement a phone's edit mode would win itself the full-bleed geometry the mobile dock deliberately gave back (the source-order trap). State: `stores/dashboard.js` — `tabs[] {id: 'db'+skeletonId, skeletonId, name}` + `activeId` persisted under `pathos_dashboard_tabs` (windows.js persists only panels; per-dock CONTENT persists in the dock's own store), `isEditing` session-only on purpose (edit is a posture, not content). **MAXIMIZE IS THE OTHER EXTENDED BOARD, and since 2026-08-12 it is the desk drawn TO the chrome instead of under it** (user ask, three joins in one sitting: "remove its padding from the top so it is right below the thin header bar", "add to it some padding on the left edge so it starts drawing right next to the left drawer", "reduce its padding on the right so the right edge is next to the feed stack and pin side bars"). `.dock-window--dashboard.is-max:not(.is-edit)` in `_components.scss`, beside the geometry it overrides: `top: var(--media-tabs-h, 0px)` — **the silver rail's underside, with nothing in between** (2026-08-12, restated in the same sitting the crown strip was deleted: "make sure the dashboard component on extended mode is touching the silver thin top header bar, with no padding in between"); the creation footprint's 14px `--dock-gap` off the top chrome is a WINDOW's daylight, and an extended board is not a window on a page — it is the page), `right: var(--dock-right, 0px)` (the gap comes off the rail reserve too, so the board's right edge lands on the same line every other surface stops at), and from `min-width: 1024px` `left: var(--dock-rail-w)` + the creation coat's `border-left` back — the drawer is only a STANDING rail above Quasar's `show-if-above` breakpoint, so below it `left: 0` from `.dock-window--creation.is-max` is the honest answer. Both top corners square: the box touches chrome on all four sides now and a rounded cap would leave a notch of daylight in the drawer/header junction. ⚠ `:not(.is-edit)` is load-bearing TWICE — it keeps arranging-while-maximized on the full-bleed edit geometry (the two answers to "how big is an extended board" stay apart), and its 0,3,0 settles the pair against `.dock-window--creation.is-max` without depending on source order. ⚠ Both blocks are `min-width` gated so the phone never sees them: the ≤600px block gives a maximized dock its gap daylight back, `--dock-right` is 0 down there, and this pair would otherwise outscore it. Measured at 1380×900 (empty era): dock `l 720 / w 660`, top 38, bottom edge exactly the bar's 849; mobile `l 8 / w 359` with 17px dots. Verified 2026-08-11: edit box `x 0 / w 1394` on 1440 (right stops at the rail). Verified 2026-08-12 — maximized: `42,24 → 1394,849` on 1440×900 and `42,21 → 1054,751` on 1100×800 (drawer's right edge 42, crown bottom 24/21, rails' left edge 1398/1058, footer top), `0,21 → 854,751` on 900×800 (no standing drawer, so no left join), `8,30 → 382,794` on 390×844 (the phone's gap daylight untouched); maximized **+ edit** still `0,24 → 1440,849`, the full-bleed geometry unchanged. |
| `dashboard/DashboardGrid.vue` | **One open board's content** (dashboards phase 4, 2026-08-10) — a 2×N grid of cells dealt from the ITEMS walk + the LAYOUT JSON: layout cells first (`{virtual}` → `SessionActivityCard`; `{ref}` → the referenced item), then every ITEMS entry the layout didn't place. **ONE READ PER BOARD**: `GET /dashboards/:id` (card+items+layout) then ONE `POST /skeletons/walk-batch` over every skeleton ref on the board (ITEMS ∪ layout refs) — pre-walked results deal into `ResourceSkeletonMini` cells (never a walk per cell; the batch endpoint exists for exactly this surface); node cells mount `ElementMini` (it already owns media/locked/embed dispatch); failed batch entries render LockedChip (gate) / InfoChip (404). Grid = `repeat(2, minmax(0, 1fr))` (the `auto`-minimum gotcha law), one-column under 600px; a board with ONE cell spans both columns. Each `.dash-cell` publishes the HOUSE CONTRACTS: `--media-max-h: 240px` (tiles; 420px when wide — silence would mean 180px thumbnails) and `--resource-mini-max-h: 300px` (60vh wide) for the mini table's scroll ceiling. EDIT MODE: the two smart inputs in a toolbar (picks → `addItem` → reload) + per-cell ✕ riding the pin splice (`removeItem`, only on cells with a `link_id` — virtual/layout-only cells carry none). Emits `resolved` (the board card) so the dock names the tab. |
| `dashboard/CuentistaRow.vue` | ⭐ **The agent seat under the dashboard strip** (dashboards phase 7, 2026-08-11) — collapsed one-liner ⇄ the pair-chat thread, grey family with the ONE quasar-teal accent as the speaking light (pulses while the seat owes an answer). Resolves the seat by username off `/refs/search`, find-or-creates the pair chat (`chatService.open`). **Asks grant**: every send carries `shareRefs` = the open board's ref + the grid's shown item refs + dropped elements, and the same refs ride the body as `-[[pathos:…]]` micro chips — exactly what the listener reads its context from. The whole row is a drop target (`application/x-pathos-ref` → `NanoRefChip`s, inert until send). Thread renders through MessageBubble + PollCard; `poll.*`/`chat.message` events for the pair chat reload it live. PollCard's `modify` emit prefills the input with the receipt's original PROMPT; `applied` bubbles up so the dock reloads the grid. |
| `dashboard/NanoRefChip.vue` | **The NANO tier** (phase 7; the NanoLabelSlider naming precedent) — the smallest reference form: kind glyph + 8-char hash + ✕, deliberately INERT until send (no resolution, no routing, no fetch). A dropped element waits here; on send its ref joins the ask's body AND shareRefs. |
| `chat/PollCard.vue` (transformation face) | **Phase 7**: an approval poll whose QUESTION carries the receipt chip (`-[[pathos:skeletons/…\|receipt]]`, appended by transformService — the keep-in-step discovery seam, `RECEIPT_RE`) grows the third affordance **Modify** beside Yes/No (= vote NO + `POST /transformations/:id/decline` + the `modify` emit — polls stay strictly yes/no, modify is decline + fresh proposal by design) and, once **approved** and viewed by the decider, the **Apply** button — `POST /transformations/:id/apply` under the viewer's own JWT; 40904 (already applied) flips the card to `applied ✓` gracefully. |
| `dashboard/SplitLayout.vue` | ⭐ **The RECURSIVE SPLITTER** (dashboards phase 5, 2026-08-10) — the board as a splittable multi-dashboard canvas, rendered whenever the LAYOUT carries a `split` tree (read mode shows it too; edit mode adds the affordances). The tree grammar + THE MIN PANE SIZE (240×160) live in **`utils/splitTree.js`** — stated ONCE; a restated copy owes an fsck --static witness. Dividers ride the BottomSplitter pointer-drag pattern (pointer capture, clamp, dblclick → half): the clamp holds both panes ≥ the min (verified: a hard-left drag lands at exactly 240px), a split too small for both minimums pins its divider mid-way, and a pane under the min swaps content for its NAME PLATE (ResizeObserver). Leaves resolve their ref (`refService.summary`) and mount `DashboardGrid` with `nested` — a nested grid never renders its own split (depth policy + cycle guard; the async-import seam breaks the component cycle, MarkdownBody's law). EDIT MODE: empty leaves are drop targets on `application/x-pathos-ref` (`is-dragover` highlight, try/catch parse ignores foreign drags), every leaf offers split-h/split-v, filled leaves ✕ (clear), and a divider whose BOTH panes are empty grows the centered FUSE control (⚠ it sits where a dblclick lands — a dblclick on an all-empty split fuses, by design the two controls never coexist with content). Every change emits `changed` → DashboardGrid debounce-saves (arrangements are history); fusing the last split away returns the flat grid. Export button (grid toolbar): mints a NEW board with LAYOUT = the tree + ITEMS = the leaf refs — the arrangement is itself a dashboard. |
| `chat/ConversationPicker.vue` | **The share-to-chat dialog** (dashboards phase 5, 2026-08-10) — behind every share button (dashboards: the dock's tab strip; posts: the feed card's action lane). Owns the WHOLE prefill flow: `chatService.list()` → pick → the chat store's drafts seam (`setDraft` with the `[[pathos:<ref>]]` chip appended, `setActive`, `open`) — then ChatDock's existing machinery finishes it (REF_RE → AccessTreeDialog `mode="share"` → confirmed rows = `shareRefs` grants → send). **Sharing grants, never publishes.** Note the access tree lists the element AND its parts (a post row + its CONTENT node) — each row toggles separately, and only toggled rows grant (verified: post-only untoggled = recipient still 403; both toggled = 200). A fresh pair chat honors the opener rule — after the opener message the composer disables ("Waiting for them to accept…") until the other seat accepts, ChatDock's own gate surface. |
| `dashboard/SkeletonSearchInput.vue` + `dashboard/NodeSearchInput.vue` | **The edit mode's smart inputs** (dashboards phase 4, 2026-08-10) — one shared skin (`smart-input.scss`, imported by both scoped blocks so the pair can never drift): white field on the dock's grey dials, teal only on focus/hover. Skeletons: recent-first on focus (`GET /skeletons?sort=recent`), name LIKE as you type (`?q=`), rows show name + SCHEMA badge + #id. Nodes: empty focus lists the ACTING entity's recent uploads (`GET /nodes/uploads?ownerId=`), typing rides the FULLTEXT shadow (`GET /api/search` → `nodes`). Enter picks the first row; click picks; `@mousedown.prevent` on rows keeps focus in the field through a pick (else focusout closes the drop before click lands). Every row is a **drag SOURCE** speaking `application/x-pathos-ref` (`{kind, address, primary}`) — the phase-5 layout slots are the drop targets. Both emit `pick {kind, address, id, name}`; the host owns the addItem call. |
| `maker/MakerDock.vue` | ⭐ Docked post maker rising from the nav bar — draft tabs (`stores/maker.js` + localStorage; comment drafts carry a reply icon + tooltip), Mac traffic lights running **red-yellow-green close/min/max since 2026-08-04** (the true Mac order, reversed from the old green-yellow-red on all five dock windows — maker/uploader/builder/label/chat); minimized state renders as a minitab in `NavigationBar`'s strip. Body = `PostMakerSurface`. Posting a comment draft from the dock navigates to the parent thread via `gotoCommentThread` (unravel + halo) instead of the new post's own page. **Wears `.dock-window--creation`** (2026-07-27, shared with the uploader / skeleton builder / label maker — **and with CHAT since 2026-08-03**, user ask: "make the window occupy the same dimensions as the maker and uploader windows when expanded"; so this is now the footprint of all FIVE bottom docks, verified pixel-identical to chat's at 1280×860 — x 640, 580×778, 60px right, 50px bottom): the per-dock `80vw × 80vh` footprints are gone and the docks share ONE geometry stated in `_components.scss` — `left: 50vw`, `right` clearing the stack/pins column by a 14px `--dock-gap`, `top` the same daylight under the top chrome — **and since 2026-08-24 under what HANGS off it** (user ask: the four creation windows must "leave a little space between their top border and the header nav bar tabs"): `--media-tabs-h` is the RAIL, while `.media-tabs__row`'s parked tabs are absolute at `top: 100%` and hang below it, so the daylight was being measured off the wrong line and a tab's bottom edge landed exactly on a dock's top border (measured: both at y=29, and the rail at z 3125 draws over every dock, so the tab read as welded to the window's cap). The top is `--dock-gap + --media-tabs-h + --media-tabs-park-h` now — 43px, 14px of clear air under the tab — and the reserve is PERMANENT rather than conditional on a tab being parked, since a dock has a `top` transition and would visibly slide every time a flyout minimized. (The dashboard's `.is-edit` / `.is-max` geometries still weld to `--media-tabs-h`: those two are the board becoming the page, so the tabs lie over them the way they lie over the feed.) — bottom anchored straight to the NAV BAR's top edge — `var(--nav-footer-h)` (2026-07-27 second pass; it stood one `--frieze-h` higher on the frieze footer band via `--dock-bottom` for a day, and chat read that token until 2026-08-03 — **band and token are both GONE since 2026-08-04**, the base `.dock-window` shell stating `bottom: var(--nav-footer-h)` directly as a default every dock overrides) — BOTH top corners rounded — **`--dock-radius`, a seventh dial since 2026-08-24** (user ask: "make their corners a little less round"), `--radius-lg` → `--radius-md` (17.5px → 11.9px measured); a dial rather than a literal because the cap is restated three more times in `_components.scss`, always in a rule giving a squared corner BACK (the ≤600px block's left cap, the dashboard's edit-mode restatement of both), two of them inside a media block where a later pass would miss them. It sits on the shared FOOTPRINT, so all six windows round the same amount — one box in six colorways may not disagree about its shape. — and a flat plaque in `--brown-3` lines (no glass/blur), **`--light-cream` since 2026-08-24** (user ask: the post, skeletons, labels and uploads windows should "all have light-cream background color"), from the `--brown-1` it had worn since 2026-08-02. That is the tone the nav bar these docks are WELDED TO took on 2026-08-17 along with the drawer, both side widgets and the top rail (_tokens.scss § `--light-cream`, "the sheet of ALL FIXED CHROME"), so the weld no longer joins two materials. It reaches exactly the four windows the ask named and that is the dials doing their job: chat and the dashboard repoint `--dock-coat` themselves, so the base value is by construction "the colorway of the docks with no colorway of their own" (verified: cream `rgb(252,243,224)` on the four, `--lime-1` on chat, `--grey-3` on the dashboard, all six on the new cap and the new clearance). ⭐ **THAT SPLIT LASTED TWO DAYS — 2026-08-26 gave ALL SIX the bar's `--plaque-coat`** (user ask: they should all be light-cream "with the same veil/effect as the footer navigation bar so the windows match the exact same bar color"): the base dial went from flat `--light-cream` to the two-layer plaque and both colorways stopped declaring it, so the coat is one value in one place for the whole family and a colorway is its lines, wells and ink. Verified: shell AND `.dock-bar` on each of the six report `.nav-bar`'s exact `background-color` + `background-image`, rules still brown-3 / lime-3 / grey-4. `--dock-rule` deliberately stays brown — the cream's line ink elsewhere is `--grey-6`, and moving that dial would repaint the tab rims and the header underline in the same stroke. **The coat and the line are DIALS** (2026-08-03): `--dock-coat`/`--dock-well`/`--dock-rule`/`--dock-rule-strong`/`--dock-ink`/`--dock-ink-mute` name every tone this chrome draws with, defaulting to the brown family, so a dock in another colorway is a handful of custom properties on the root and nothing else (`.dock-window--chat` is the first — see ChatDock). They are variables rather than a repainting modifier because the pieces are reached at three specificities here (root, `.dock-bar`, `.dock-tab`) plus once more inside the mobile block, and a modifier would have had to out-specify each separately — the mobile `border-left` restatement in particular would have quietly won against it. Its drop shadow reaches **up and to the LEFT only** since 2026-08-02 (`-10px -10px 30px -6px rgba(0,0,0,.42)`, was `0 12px 38px rgba(0,0,0,.45)`): this dock's bottom edge IS the nav bar's top edge, so a shadow offset 12px DOWN laid its darkest part across the bar's whole width and the dock read as floating over it (the user's words). A bottom-anchored panel RISES from the bar — it is welded to it, and a welded panel casts nothing onto it. The negative `-6px` spread is what guarantees zero downward reach: the blur's own half-radius (15px) minus the spread minus the `-10px` y-offset is `-1px`, and the same arithmetic clears the right side where the dock meets the side-widget column. Chat wears this shadow too since 2026-08-03 (it kept the base shell's `0 -10px 40px` while it stood ON the frieze band; welded to the bar, it casts what the others cast). The dock bar is inked like the parked side widgets (`--brown-8` glyph + title, `--brown-4` meta) and `--brown-2` resting tabs (active keeps paper + the teal inset). Maximize = `left: 0` (top gap and right daylight stay); `<900px` takes full width; **`<600px` the dock spans the width on a NARROWED gap** — `--dock-gap: 8px` and `left: var(--dock-gap)` only, since `top`/`right` are already stated in terms of that variable and `--dock-right` reads 0 on a phone, so all three edges keep 8px of daylight and BOTH top radii come back (2026-08-03, user ask: "a little bit of space left between the top end of the screen and the window and also a little padding on the sides"; change request #675 had taken the gaps and radii away entirely and the dock read as a PAGE rather than a window — 8px gives #675 back most of what it asked for, 16px of a 375px screen instead of 28, while keeping the window a window. `border-left` + `border-top-left-radius` are restated there because the `<900px` rule squares the left side off and fires here too — and stated as `var(--dock-rule)`, never a literal, so chat's left border comes back GREEN). The dock body's padding drops to 8px so the room goes to the editor. **The HEADER LAYOUT is shared desktop+mobile since 2026-08-04** (user ask — desktop should match the pattern the 2026-08-03 mobile pass introduced; it lived in the mobile media query for a day): the traffic cluster leaves the flow (`position: absolute; left: 8px`) and the bar goes `justify-content: center` on a symmetric `0 80px` padding **in the base `.dock-window--creation` rule**, so on every viewport the title is centred and the dots sit at the LEFT edge, iOS-sheet fashion — out of the flow rather than `order: -1` because the five docks put different things between title and lights (the makers a `q-space`, which the rule hides; chat a `.dock-bar__left` group) and no single flex arrangement centres the title in all of them. The 80px is sized for the mobile cluster's real footprint plus air (3 × 17px dots + 2 × 8px gaps = 67, at an 8px inset = 75 — it clears the smaller 13px desktop dots with room to spare) and the title/meta truncate — keep those numbers in step, because a centred flex row that outgrows its box grows out of BOTH ends and an untruncated count would put the title's first letters behind the red dot (which LEADS the cluster since the 2026-08-04 order reversal). The only mobile-specific piece left is the touch bump: dots 13px → **17px** (glyph 9 → 11px) on a wider 8px gap. The 50/50 capture split keeps its old bottom-anchored 80vh panes (`top: auto`). The docks' z floor moved with it: `stores/windows.js` `Z_BASE` 2400 → **3010**, because the feed container (z 3001) and its flyout (3002) painted over every dock — the sandwich is feed 3001/3002 → docks 3010+ → drawer 3050 → stack/pins 3100, so the side bars stay above every dock, parked or expanded ⭐ **THE WINDOW'S NAME IS A PLATE, AND ITS TABS LIGHT UP GREY (2026-08-26, two user asks)** — (1) the bar's glyph + `Post maker` are wrapped in `.dock-bar__plate`, the FEED CARD'S OWN POST PLATE (`.post-square__foot .post-square__chip` — MicroChip rim `rgba(ink,.18)` / wash `.04` / ink `.78`, 4px corners, `--font-display`), so the window states itself in the object its published posts state themselves with; the draft count stays OUTSIDE the plate (the plate names the window, it does not report on it). (2) `.dock-tab.is-active`'s lit lip and `.dock-tab--new:hover` leave the shared chrome's teal `#00829c` for **`--maker-contrast`**, the post window's one contrast dial. Both rules are scoped to `.maker-dock`: `.dock-bar` / `.dock-tab` are chrome shared by six windows, the tabs simply carry this component's scope attribute so a two-class rule outranks the global one, and the other five docks are untouched ⭐ **AND THE WINDOW BECAME ITS OWN COLORWAY, SHEET INCLUDED (2026-08-26, two more asks the same sitting)** — (a) `--dock-coat: var(--maker-coat)` on `.maker-dock`: the plaque's construction with both dials turned, a `--light-cream` sheet under a **40% `--blue-grey-1`** veil where the shared one is 30% `--grey-3` (composite rgb(246,241,231) against the bar's rgb(248,242,228)). ⚠ This is the **ONE SANCTIONED BREAK** in the one-plaque law written the same day; `fsck --static`'s `dock-coat` witness now walks the WHOLE FE (it read `_components.scss` alone before, so a per-window re-declaration in a .vue file passed unseen) and fails on any declaration not in its `SANCTIONED_COATS` map. (b) The five `--dock-*` dials leave BROWN at `.maker-dock` — `--dock-rule` → `--maker-contrast` (inner borders: shell, bar underline, tab strip, tab rims), `--dock-rule-strong` → `--blue-grey-8`, `--dock-ink` → `--maker-contrast` (titles), `--dock-ink-mute` → `--blue-grey-4`, `--dock-well` → `--blue-grey-1`; the one brown that is NOT a dial (`.dock-tab:hover`'s `--brown-10` ink) is overridden here too. The other four creation docks keep the warm chrome — this is the seam chat already uses to go lime |
| `maker/PostMakerSurface.vue` | ⭐ The maker's **editor surface** (MakerHeader + slotted title + NoteEditor + `RefBrowser` + submit), shared by the dock and the viewers' embedded comment composers (`embed` prop = compact density, capped refs column, Close button). **Dense dock layout (change request #675, 2026-08-02)**: the title input rides MakerHeader's `#title` slot so the meta chrome is a flex-**wrap** control row — `title \| author \| labels` on one line when the editor column is wide, title above the picker pair when narrow (fixed grid tracks overflowed the ~340px half-dock column) — with captionless pickers (`dense` prop → compact AuthorPicker/LabelPicker/LabelBox without the embed's shrunken type) and the footer hint cut to ONE ellipsized line + a `help_outline` tooltip carrying the full references doctrine; the refs column ceded ground (`minmax(230px, 290px)`, was 280/340). **Stacked layouts (≤900px) are plain block flow, not a one-column grid** — a vh `min-height` on the editor doesn't count into a grid row's intrinsic size, so the refs row painted over the note's overflow. **Phones (≤600px)**: NoteEditor mounts in `edit` mode (a 375px split pane leaves no room to write; `windows.isMobile`), the note gets `min-height: 48vh`, the hint hides (tooltip stays). **Retouch (2026-08-02)**: AuthorPicker's selected value is ONE ellipsized line that can never escape the 30px control (`.selected-row` min-width 0 + nowrap `.q-field__native`; full name on the title tooltip; the kind chip **hides in compact** — a long `ORG_ALTER_EGO` chip crushed the name to nothing); LabelBox's chip well caps at ~two rows with an **inner scroll** (`.box-content` max-height 68px / 52px compact, thin scrollbar) and compact chips tightened another step (18px, author names ellipsized at 22ch). Comment drafts (`draft.parent = { kind: post\|comment\|node, id, hash, route, label }`) show the **comment-target banner** — "this post will be published as a comment on <chip>"; the chip opens the parent, unraveling from the thread root when the parent is itself a comment. Comment drafts render **no RefBrowser** (`.no-refs` single-column grid) — no reference rows may appear in a comment pane before the comment exists — and submit the body **exactly as typed** via `commentOn` / `commentOnNode`: the ancestry lives on the comment's skeleton (PARENT slot + COMMENTS surround), never as text in the content node. Plain drafts keep the RefBrowser, append staged-but-uninserted refs as a trailing References list, and post via `POST /posts`. Closing an embed **parks** the draft (`makerStore.parkDraft`): kept as a dock tab, dock surfaces minimized so its minitab is visible ⭐ **ONE CONTRAST TONE, AND THE CARD'S PLATE ON THE PUBLISH CONTROL (2026-08-26, two user asks)** — `.maker-surface` declares **`--q-primary: var(--maker-contrast)`**, which is the whole repaint: the brand violet `#6C63FF` comes from `quasar.config.js`'s `framework.config.brand` (NOT `quasar.variables.scss`, which maps `$primary: $ink` and never reaches the screen — so the tone is ungreppable in the stylesheets), it arrives as a custom property on the body, and custom properties inherit THROUGH component boundaries, scoped styles included. One line therefore takes the previewer's Edit│Split│Preview toggle (NoteEditor's `toggle-color="primary"`), every q-field focus ring/label and the ref browser's spinner off the violet, while the rest of the app keeps the brand. The submit button drops `color="primary"` for `.maker-surface__post` — the feed card's post plate copied dial for dial (chrome only, not the MicroChip component: that chip addresses an element that EXISTS, and this button's job is that it does not yet), hovering by FILLING with the window's contrast rather than taking the card's teal ink, and softening its rim under Quasar's `.disabled` wash **The contrast walked three tones in one sitting** — `--grey-6` → `--blue-grey-8` → `--blue-grey-6` — and is a DIAL now (`--maker-contrast`, `_tokens.scss`): name the role here, never the level. The refs card's border joined the window's inner borders in the third ask. The title input dropped its **floating label for a placeholder** in the second: a q-input's label floats into the control's top, which is exactly why Quasar's dense field is 40px, and the row carries its own caption now |
| `maker/RefBrowser.vue` | Kind-scoped reference search (`GET /api/refs/search`) + reorderable ref list + insert-at-cursor `[[pathos:…]]` chips. Each staged reference wears an **auto/micro/mini stamp** (2026-07-31, tiny cycling toggle persisted on the draft's reference object): `PostMakerSurface.sigilOf` writes the matching sigil (` `/`-`/`!`) both on invoke-at-cursor and in the appended References list **KIND PILLS ARE ONE GREY NOW (2026-08-26, user ask)**: `.ref-kind.is-active` was tinted per kind off `KIND_TABS[].color` through an inline `--kind-color` (posts `#6c4d72`, nodes `#2C3D4E`, labels `#00829c`, paths `#4d8a83`, skels `#5b6c82`); the binding and the keys are gone and the dial resolves to **`--maker-contrast`**, the post window's contrast dial. The kind palette still classifies where it classifies MANY things at once — the result rows' icons, `utils/kinds.js` — but a row where exactly one pill is lit does not also need the lit pill to say which kind, since its own label does **Its SECTION TITLES letter in the contrast too** (2026-08-26, third ask) — every word that NAMES something in this window is one colour and every word that IS something is ink — and the resting pill's rim is `--blue-grey-4`, one ramp step up rather than a wash of the contrast: five rims at full strength read as five pressed pills |
| `maker/UploaderDock.vue` | ⭐ Docked uploader, same chrome as MakerDock (incl. `.dock-window--creation` — see that row) — and since 2026-08-26 **the maker's colorway construction in TEAL**: the five `--dock-*` dials, its own sanctioned sheet (`--uploader-coat`, named in `fsck --static`'s `dock-coat` witness), the bar's name plate, and the maker family's dials (`--maker-contrast` / `--maker-pale` / `--maker-deep`) + `--q-primary` re-pointed on `.uploader-dock` so the shared `MakerHeader` (mounted `dense` — its row captions were being DUPLICATED by the pickers' own titles) and every Quasar accent follow; all accents read `--uploader-contrast` (→ `--teal-6`, the teal at blue-grey-6's Material index). Each tab is one upload in course (`stores/uploader.js`; staged File objects are session-only) and shows **all three methods at once** (2026-08-26 — the mode pills and the per-tab `mode` died): **note** 45% of the editor's height (a bare `<textarea>` — NoteEditor's preview chrome left with the pills; still submits as a `.md` file), **link** 15% (input + button; the typed URL still resolves via `POST /embeds/resolve`, debounced 350ms + sequence-guarded, but recognition is a one-line hint now — the `EmbedFrame` player preview left with the room to render it), **files** 40% (drop zone + staged **preview tiles**: object-URL `<img>` / first-frame `<video>` for media, kind glyph otherwise — revoked when a file leaves staging; the drop zone is the section's flexible piece — alone it fills everything under the head, staged it splits evenly with the grid). Each section has its own post-plate submit (`busyKind` in the store says which is in flight); a success clears only the section that fired, and the whole stack sits in its own box (1px `--uploader-contrast` rim, `--radius-md`, no fill — `.method-stack` IS the box). The explorer column is **40% of the screen's width** — `min(40vw, calc(100% - 312px))`, exact when maximized, capped by the editor's 300px floor at rest. ≤900px: the body is BLOCK flow (a grid auto-row cannot see the cells' aspect-ratio heights and the editor painted over the explorer — gotchas has the two-level story), explorer below the editor with its head on the first phone screen; the methods are two contiguous `4 / 3` cells (born square, densified same day: `aspect-ratio` + `min-height: auto`, tightened gaps) over the thin full-width link bar. Witness flow: `flow-uploader-teal.mjs`. Stacks with the other windows via `stores/windows.js` (opening it covers the maker, closing/minimizing reveals it again) |
| `maker/FileExplorer.vue` | The uploader dock's right column — paginated `GET /api/nodes/uploads` batches, search by original filename, list mode (title · preview · date-time · labels) + gallery mode (rectangular tiles); click routes to the node viewer |
| `maker/SkeletonBuilderDock.vue` | ⭐ Docked **schema builder**, same chrome as the other docks (incl. `.dock-window--creation` — see MakerDock) (`stores/skeletonBuilder.js` + localStorage `pathos_skeleton_builder`). Each tab is one schema in course, in one of two modes: **start** (define a new schema / instantiate or fork an existing schema / search schemas by name via `GET /skeletons/templates?q=`), **define** (name + fields — **each field IS a label**, picked or minted in place through `labels/LabelFieldPicker.vue` — plus an optional **constraint** kind ∈ entities\|posts\|nodes\|labels\|paths\|skeletons\|moments\|links → `POST /skeletons/templates` with `{ labelId, kind }` slots; editing an owned schema shows locked rows + append-only new fields → `POST /:id/slots`). **Instantiate** (from start list, define footer, or the schema viewer) calls `POST /:id/instantiate` and **navigates to the new instance's `/skeletons/:id` viewer** — population no longer happens in the dock. **Fork** (2026-07-18) copies a schema's fields into a fresh define form to rename + modify and stamps `draft.forkOf {id,name}` (a banner marks the form); submit goes through `POST /skeletons/templates/:id/fork` so the new schema records real lineage (`forked_from_id` + spine ancestry) — the schema viewer shows a "forked from" chip (`walk.skeleton.forked_from`). The seeded per-kind element schemas (NODE/LABEL/MOMENT/SECRET/LINK/PATH/SKELETON, forks of ELEMENT) appear in the start list like any other schema. `openForkDraft(walk)` is the ownership-independent fork opener used by the SkeletonPage Fork button and `ElementActions` (the generic `/skeletons/:id/fork` endpoint refuses origins — schemas fork through the dock's draft → `POST /skeletons/templates/:id/fork`). |
| `skeletons/SkeletonInstanceEditor.vue` | ⭐ The owned-instance **population surface** rendered by `SkeletonPage` (left = editable `FIELD \| ACCEPTS \| CONTENT` table, right = Populate panel). A bound cell renders the referenced element as its full Mini panel (`shared/ElementMini`), with a pencil button (textable fields — reopens the inline editor) and a clear button beside it. Click an empty cell to edit on the fly: unconstrained/`nodes` fields open an inline text box (`PUT /:id/slot/:name` `{ text }` — first bind mints a real NOTE node; re-edits **version that node** via the ancestor chain, so its viewer shows prior texts); **`moments`-constrained fields open a native DATE input instead** (Thread H, 2026-07-31 — `event` icon, "click to pick a date"): committing calls `POST /moments/ensure` (mint-or-reuse at UTC midnight, unlocated) and binds the returned `moments/<hash>` ref, so BIRTHDAY-style fields fill without hunting for a moment element; any field also accepts a dragged/picked ref (`{ ref }`). Right panel = `SlotRefPicker` (kind-locked search, `entities` included) + **Upload** / **Build post** buttons (open the uploader/maker) + a **fresh tray**: elements minted while populating are delivered by `stores/skeletonBuilder.deliverFresh` (no navigation) and drag/pick straight into a field. `onMounted`→`beginPopulate`, `onBeforeUnmount`→`endPopulate`. |
| `maker/SlotRefPicker.vue` | The builder's search column — kind pills for every referenceable kind incl. moments + links (locked to the selected slot's declared kind), debounced `GET /api/refs/search` (matches by title/name AND hash prefix), results clickable (fill selected slot) and draggable (`application/x-pathos-ref` payload) onto slot rows |
| `maker/LabelMakerDock.vue` | ⭐ Docked **label maker** (`stores/labelMaker.js`; nav-bar creation buttons — a `q-btn-group` "create bundle" until 2026-08-23, four individual buttons since — `label_important` icon; wears `.dock-window--creation` — see MakerDock). Left: `labels/LabelTreeMini` in **appendable** mode (2026-07-19) — every unraveled layer ends with an add row: inline create on owned layers, suggest/fork hand-off on foreign ones; root layer ends with an inline new-root input. Right: action panel on the selection — breadcrumb chain, sys/yours badges, then **child** / **brother** (both `LabelMiniMaker` with `can-suggest`; brother targets the parent), **rename** + **move** (owner-only, `PUT /labels/:id` — move picks the new ancestor via a second mini tree or detaches to root), **suggest** (foreign trees only: name + optional message → `POST /labels/:id/suggest` mints the owner's poll; "Fork instead" escape in the panel), **fork tree** (`POST /labels/:id/fork`, selects the owned copy so it opens in the maker ready to grow), **delete** (owner-only, non-system: danger panel → `DELETE /labels/:id` deletes the whole branch; flash reports the count). Flashes render regardless of selection (a delete clears it). No selection → plant a new root. |
| `posts/PostCommentItem.vue` | ⭐ Recursive comment card: inline reply embeds `PostMakerSurface` (draft parented to the comment), vote panel, and a "N comments / forks" dropdown (`reply_count + fork_count`) that merges the child's own `listChildren` + `listForks` — comment AND fork trees unravel recursively. Honors the `unravelPath` prop (`?unravel=` chains): expands along the chain and halos the target (`.is-unravel-target`, luminous-yellow like the nav return halo). |
| `nodes/NodeForkItem.vue` | Recursive fork-NODE row for the node viewer's Forks pane (`GET /nodes/:id/forks`): owner, excerpt, `node #id` link, and a per-fork "N forks" toggle that recurses into the fork's own forks. Same card idiom as `PostCommentItem`. |
