Adopted by `LinkViewerPage`, `PathViewerPage`, `LabelDetailPage` sidebars (Node/Post pages keep their bespoke section UIs over the same walk-backed data). Since 2026-07-30 takes an optional **`provenance-ref`** (`'<kind>/<hash>'` of the element itself) and mounts `ProvenanceBadge` in the header row — link/path viewers pass their element's address. Since 2026-07-31 (Thread K) also an optional **`access-ref`** — when set, `AccessContractPanel` closes the surround (path/link viewers pass the same address) |
| `shared/AccessContractPanel.vue` | **The element viewer's access contract** (Thread K, 2026-07-31): the owner's who-can-read band over `GET /access/status` (decorated entries) — one row per recorded grant (world/person icon · name · by whom · moment), a `history` chip to the ACCESS path viewer (`access_path_id`), and per-row **revoke** (`POST /access/revoke` — mirror rows removed, chain history stays; revoking the pioneer = unpublish). Self-hides unless `gated && canManage`, so it mounts safely anywhere an address is known: NodeDetailPage side column, SkeletonPage (owned-instance editor + read/grid branch side column, schemas excluded), and via `ElementSurround`'s `access-ref` on path/link viewers. |
| `shared/ProvenanceBadge.vue` | **The truth spine's face** (Thread B, 2026-07-30) — `FileContractBadge`'s general sibling: where that one asks "are the bytes behind this FILE node intact?", this asks "does this element's CHAIN story hold?" via `GET /api/verify/<kind>/<hash>` (`services/verify.service.js`). Renders **chain verified** (mint carved chip — click downloads the portable bundle for the offline verifier), **unsigned** (FLAT muted chip — legacy is a fact, not an alarm; covers `unsigned-legacy` + `unchained`), or **chain broken** (red carved chip, `gpp_bad`) with the failing check in the tooltip. Drafts, non-verifiable kinds and transport failures hide it (no verdict ≠ a verdict). Mounted via `ElementSurround`'s `provenance-ref` and in `NodeDetailPage`'s meta row |
| `shared/MarkdownBody.vue` | ⭐ The **only** markdown renderer (marked + DOMPurify + `utils/pathosRefs.js`). `[[pathos:<kind>/<hash>\|label]]` renders as an inline chip; `![[pathos:<kind>/<hash>]]` as a **block embed** — the full `ElementMini` panel in the text flow; `-[[pathos:…]]` (2026-07-31) as a **forced micro chip** — the author's sigil beats the surface everywhere. Chip tier for bare refs per surface via the `refDisplay` prop (`mini` / `micro` / `info` / `auto`). The **`auto` tier** (feed cards + post viewer) mounts `nodes/NodeRefAuto.vue` for bare NODE refs: the micro chip renders immediately, one `GET /nodes/by-path` probe runs behind it, and a node carrying an `embed` descriptor or a media `file.kind` (image/video/audio) swaps to the teal `NodeMini` panel — its `upgrade` event tells MarkdownBody to widen the placeholder to `pathos-ref-embed` figure treatment. Non-node bare refs on `auto` stay `RefMicro` chips. **`breaks` prop** (2026-07-25, default `true`): the platform default treats a single newline as a hard `<br>` — the chat/comment convention the app was written around — and it is now overridable **per render** (`marked.parse(text, { breaks })`, not the global `marked.setOptions`, so one surface opting out cannot move another). Pass `:breaks="false"` for **hard-wrapped prose**: the docs and every post mirroring one are wrapped at ~72 chars in source, and under the default each of those lines becomes its own `<br>`, laying the paragraph out at the AUTHOR's wrap width rather than the container's — the symptom is body text that looks cut off at half the box (`FeedStream`'s post squares, where it measured ~50% fill before and 96–99% after). **`plainRefs` prop** (2026-08-04, default `false`): `true` short-circuits the whole ref pipeline at the `parsed` computed — no placeholder spans, no chips, no probes; `[[pathos:…]]` renders as literal text. For surfaces showing a markdown FILE as a *document* (the floating media viewer): the file's refs are quoted content there, not the surface's links. |
| `shared/EmbedFrame.vue` | ⭐ The platform's **one `<iframe>`** (2026-07-26). Everything it needs arrives as DATA on the `embed` descriptor an `EMBED_RULE` produced API-side (`node.embed`, or `POST /api/embeds/resolve`): `src`, `title`, `allow`, `aspect`, `icon` (the caption's q-icon, 2026-07-27 — `play_circle` under a video, `menu_book` under a Wikipedia article, defaulting to `play_circle` when a rule states none), `mode` + `zoom` (2026-07-27 — `mode: 'page'` swaps the ratio-locked geometry for a DOCUMENT's: `.embed-frame__box--page` takes the surface's full width and `--media-max-h` directly as height (fallback 60vh), and the iframe is laid out at `100% / zoom` then `transform: scale(zoom)` from the top-left corner — the only way to shrink a cross-origin page's type; zoom is re-clamped to 0.4–1 in `boxStyle` before it lands on the transform). No surface builds an embed URL, and no rule ever ships markup — that separation is what keeps a forkable rule from becoming stored XSS. A ratio-locked box (`aspect-ratio` from the rule, `loading="lazy"`, `referrerpolicy="strict-origin-when-cross-origin"`) over an optional provider/source caption, wrapped in `@click.stop.prevent` because Minis are router-links and a click on the play button must not navigate the panel away. The box publishes the rule's ratio TWICE — as `aspect-ratio` and as a plain number on **`--embed-ratio`** — because a surface can only bound such a box by its WIDTH (`aspect-ratio` derives height from width, so a `max-height` clamps the derived value and letterboxes the player inside its own frame), and the width matching a height limit is `limit × ratio`, which the surface cannot compute from the `16 / 9` string. Since 2026-07-26 the box does that conversion ITSELF — `max-width: min(100%, calc(var(--media-max-h, 100vh) * var(--embed-ratio)))` — so a surface publishes only a HEIGHT budget and never has to know the rule's aspect (a 4:3 rule must cap at a 4:3 width). It replaced a `30vh` rule written into the feed card's stylesheet; see **the media budget** under `shared/MiniPanel.vue`. The caption grew a **`#cap-end` slot** (2026-07-27): an optional `margin-left: auto` span at the row's right end, for whatever small marks the surface wants on the provider/url line — `NodeMini` parked its flat vote thumbs there until 2026-08-23, and **the slot has had no caller since** (that ask removed the votes and turned the caption off outright — `:caption="false"`, because NodeMini prints the provider/url line in its own HEADER now and would otherwise state the same address on both sides of the picture). The slot is kept: it costs a `v-if` and it is the documented way onto that row. The url beside it keeps its ellipsis (its `overflow: hidden` zeroes the flex minimum, so it gives first). Mounted by `NodeContentViewer` (full mode, frame **above** the address — the node's content IS the address) and `NodeMini` (**captionless since 2026-08-23** — it renders the provider/url itself in its header; this is the branch a feed card's `![[pathos:nodes/…]]` block embed lands on); `UploaderDock`'s link section mounted it too until 2026-08-26's three-methods rework (recognition is a one-line hint there now). Concept: `docs/concepts/embeds.md` |
| `nodes/NodeCard.vue` | Card surface for a node |
| `nodes/NoteEditor.vue` | Markdown editor used by MakerDock (and by the UploaderDock's Write mode until 2026-08-26's three-methods rework — the uploader's note section is a bare `<textarea>` now). **Dense chrome (maker retouch, 2026-08-02)**: 5px mode bar, 10/14px pane padding, textarea 0.85em/1.6; the split **preview is a working proof, not a reading surface** — `.md-preview` steps headings down hard (`h1` 1.45em … `h6` 1em, first-heading top margin zeroed) and tightens the block rhythm (0.6em paragraph/list margins) via `:deep` overrides on the shared `.md-rendered` pipeline ⚠ **THE MODE TOGGLE HAS NO TONE OF ITS OWN** — `toggle-color="primary"` resolves to whatever `--q-primary` the host window declares, so the same component lights VIOLET on the node editor and **`--grey-6`** inside the post maker (2026-08-26, user ask — the dial is on `.maker-surface`, see PostMakerSurface) |
| `nodes/CommentItem.vue` | Legacy node-level comments (kept for migration). `CommentMaker.vue` was **deleted 2026-07-17** — the embedded `PostMakerSurface` replaced it everywhere |
| `nodes/ForkMaker.vue`, `ForkCard.vue` | Fork creation + card |
| `nodes/OriginPanel.vue` | Provenance panel — entity, secret, moment chain |
| `labels/LabelChip.vue` | Single chip |
| `labels/LabelViewer.vue` | ⭐ Ancestry-aware chip; detects MOMENT chain for clock styling. |
| `labels/LabelScroll.vue` | Horizontal scrollable row of LabelChips |
| `labels/LabelTree.vue` | Tree view of a label + children |
| `labels/LabelTreeMini.vue` (+ `LabelTreeMiniNode.vue`) | ⭐ The **mini recursive label viewer/picker**: search across all labels OR walk the forest from its roots with lazy branch expansion (`GET /labels/:id/children` on first caret click); sys/yours badges; select-only (emits `select`) by default. The `appendable` prop (label maker dock's browse tree only) closes every unraveled layer with an **add row** — owned layers inline-create a sibling/son right there (leaves become expandable so a son can be planted anywhere; emits `created`), foreign layers show "suggest / fork…" (emits `append-foreign` with the parent) — and appends a root-layer inline input |
| `labels/LabelMiniMaker.vue` | Inline create form: new root or child under a parent; when the parent tree isn't owned it pivots to the **suggest/fork flow** — `can-suggest` (label maker dock) adds a "Suggest to owner" button (emits `suggest` with the parent), fork (`POST /labels/:id/fork`) keeps working inside the owned copy. Emits `created` / `forked` / `suggest` |
| `labels/LabelFieldPicker.vue` | Form control whose value IS a label — button + `q-menu` hosting `LabelTreeMini` (pick) and `LabelMiniMaker` (create, with parent picking + fork pivot). The schema builder's field rows |
| `labels/LabelSlider.vue` | Multi-label horizontal slider on cards |
| `maker/MakerHeader.vue` | Author picker + label picker shared by every maker. ⭐ **THREE LABELLED ROWS SINCE 2026-08-26** (user ask: "put them tiny labels and make them be both in their own row instead of having entity and label input together") — `title / publishing as / labels`, each a tiny right-aligned mono caption (0.6em, `--maker-contrast`, in a `--row-label-w` column so all three fields share one left edge) beside ONE full-width control. It was a single flex-**wrap** row with captionless pickers; stacking frees the horizontal room the captions now use, and there is nothing left to wrap. Phones put the caption above its field. ⭐ **AND IT OWNS THE FIELD AESTHETIC FOR ALL THREE** (same day): `.row-field :deep(.q-field--outlined)` — 26px box, 13px corners, `--blue-grey-1` fill, **2px `--maker-contrast`** rim, thick icons. Stated once and reaching a SLOTTED child (the title input carries PostMakerSurface's scope id, not this one's — `:deep()` anchors on `.row-field`, which is ours, so it lands anyway). Four Quasar dials have to move together or the box does not shrink: `.q-field__control` (height + radius), `:before` (the rim — needs `border-radius: inherit` or the box rounds and its outline stays square), `:after` (the focus ring, same), and `.q-field__marginal` (40px in dense, and the reason a 26px control silently stays 40px). ⚠ `min-height: 0` on `.q-field__native/__input` **collapses a `use-input` field to zero height** — it emptied the labels row; 22px (26 less the two rims) is the value. ⚠ `q-icon`'s `size` prop writes an INLINE font-size that beats these rules — AuthorPicker's kind glyph is stepped up in its own template |
| `maker/AuthorPicker.vue`, `LabelPicker.vue`, `LabelBox.vue` | Building blocks. AuthorPicker renders the caller's identity tree from `GET /api/identity/authorables` — options indented by `depth`, defaulting to the `acting` identity (the one the JWT acts as, possibly an alter-ego). Org rows (2026-07): ORGANIZATION + ORG_ALTER_EGO (mask) entries list with teal icons; rows with `switchable:false` (a MEMBER seat's org entity) are dropped — publishing as the org is an admin power. A restored value (reopened draft) is kept only if it still exists in the tree; a stale id (e.g. a pre-reseed draft's author) resets to `acting` so the field never shows an unmappable raw id ⭐ **2026-08-26**: both pickers STOPPED stating their control's box (`.is-compact` carried a 30px height that would outrank the header's 26px on specificity — four classes against three); AuthorPicker's kind glyph went 13px → 15px (an inline `size` beats the header's icon rule). **LabelBox** joined the window's aesthetic — `--blue-grey-1` chips with `--maker-contrast` rims, its header a title in the contrast, the moment chip distinguished by tone within the family (a `--blue-grey-4` fill, `--blue-grey-8` rim) instead of by the `rgba(45,30,15,.55)` brown ground and amber ink it wore from the dark-surface era. ⚠ **A REAL BUG WENT WITH IT**: `.canonic-chip` painted its NAME in `rgba(ink,.85)` on a `rgba(ink,.85)` fill — the same value twice — so the author's name was INVISIBLE and the chip read as a blank dark plate with two icons. Same dark-era leftover; the dock went to paper on 2026-07-27 and the pair was never re-read |
| `entities/AlterEgoPanel.vue`, `AlterEgoTreeNode.vue` | Sub-entity tree + creation module + identity switching on the entity viewer; the recursive tree node highlights the acting identity and offers per-node switch buttons |
| `paths/PathChainView.vue`, `PathChainStep.vue` | Per-step ancestry display |
| `skeletons/SkeletonSquares.vue` | Origin-skeleton square on `/skeletons` (LabelSquares' carve-the-pit language, skeleton-slate tint). Head click = select + dig; the pit mounts `SkeletonInstanceList` instead of recursing squares — instances all inherit the template's name, so nested name-squares would just repeat the same word. |
| `skeletons/SkeletonInstanceList.vue` | Paginated instantiation list (`GET /skeletons/:id/instances`, 30/page + "+ N more…"): each row shows title (or compacted `ELEMENT:` name), author, age, 2-line excerpt, `SkeletonRefLinks` (post link on POST rows only), per-schema icon; rows select into the Structure panel and carry their own dig toggle (nested list) for instances-of-instances |
| `skeletons/SkeletonUsages.vue` | Right-hand Structure panel of the explorer — identity (MicroChip / owner / moment / lineage), slot inventory as InfoChips, label chips for whichever skeleton is selected |
| `skeletons/SlotRenderer.vue` | Read-only field row on `SkeletonPage` (non-owned instances / plumbing views; prop `entry`). TITLE renders inline text, a node CONTENT renders through `NodeContentViewer`, plumbing slots with a hint (LABELS_PATH, the navigation PATH_VIEW template's INTERACTION_TREE) stay compact InfoChips — every other bound reference blooms into the kind's full Mini via `shared/ElementMini` |
| `skeletons/SkeletonMini.vue` | The skeleton member of the Mini family (`MiniPanel` chrome, NodeMini-sized). Summarizes a schema or populated instance as a KEY → value table: bound NOTE text inline (2-line clamp), other bound refs as dense InfoChips, unbound fields as their ACCEPTS hint / `(empty)`; populated rows sort first, footer counts `bound/total fields populated`. Self-resolving via `id` or `skeletons/<hash>` address (one `GET /skeletons/:id/walk`); header shows the SCHEMA badge or `instance of <schema>` chip. Dispatched by `shared/ElementMini` for every non-POST skeleton ref — so `[[pathos:skeletons/…]]` chips on post bodies bloom into it. |
| `dashboard/SessionActivityCard.vue` | **The session projection card** (dashboards phase 3, 2026-08-10) — the default USER_HOME dashboard's one VIRTUAL cell (`{"virtual":"session-activity"}` in LAYOUT): renders `GET /api/identity/sessions` rows (Session \| Device \| IP — login/last-seen, one-word device off the user-agent w/ the full string on the tooltip, ip) in **SkeletonTable's chrome** — the same `--skel-table-*` dials consumed, so a host that re-tones one re-tones both (chrome restated, not the component: a session row is not Field\|Type\|Data). Owner-only + sid-less by construction (the endpoint answers for the caller's login only); a PROJECTION deliberately not a skeleton — the receipts-later path (lazy SESSION_EVENT) is written in the plan. The `current` session marks with the teal inset bar + "this device" pill. |
| `skeletons/ResourceSkeletonMini.vue` | ⭐ **The EMBEDDABLE MINI TABLE** (dashboards phase 2, 2026-08-10) — one skeleton readable inside another: `MiniPanel` chrome (`bodyFit` — a data table's size is its meaning) around a `SkeletonTable` body, in exchange for its OWN scroll contract (`--resource-mini-max-h`, published by the surface; silence = uncapped). Dispatched by `ElementMini` for every non-POST skeleton ref, so `![[pathos:skeletons/…]]` blooms into it (GITHUB_PR instances resolve through the same walk and render their native card BARE — the chrome would double-frame a card). **RECURSION** (ClaimTopologyNode precedent): a cell whose ref is a skeleton nests another ResourceSkeletonMini; `visited` (ancestor REF STRINGS, checked before any fetch — an A↔B cycle renders an inert dense chip and costs zero requests past the first table) + `depth` (auto-nest budget 1: the top table expands its skeleton cells; deeper cells render an InfoChip leaf + `unfold_more` click-to-expand, so depth is user-driven past the first level). The panel is deliberately NOT a router-link (nested panels inside a linked panel would nest anchors — the OrgLogoChip lesson); the head carries an explicit `open_in_full` glyph-link instead. Own-head row: kind icon + name + SCHEMA badge / instance-of + hash + open. Re-toning: NodeMini's dial pattern — `--resource-mini-coat/-rule/-rule-hover/-head-ink` repaint the chrome; the table's own `--skel-table-*` dials pass through untouched. Non-skeleton cells restate SkeletonTable's default cell (text / dense chip / unbound) — a scoped slot replaces the whole default, keep the two in step. **PHASE 6**: computes `editable` (walk's `is_resource` && `!locked` && owner === `auth.entityId` — the server re-checks all three on write: 40300/40303/40010), passes it down, owns the `edit` commit (`setSlot` → refresh, or the `changed` emit when pre-walked so the grid reloads its batch), flashes constraint refusals inline, and the head grows the **lock toggle** (owner-only on resources; `lock`/`lock_open` glyph → `POST /skeletons/:id/lock\|unlock` — coral when locked). |
| `skeletons/SkeletonTable.vue` | ⭐ **The DENSE TABLE — one skeleton as a real data table** (dashboards phase 1, 2026-08-10): a `<table>` (`table-layout: fixed`) with columns Field \| Type \| Data at SkeletonInstanceEditor's 26/16/58 (the editor is its read-write sibling), drawn in the FLYOUT GREY FAMILY — plaque `--grey-3`, rules `--grey-4`/`--grey-5`, ink `--brown-8`/`--brown-4`, teal ONLY on hover (a 2px `--teal-12` inset bar on the first cell — the NodeMini-in-flyout precedent). Tones are SIX DIALS (`--skel-table-coat/-rule/-rule-strong/-ink/-ink-mute/-hover`, NodeMini's dial pattern at the dock's six-dial naming) so hosts re-tone without a repainting modifier. Type column = `expectedKind` pill in `kindFor(kind).color` (mute-ink 'any' when unconstrained), shown for BOTH bound and unbound rows — a table states its column types where the compact Mini only hints them on empties. Data column = `textValue` inline (pre-wrap, no clamp — the walk's 2000-char cap bounds it; the HOST owns the ceiling), other refs as dense InfoChips, unbound in italic mute. Declaration order preserved (a table reads as the schema's shape; the Mini re-sorts bound-first). Two mounting modes: pre-walked (`skeleton` + `slots` — the dashboard grid batch-walks once and deals results out) or self-resolving `refOrId` (id or address → one walk, `resolved` emitted so hosts title themselves). GITHUB_PR instances bloom into GithubPrCard exactly as in SkeletonMini. A `#data` scoped slot lets phase-2+ hosts replace the default cell rendering per row. Mounted at three scales: SkeletonFlyout's generic face (max detail), ResourceSkeletonMini's body (phase 2), dashboard grid cells (phase 4). **PHASE 6 — the mutation surface** (`editable` prop; the HOST decides — RESOURCE + unlocked + owner — and owns the `setSlot` on the `edit` emit): textable cells go tap-to-edit (inline input in the grey family; unit cells get a numeric input + STEP steppers with float-dust trimming), the Type column wears the **unit symbol chip** beside the kind pill (`slot.unit {label, symbol, step}` off the walk), and every row grows the **history glyph** — a popover loading `GET /skeletons/:id/history?slot=` on open (actor · value · moment, superseded rows dimmed). ⚠ The `#data` slot replaces the whole default cell, so the slot SCOPE carries `canEdit` + `beginEdit` — slot content compiles in the HOST's scope (RSM restates the tap affordance on its own spans; the table's editor row itself renders outside the slot). |
| `shared/HashLink.vue` | Inline routed hash link — icon + truncated hash + open-in-new arrow; routes to the kind's viewer page via `utils/kinds.js` when an id is known. Fallback chip in the file tree. |
| `shared/SkeletonRefLinks.vue` | Mono nav links for a skeleton id: `sk #N` (structure view) and, **only when `post` is true**, `post/N` (content view). Posts ARE skeletons — the two links are two views of the same id — but no post exists for other schemas' instances, so callers listing arbitrary skeletons pass `:post="row.name === 'POST'"` (default `true` fits the post-only surfaces: comments, forks, feed). |

### Micro + Mini component family

The canonical reusable preview surface for pathchain elements. One `XMicro` (inline chip) and one `XMini` (mini card) per kind, all built on shared primitives. Adding a new viewer kind = one Micro + one Mini wrapper, not new chrome.

| Component | Role |
|-----------|------|
| `utils/kinds.js` | ⭐ Single source of truth: on-disk prefix → `{ kind, icon, color, route(id) }`. Also `parseAddress`, `hashOf`, `shortHash`, `isHash`, and `parseRef` (decoded-field → navigable reference). `HashLink`, `AddressChain`, `MicroChip`, `RefChip` all import from here. |
| `shared/MicroChip.vue` | ⭐ Generic `icon / type / hash` chip. Container-adaptive width; routes to the kind's page when given an id. A linked chip IS an anchor, so **`.is-link:hover` forces `text-decoration: none !important`** (2026-07-26): inside rendered prose the surface's own `a:hover { text-decoration: underline }` outscored the `none` on `.micro-chip` and struck a line through `node / a1b2c3…`. The box is the affordance; see `MiniPanel` for why this is `!important` and not a specificity fight. **`linked` prop** (2026-07-26, default `true`): pass `false` and the chip renders as a `<span>` even when the kind has a viewer route — for a chip that TRIGGERS rather than navigates. It keeps its icon, hash and address tooltip; restate `cursor`/`:hover` at the call site, since `.is-link` no longer applies. (The feed card's foot chip was that pattern's live user until 2026-08-10 sent it back to being a link.) **`typeLabel` + `typeIcon` + `sep` props** (2026-08-10, all three defaulting to the previous rendering): `typeLabel` replaces the WORD between the separators — the kind's own slug says what the element IS, and a chip standing for a different READING of it says so instead (the feed foot's chip stands for `skeleton` over a `posts` kind, because a post's address is a handle on its skeleton); `typeIcon` DRAWS that word instead of spelling it and wins over `typeLabel` when both are given, for a chip sitting in a strip whose every other mark is a glyph (the feed foot passes the cap's `sym_o_orthopedics`, so it reads `[post] :: [skeleton] :: <hash>`); `sep` replaces the `/` on both sides, for a chip inside a strip with its own punctuation (the feed foot passes the cap's `::`). None of them touches the hash, the route or the tooltip — the chip still addresses exactly what it addressed, and the reading the glyph names stays on the tooltip. |
| `shared/MiniPanel.vue` | ⭐ Slot-based mini-card chrome (`#title`, `#chips`, `#labels`, `#hash`, `#body`, `#foot`). Every Mini uses it. **A panel is never underlined** (2026-07-26): `.mini-panel-link:hover { text-decoration: none !important }`. A Mini quoted into a post body IS an anchor inside rendered prose, and every prose surface underlines its links on hover (`.md-rendered a:hover` in `css/_components.scss`, `.post-square__md :deep(.markdown-body) a:hover` in FeedStream, `.post-md-rendered` in PostViewerPage) — the decoration is drawn by the anchor and propagates down its whole box tree, so hovering a panel struck a line under every run of text in it at once: title, excerpt, chip, counts. `!important` rather than a stronger selector because those rules are SCOPED (four classes + an element) — out-selecting them means beating the highest-scoring surface that exists today and losing again to the next one written, and the rule is not a preference to weigh against theirs: prose styles inline links, and this is a BLOCK quoted into prose. Panels state hover with the border, the shadow and the 1px lift. Two escape hatches (2026-07-26), both `NodeMini`'s: **`#head`** replaces the default zone STACK outright for a Mini that composes its own header ROW, and **`bodyFit`** lifts the body's `max-height: 110px` scroller. That cap is an EXCERPT rule — "a Mini quotes a few lines and you open it for the rest" — and it is wrong when the preview IS the content: it was cropping a 480×270 player to its top 110px, play button and all. **The media budget** rides on that: a medium quoted into a post must show WHOLE, and the size that means is the largest one fitting the window the post is read in, which the Mini cannot know (same panel in a 60vh-capped feed card and in a full-height post viewer). So the SURFACE publishes **`--media-max-h`** and the components size themselves from it — `NodeMini` as a `max-height` on the `img`/`video`, `EmbedFrame` converted into the width cap its ratio-locked box needs. Publishers: `.post-square__pit` (`max(120px, calc(min(var(--post-square-max, 100cqw), 60vh) - 264px))`, off the CARD's ceiling — a flat `30vh` knew nothing about the width half of `min(width, 60vh)`) and `.post-md-rendered` (`max(200px, calc(100cqh - 136px))`, off a `container-type: size` on `.markdown-scroll`). Unset, the fallbacks are a 180px thumbnail and an uncapped frame. |
| `{entities,nodes,posts,paths,labels,moments,links,secrets}/XMicro.vue` | Thin per-kind wrappers over `MicroChip`. |
| `{entities,nodes,posts,paths,labels,moments,links,secrets}/XMini.vue` | Per-kind mini cards over `MiniPanel`. `LabelMini` is recursive (inlines the parent label, depth-capped at 8). **`NodeMini` is the odd one out** (2026-07-26): it is the panel that gets quoted INTO posts by a `![[pathos:nodes/…]]` block embed, so it had a colorway of its own to read as another element on both the feed's indigos and the viewer's white paper — a uniform `--teal-1` coat (no chrome/body tone split), `--teal-3` drawing EVERY line (1px box on a 2px base, no shadow) and `--teal-10` inking what the panel writes in its own zones. **⚠ THE 2026-08-23 REFIT (nine user asks, then six more the same day) TURNED THIS PANEL INSIDE OUT — read this clause before trusting any sentence after it.** **SECOND PASS, in order:** the link line and the address chip **swapped back** (chip to the header, link line to the foot), the chip landing **left of the integrity dot**; a NEW leftmost header section states the node's NAME — `[tiny node glyph] :: [nodeLabel]`, where `nodeLabel` is the title or else `node #1758 · URL` (id + `node.type.name`, and deliberately NOT `effectiveTitle`, which answers with the embed's PROVIDER first — the ask names that exact node `URL`, not `YouTube`); the panel's LINES went **`--grey-5`** (hover **`--grey-7`**, darkening where the teal lightened — a neutral panel has no chroma to answer the pointer with); the COAT reversed from transparent to the card's own **`--card-coat`** (see that token — transparent showed the `--grey-1` PIT, not the cream card, which is why it had to be painted); the panel now **BLEEDS to the pit's border** through the `--quoted-bleed-x` contract; the embed **centres** (it was flush left — measured 0px/65px — because `.embed-frame__box` caps its own width and a block box with `margin: 0` sits left); and the address chip's ink is **`--teal-10`**. **THIRD PASS, same day:** the chip and the title **swapped again** — the address chip LEADS the row now and the title follows it, which is the reading the panel settled on (what it IS, then what it is CALLED). The title section lost its 10px kind glyph and its `::` ("remove the icon and the '::'"): the glyph restated in a picture what the chip beside it says in words, and the separator existed only to hold the glyph off the text, so removing one removed the other's job — `kindFor` came out of the imports with them, nothing on this panel drawing the node kind as a picture any more. A **COPY button** joined the chip's zone (`.node-mini__copy`, a bare `content_copy` glyph at 60% ink, no box, brightening on hover) and hands over the **FULL hash**, not `chipHash`'s 10-digit cut — the short form is for reading and the whole one is what a `[[pathos:]]` ref or an API call needs; house feedback idiom (FeedStream's `copyAddress`, ElementFlyout's `copyPath`), the glyph flipping to a `--positive` check for 1600ms with a denied clipboard swallowed. And the **integrity dot moved into a ZONE of its own** (`.node-mini__zone--dot`), which is what puts hairlines on both sides of it: `.node-mini__zone`'s `& + &` rule fires only between ADJACENT zones, so a bare span in the middle of the row broke the chain and the flyout beyond it went unruled too. Every boundary is a hairline now — chip│title│dot│flyout, three of them, verified 1px `--grey-5` each with none on the leading zone; the `v-if` degrades correctly (no verdict ⇒ the flyout's previous sibling is the title zone and it keeps its rule). Finally the title was **CENTRED in the slack it absorbs and set in NASALIZATION** (`--font-display`, the face MiniPanel's own `#title` slot and every parked dock tab wear). It is the elastic zone, so it owned every spare pixel in the row and sat hard against the chip's hairline with all of that space trailing it; `justify-content: center` on the zone plus `text-align: center` on the run centres both the box and the text inside it once the box is squeezed narrower than its content (measured 143px/142px). The face is set as `font-family` rather than by adding the `.nasalization` utility class, because that utility also carries `letter-spacing: 0.05em` and at 0.76em that tracking costs about a character of the ellipsis on a narrow card. **FIRST PASS:** The panel gave up its COAT (transparent now: "make the node viewer's background color the same as the post card's background" — not a copy of the card's tone but NO tone, because `.post-square` is a `--light-cream` sheet under a 75% `--grey-3` `::before` and the composite ~rgb(242,239,235) is a number neither file states; a transparent panel is showing the actual card and cannot drift when the card is repainted, which it has been three times this month), its RADIUS (`border-radius: 0`), its heavy BASE (all four borders 1px — "make all its borders the same thinness"; **⚠ AND THEN THE SIDE BORDERS CAME OFF ENTIRELY** (a further ask the same day: "remove the borders from the right and left of the mini node viewer") — `border-left-width` / `border-right-width: 0`, so the panel is bounded TOP AND BOTTOM only, at `--grey-5`. It is the finish of the bleed: once the panel reaches the pit's padding edge its own side lines sit ONE PIXEL inside `.post-square__pit`'s border, two hairlines of the same tone a pixel apart running the panel's full height, which reads as a doubled edge rather than as two boxes. The container's line already states where the content column ends. Written as WIDTH rather than `border-style: none` because both this component's hover rule and MiniPanel's set `border-color` on the panel, and a coloured zero-width border stays invisible where a re-declared style might not. The head/body/foot dividers are untouched — they are `& > * + *` `border-top`s INSIDE the panel, not part of its box (measured `1px / 0px / 1px / 0px` on the box, `0 / 1px / 1px` down the children). ), its ROUND PILL and the whole left header section with it, and its VOTES. The address chip went DOWN to a foot that came back for it, the embed's link line came UP into the header in the same swap, the body lost its side padding (content meets the border) and centres what it shows (text justifies), and every padding on the panel was cut roughly in half or to zero. Header text is `nowrap` + ellipsis end to end — measured at 375px, the row holds ONE 17px line and the elastic run takes the cut. **What SURVIVES both passes**: the `--teal-10` zone ink (which GAINED the address chip), the four dials, `raw`/`body-fit`/`--node-source-max-h`, and the integrity dot. The `--teal-3` line system and its `--teal-11` hover did not. **The panel was HEADER + BODY from 2026-07-27 to 2026-08-22; it is HEADER + BODY + FOOT again since 2026-08-23.** Its header is ONE ROW split by full-height vertical hairlines (padding on the zones so the rules touch both edges), and the row reads **`address chip + copy │ title │ dot │ flyout`** since the 2026-08-23 third pass — four zones, three hairlines (**`name │ address chip │ dot · open`** in the second pass, **`link line │ open`** briefly, **`round pill │ address chip │ open`** from 2026-07-27 until that day): (1) the **ROUND PILL** (`.node-mini__round-pill`) held the left end from 2026-07-27 and is **DELETED, with its whole zone, on 2026-08-23** ("remove the button from the left section on the header — remove the section"). It was the old foot pill's material closed into a fixed 18px CIRCLE around the kind glyph alone: `--grey-3` fill, a 1px `--grey-5` ring OUTSIDE the box + a 1px `--grey-5` seam inside its bottom edge (both box-shadows — the one border is spent on the base, and a shadow follows `border-radius` where an outline may not), a **2px `--teal-12` base** curving along the bottom arc (the pill's own device — its 999px ends did the same), `--grey-8` ink with the hairline `-webkit-text-stroke: 0.35px`. **THE MATERIAL OUTLIVED THE ELEMENT BY ONE DAY**: the PIN TACK took it whole on 2026-08-22 with only its base swapped to `--red-13`, so `NavigationBar`'s and `PinsDrawer`'s `.tack-btn` are where that object exists now — copy it from there, and note the `tack-skin` witness reads those two rules as its definition (it is what CAUGHT this deletion, failing with `rule not found: NodeMini.vue (the source pill)` the moment the element came out); (2) the **ADDRESS CHIP** stood where the title text stood from 2026-07-27, and went back DOWN TO THE FOOT on 2026-08-23 ("switch the node nano chip on the header with the link display section at the bottom") — a plain `NodeMicro` in MicroChip's own STOCK skin (the exact chip the feed card's foot wears: `rgba(ink)` fill, 4px radius, mono 0.72em), `icon / node / hash` with the hash cut to 10 digits + `…` (**`chipHash`**, renamed from `footHash`; full path on the chip's tooltip). It came straight back up in the SECOND pass and now stands **left of the integrity dot** (`.node-mini__zone--chip`, `flex: 0 1 auto` — squeezable, never stretched, the slack belonging to the name), its ink overridden to **`--teal-10`**. The **LINK LINE** (`.node-mini__foot-link`) had the header in between and is the FOOT's occupant now — glyph · provider · address, the three runs `EmbedFrame`'s figcaption used to print UNDER the frame, resolved by a `linkLine` computed with two cases: an embeddable link gives its rule's icon + provider, a plain `http(s)` url gives `link` + its host, and anything else resolves to `null`. That null is load-bearing: the foot is a **conditional SLOT** (`<template v-if="linkLine" #foot>`), because MiniPanel renders its `<footer>` on `$slots.foot` merely being PRESENT — a slot passed and left empty draws an empty band under a divider. Verified on `/labels/9`: `URL` nodes carry a foot, `NOTE` and `FILE` nodes have none. The address is the elastic run and the first thing the ellipsis eats; `linkTitle` carries the whole thing on the tooltip. **The header's leftmost section** is the node's NAME (`.node-mini__zone--name`): a 10px kind glyph, a literal dimmed `::` that never shrinks (a separator that can vanish stops separating), and `nodeLabel` — the heavily restyled Nasalization PILL and all its `!important` MicroChip overrides died with the foot; the human title survives on the title ZONE's tooltip (`effectiveTitle` — post title > embed provider > `Node #id`), and the `raw` mode mono-on-grey-1 title treatment is gone with the text (raw still governs the BODY: source pane, `body-fit`, `--node-source-max-h`); (3) the open glyph, `13px → 10px` with the density ask (`--coral-deep` on hover). **The VOTES are GONE (2026-08-23, "remove the voting section")** — and with them `.vote-flat` and NodeMini's use of `EmbedFrame`'s `#cap-end` slot, which had no other caller. From 2026-07-27 they rode the embed's caption line as a bare glyph + count in caption-scale `--grey-8`, the viewer's OWN vote keeping its semantic green/red in the ink alone; they went out with that caption, which is the panel's HEADER now, and were not carried up with it — a preview states what the node IS, which is also why the comment/fork tallies came off with the band. **THE FOOT IS ONE THING, FULL WIDTH, CENTRED** — those band rules were written for the address chip on the first pass (`flex: 1 1 auto; width: 100%; justify-content: center`; MicroChip is built to smuggle into running text at `flex: 0 1 auto` with a 9ch floor, exactly wrong for the only thing in a band) and did not move when the second pass swapped the tenant for the link line: verified 554px occupant in a 554px foot. `GET /nodes/by-path` still feeds `votes` for whoever wants them; nothing reads `comment_count`/`fork_count` here anymore. **HOVER in the colorway** (2026-07-26): `:deep(.mini-panel.mini-panel--hover):hover` lights the whole LINE SYSTEM — outer border, MiniPanel's head/body divider, the header's zone splits — to `--teal-11` via two custom properties (`--panel-rule` MiniPanel's, `--node-rule` NodeMini's own; `border-color` restated because MiniPanel's hover sets the property directly; both class names on the selector because a one-class `:deep()` only ties). (The round pill's ring and base used to stay put through hover — they bounded an object rather than dividing the panel; moot since the pill left.) Styling the panel takes BARE `:deep()` (`MiniPanel` sets `inheritAttrs: false`, so a class on the component is dropped while the scope id still lands). **FOUR dials** (down from five — `--node-mini-foot-glyph` died with the foot), the `--frieze-bar-base` seam pattern — ⚠ **all four currently have NO CALLER**: `SkeletonFlyout` was the only one and did not survive the 2026-08-17 flyout fusion, so `grep` finds these set nowhere in `dashboard/src`. They are a live seam with no consumer, not dead code, and the defaults below are what every panel actually renders: `--node-mini-coat` (both faces of the coat — **defaulting to `transparent` since 2026-08-23**, so a host that wants a tinted node panel back sets this one property), `--node-mini-rule` (the whole line system in one value), `--node-mini-rule-hover` (its mandatory partner — a half-dialled panel flashes MINT), `--node-mini-head-ink` (the header row's zone ink, which since the chip and pill are their own neutral objects lands mostly on the open glyph). What is NOT dialled: the source pane's `--teal-10`, which is what keeps saying NODE after coat, lines and head ink have gone host-toned. **No WEIGHT dial**, deliberately — `--node-mini-rule-w` existed for part of 2026-07-27 and came out: every line is 1px because at Mini scale that is a hairline; a host that finds them faint has a TONE problem. (One note if wanted back: MiniPanel hard-codes 1px on border + dividers, so a width dial must be restated over both. The second note — that the heavy 2px base would have to become `calc(w * 2)` — died with the base on 2026-08-23.) `raw` reads the TEXT body as SOURCE instead of excerpt — whole `bodyOf(node)`, every marker intact, `pre-wrap`, monospace at 1.62 on a `--grey-1` floor — opting out of MiniPanel's 110px cap (`body-fit`) and scrolling ITSELF at `--node-source-max-h` (published by the surface, the `--media-max-h` contract). |
| `moments/MomentWorldMap.vue` | The **cute tiny world** (2026-07-19): dotted equirectangular land grid (generated `moments/worldDots.js` — 3° cells rasterized from public-domain country polygons) as an inline SVG, a gold pulsing pin projected at the moment's lat/lon, and a caption reading **city, Country** (bold city) from `human.city`/`human.country`, falling back to `place`. Rendered by `MomentViewerPage` beside the facts block whenever the moment has coordinates. |
| `shared/ElementMini.vue` | ⭐ The "any element as a Mini" dispatcher — self-resolves a `<kind>/<hash>` address (or takes a pre-resolved link target) into the matching Mini: Node/Path/Post/Label/Entity/Moment/Link/Skeleton. Skeleton refs split on the summary route: POST instances render `PostMini`, every other skeleton (schema or populated instance) renders `skeletons/ResourceSkeletonMini` — the embeddable mini TABLE (2026-08-10, dashboards phase 2; `SkeletonMini` before) — with the recursion guards `depth`/`visited` threaded through as props (default 0/[]). Secrets and unresolvable refs degrade to an `InfoChip`; refs the viewer can't read (403 + `{locked}` summary — access doctrine) render `LockedChip`. **The skeletons case checks `summary.locked` FIRST** (2026-07-31): the locked stub CARRIES `id`/`primary` by doctrine (the hash stays visible), so the `id == null` guard passes and a private skeleton used to mount a `SkeletonMini` that 403'd on every field. `RefMicro` gained the same branch — a locked inline ref renders `LockedChip` instead of an inert micro chip. Used by post content rails, MarkdownBody's `mini` tier, pins, and both skeleton-instance field surfaces. Its 480px cap is a prose MEASURE and a figure is not prose, so a media node (embed / image / video, read off the shape it already resolved) gets `.element-mini--media` and the surface's full width instead. |
| `shared/LockedChip.vue` | Access doctrine's locked tier: the element's hashname in a micro-style chip with a **lock bubble** on its edge; clicking opens `RequestAccessPanel` in a q-menu. Rendered by `ElementMini` for unreadable refs; `InfoChip` has a matching inline `.is-locked` state (dashed border, lock bubble, no route, same panel). |
| `shared/RequestAccessPanel.vue` | The "Request permission" surface behind every locked chip AND the access-denied banner's prompt: optional message + request button → `accessService.request` (lands as a chat message + access poll on the owner's side). Since 2026-07-31 it opens with **Talavero's banner strip** (`.req-access__talavero` — the landscape shot from `assets/access-octopus-gray.png` cropped to his half): the platform's guardian appears at chip scale wherever an element is locked, matching the full-page `AccessDeniedBanner`. |
| `claims/ClaimBand.vue` | **The claim reader band** (Thread D reader surface, 2026-07-31), mounted by `SkeletonPage` above a CLAIM instance's structure view: STATUS pill + live endorse/dispute tally + `ProvenanceBadge` on the claim's spine (`paths/<hash>`) in the head; statement through `MarkdownBody` (`info` tier); confidence line; the evidence tree; stance controls — endorse one-click, dispute opens the mandatory-ref form (`:dark="false"` on its inputs — light panel under global Quasar dark), retract author-only behind a `$q.dialog` confirm. Votes rebind STATUS server-side, so `@changed` → SkeletonPage `reloadSlots`. |
| `claims/ClaimEvidenceNode.vue` | One recursive row of the evidence tree: an `InfoChip` per evidence ref; a ref whose summary carries `claim` fetches that claim and nests its evidence beneath (depth-capped at 3 — a self-citing claim or long dispute chain can't run away). Private evidence = locked chip via the summary endpoint. |
| `claims/ClaimTopology.vue` + `claims/ClaimTopologyNode.vue` | **The Disagreement band** (Thread D stage 2, 2026-07-31), mounted by `ClaimBand` under the evidence tree and re-keyed on every vote/retract (`topoKey`). ONE read — `GET /api/claims/:id/topology` — feeds the whole band; recursion never refetches (the graph rides down as a prop). Renders nothing when the claim has no edges. Three directions: *disputed by* rows recurse label-tree-style (`ClaimTopologyNode` — an attacker expands with its own disputers/supporters, `MAX_DEPTH` 3, visited-set against cycles); *this claim stands against* and *cited as support by* are flat reverse-navigation rows. Every row wears the edge's provenance: voter `InfoChip`, a dotted `comment` link to the carrying comment, relative time. Locked nodes render as `InfoChip` locked stubs and never expand; a dispute standing on a non-claim element keeps its row with the evidence's chip. Status pills reuse the shared claim palette. |
| Claim STATUS on chips | `refs/summary` answers CLAIM instances with `claim: { id, status }`; **`InfoChip`** renders a status **pill** (`.info-chip__status`), **`MicroChip`** a status **dot** (`claim-status` prop, fed by `RefMicro`'s resolved summary; word rides the tooltip). One palette everywhere: open `#5b6c82` / supported `#2e6a3a` / disputed `#a03d3d` / retracted `#8995a8` struck-through. |
| `shared/AccessDeniedBanner.vue` | The main viewers' full-page access-denied surface: when a direct read 403s (code 40310), the node/path/post/link/skeleton pages render it instead of the element grid (and instead of the spinner hanging). A subject-panel-family **frame** (white body, chrome tokens) sized to the viewport (`calc(100vh - 16px - --nav-footer-h)`; SkeletonPage overrides `-32px` for its `q-pa-md`) so it never scrolls: a very thin chrome header with the item's kind icon (`kindFor`) + full hashname + lock, then the picture banner inset on an 8px chrome pad. Desktop picture: the porcelain-octopus landscape (`assets/access-octopus-gray.png`) full-bleed, message + the cloth-textured **Request Access** button on its slate field (`#2e3c45`; SVG `feTurbulence` grain over the taupe felt the octopus holds). Under 768px the portrait shot (`access-octopus-mobile.png`, same slate) takes over — text above him, button below him. The button opens the shared `RequestAccessPanel` in a q-menu below it — the requester is prompted for a message to the owner before sending (owner chat + access poll) — then flips to a "request sent" confirmation. On mount the banner probes `accessService.status(address)`: `requested: true` (the viewer's poll is still pending server-side) replaces the button with an "access already requested" line, so reloads can't spam the owner. `address` comes from the 403 envelope's `error.element` locked stub, parsed by `utils/access.js#lockedInfoFromError` in each viewer's load catch. |
| `maker/AccessTreeDialog.vue` | The pre-publish **access tree** (persistent q-dialog): root row = the post itself, one row per `[[pathos:]]` ref from `POST /api/access/tree` (recursed through referenced posts). World-toggles on owned gated branches; flipping the base flips every branch. Confirm emits `{ public, publish[] }` → `PostMakerSurface.doPost` sends it as the post's `access` payload. Comments skip the dialog (they inherit the parent's audience server-side). `mode="share"` (the chat composer): the audience is the conversation's members — no root toggle, branch picks become `shareRefs`. |
| `chat/ChatDock.vue` | The **7th docked window** (windows.js key `chat`; teal `forum` button on the nav bar's right cluster; minimizes to a `chats` minitab). **THE CREATION FOOTPRINT since 2026-08-03** (user ask): it carries `.dock-window--creation` + `.dock-window--chat`, so its geometry, maximize (`is-max`, renamed from `is-maximized`), shadow and mobile chrome are the maker's to the pixel — see the MakerDock row. Three things had to GO from the scoped block rather than be overridden: `width: 74vw`, `height: 72vh` and the `.is-maximized` / mobile width pairs — a scoped `.chat-dock[data-v-…]` selector outranks the global `.dock-window--creation`, so a leftover width would have quietly kept the old footprint. It anchors straight to the bar's top edge (`bottom: --nav-footer-h`) since 2026-08-03 — the user's words: "we do not want to have the chat float on top of the footer frieze bar"; it had stood ON the frieze footer band via `--dock-bottom` before, and that band (with the token) was retired the next day. **The LIME colorway** (hue-swapped from green 2026-08-05, "even clearer tones") is its own identity: `.dock-window--chat` points the creation chrome's six dials at the `--lime-*` family (`_tokens.scss` § LIME — the brown roles one hue over: -1 coat, -2 wells, -3 lines, -4 heavy line, -10 ink — the ink sits at Material 900, not the sibling colorways' 700, since lime-8 only clears ~2.2:1 on white), and the coat is OPAQUE `--lime-1` where the shell used to be `--surface-glass-strong` over a 12px `backdrop-filter` — the platform's last translucent dock. ⚠ **THE COAT LEFT THIS COLORWAY on 2026-08-26** (user ask): `.dock-window--chat` no longer declares `--dock-coat` and inherits the bar's `--plaque-coat`, so the header row and the MESSAGE BED (never painted — it lies on the shell's coat) are cream, while the conversations column stays a `--lime-2` well in `--lime-3` rims. Still fully opaque: the veil is a background layer, not a filter. A **FriezeBar** sits between the header and the body (`.chat-dock__frieze`, `flex: 0 0 auto` or the flex column squashes it) — since 2026-08-05 it runs **`slim`** (one SVG layer, half height, the media viewer's recipe), dials base `--lime-10` and wave two `--brown-1` (no wave-one dial; slim never renders that layer). Interior: the conversations column is a `--lime-2` well in the `--lime-1` coat (the COLUMN kept its `--lime-2` well when the shell went cream on 2026-08-26), rows are `--lime-1` cards on it with the ACTIVE one going to PAPER + a `--lime-4` rim (the dock tab strip's "current = the lightest thing here" device), the thread lies on the coat itself and `MessageBubble` carries the tone difference (theirs paper on `--lime-3`, MINE `--lime-2` on `--lime-4` — the teal `rgba(#00829c,.09)` my side wore was the last translucency in here). **Vertical cap (2026-08-05)**: open (not maximized) the window pins to the bottom of the creation docks' shared frieze-to-navbar span and caps its own height at `min(60vh, <that span>)`; the green traffic light's maximize reclaims the full span (same height every other creation dock always has) and squares off the top-right corner the cap left rounded (`.dock-window--chat.is-max`) — the nav bar's footer stays visible under the window either way, since `bottom: --nav-footer-h` never moves. Left: conversations (latest activity, last-message preview) + new-conversation entity search (`refs/search` kind=entities). **A row is the feed card's IDENTITY BLOCK** (2026-08-03, user ask: "just like the author bubbles on the feed post cards … the image and org badges in a dense style"): `EntityAvatar` at 26px, name over `@handle`, `OrgLogoChip` at 15px pulled in −2px, a `+n` chip for a group's remaining seats, on the byline's own 5px gap / 1.15 leading — fed by the member cards' new `photo` + `org` (see api-reference § chats). The chip is mounted **`:link="false"`**: the row is a `<button>` and the chip's default router-link would nest interactive content inside it (invalid, and the anchor swallows the row's tap). An agent seat has no handle, so that line is dropped rather than left empty. **On a phone the column has two presentations of ONE element** (the stack/pins device, same argument — separate elements drift apart): `is-faces` (mobile ∧ not expanded) narrows the grid track to **46px** and hides everything in a row but its face, the org badge surviving as a corner dot; the head's one control flips `is-list-open`, which lifts the same column to `position: absolute; inset: 0` over the conversation at full width so the search field and the names are usable. Picking a conversation closes the overlay; leaving the phone drops it. Keep the 46px in step with `.chat-row`'s padding and the avatar's `:size`. Main: classic chat + composer (Enter sends; refs open the share tree). **LIVE since 2026-07-29 (Thread A)**: the dock watches `stores/events.js#lastEvent` — a pushed `chat.message`/`poll.*`/`org.invite` for the OPEN conversation reloads its feed within the second (measured 80 ms end to end) and acks that event targeted (seen where it lives); events for other conversations refresh the preview list. The old 15 s poll became a **60 s dropped-stream fallback**. Consent gate (2026-07): pending seats get an accept/decline banner (replying also accepts), conversation rows carry `invitation`/`declined` badges, and the composer locks with a hint while the other side is pending (after your one opener) or declined — server codes 40311/40312/40313 surface under the composer. Feed renders `org_invite` items as `organizations/OrgInviteCard.vue` (PollCard pattern: Accept — put on the mask / Decline for the invitee while PENDING, status chip otherwise). **Agent availability band (2026-07-31)**: chat members that are rostered agents carry an `agent` card (quota bundle from the AGENT_PROFILE skeleton — `free_tier`, `available`, `resets_at`); when the open conversation's agent is a free-tier one with an empty pool, a coral `.chat-dock__agent-away` band under the header shows "*scout is not available — free-tier quota resets in 2h 59m*", with the countdown re-derived from `resets_at` every 30 s client-side. Availability is read from stored state — the UI never spends a provider request to ask. **Delivery surfaces (Thread G, 2026-07-31)**: a quiet `.chat-dock__seen` line ("seen" / group-aware "seen by …", hover = when) sits under the newest of YOUR messages the other members' watermarks (`members[].last_read_link_id` vs `items[].link_id`, both from the feed) cover; a green `.chat-dock__presence` dot beside the conversation header's names = the other side holds a live SSE stream right now (`members[].online`). Silent `chat.read` events (born read — no ack, no badge) reload the open conversation's feed so the seen line moves live. **The channel contract (Thread K, 2026-07-31)**: the conversation header's `policy` button toggles `ChatContractBand` under it. |
| `chat/ChatContractBand.vue` | **The channel contract** (Thread K, 2026-07-31): the live who-sees-what panel over `GET /api/chats/:id/contract` — seats with consent pills (accepted/pending/declined tones), then one row per element the conversation put on the table (each `[[pathos:]]` ref in a message + each poll TARGET): kind icon + routed title, globe when public, first-shared moment, a `history` chip to the on-chain ACCESS path viewer, and the audience as eye / crossed-eye per member (tooltip: granted by whom · when — from the decorated ledger). Derived from recorded state only, so it cannot claim an audience the chain contradicts; reloads on the dock's `feedTick` (every successful feed read), which covers fresh shares and poll grants without its own event plumbing. **Closes with the Attention receipts section (Thread G stage 2, 2026-07-31)**: your consent `q-toggle` (GET/POST `/chats/:id/attention`), per-seat consent pills, and — ONLY while every seat consents — one row per table ref with attended/not-attended eyes per member (tooltip: visit count + last time; quiet grey for not-yet-attended, not the audience red — "hasn't looked" is not a denial); otherwise the consent hint (symmetric, per-conversation, revocable, never retroactive). |
| `stores/events.js` + `services/event.service.js` | **The push spine's client end** (Thread A, 2026-07-29, `docs/concepts/events.md`): ONE `EventSource` for the whole app, connected from MainLayout onMounted after login. Stream URL = `api.defaults.baseURL + /events/stream?t=<1h token from GET /events/token>`; reconnection is the STORE's (close → wait 4 s → fresh token → reconnect with `lastEventId` replay cursor) because the browser's automatic retry would replay an expired `?t=` forever. Signals: `unreadCount` — worn by the **NavigationBar chat button** as a tiny amber floating badge (`.chat-unread-badge`, the pin tack counter's exact treatment); opening the dock acks the whole queue (coarse v1 — every current event kind lands in a chat surface or the polls band one click away) — and `lastEvent`, the most recent pushed event (a signal, not a log). |
| Feed **trust surfaces** (Thread J, 2026-07-29, `docs/concepts/trust.md`) | On `FeedStream.vue`: the byline's **`.post-square__trust`** chip after the identity block/org badge — invite-chain distance as a tiny `--grey-1`/`--indigo-3` plate ("2 hops"; own posts read "you"), tooltip walking the whole vouch path ("Invite chain: you › allegue › Dream Operator's Garage › …") from `item.author.trust` — and the head's own **hop lens** (in the band until 2026-08-06, in the head BOX's second half through its `controls` slot since; a `.feed-stream__lens` segmented control — all / ≤1 / ≤2 / ≤3 — until **2026-08-07**, when it folded into the single `.feed-stream__hops` DROPDOWN: `connect_without_contact` + the radius as its `.feed-stream__lens-n` mark, `∞` for the open feed, the four options now words in a `q-menu`). Either face is a `--grey-1` plate in `--indigo-3` lines whose ON state inverts to a `--indigo-8` floor; it reloads the stream with `?maxHops=` and is session-local on purpose (a lens is looked through, not saved). |
| `chat/MessageBubble.vue` | One message: others left, yours right; body via `MarkdownBody` `mini` tier so refs render as Minis and locked refs as lock-bubbled hash chips. THE message viewer — messages read here first. **In the chat window's lime colorway (green until 2026-08-05)**: theirs is paper on a `--lime-3` rim (author name in `--lime-10`), MINE is the well tone `--lime-2` on the heavier `--lime-4` — so "who said it" is stated by how lime the bubble is, on a bed that is the window's own `--lime-1` coat. Both fills are OPAQUE; the `rgba(#00829c, .09)` teal tint my side used to wear was the last translucency in the window, and a tint over a coloured coat is not the colour it was drawn as. |
| `chat/PollCard.vue` | A poll in the conversation, **kind-aware** (2026-07-19, `poll.kind` from the feed item with a `target_ref` fallback): access polls ("Access poll", `how_to_vote`, "Yes — grant access"), label suggestions ("Label suggestion", `new_label`, "Yes — add the label") and approval polls (2026-08-02, resident dev flow: "Approval", `fact_check`, "Yes — approve"; statuses approved/declined wear the green/red tints; never reversible). Markdown question with chips, status pill (pending/granted/denied + accepted/declined tints), Yes/No buttons only for the DECIDER (`viewerIds` vs `poll.decider_id`) while pending → `pollService.vote` → emits `voted`. `allow-reverse` prop (the profile's My-polls band sets it; chats don't): once a poll is decided the decider gets a "Reverse — revoke/grant access" button → `pollService.reverse` (granted ⇄ denied, access flips with it) — **access polls only**; label suggestions are one-shot (pruning the branch is the undo). |
| `entities/EntityAvatar.vue` | ⭐ **An entity's face**, as a ROUNDED-SQUARE tile (2026-07-25 — a disc for the first day, until the corners of the generated square compositions started disappearing into the circular crop; the radius is a PERCENTAGE, `26%`, not an em-based token, so a 34px feed chip and a 56px profile hero are exactly the same shape, and `EntityProfilePage`'s faceless `q-avatar` fallback restates it to outrank Quasar's own `border-radius: 50%`). The photo is not a column on the entity — it is the `nodes/<hash>` reference bound into that entity's `USER_PROFILE > PROFILE_PHOTO`, resolved server-side into `{ url, … }` — so the component takes whatever the caller already has (`:entity="item.author"` on a feed card carries the photo inline) and only falls back to the cached `entitySummary()` resolver when handed a bare id. Three states in order: **the photo** (`<img>`, `object-fit: cover`, hairline rim, `@error` falls through), **the pioneer's carved-gold star**, and a **monogram tile** whose hue is a deterministic hash of the entity id (golden-angle stepped, so neighbouring ids never collide) — a real fallback, not a placeholder: a faceless author still reads as a distinct person down a column of cards. A PRIVATE photo resolves with `url: null` and lands on the monogram too — there is a face, we are simply not allowed to see it. Mounted by `FeedStream`'s identity block (34px) and `EntityProfilePage`'s hero (56px, replacing the generic person/star `q-avatar` when a photo is bound). |
| `organizations/OrgLogoChip.vue` | ⭐ **The badge an author wears** (2026-07-25). Takes the `org` card the API hands out (`{ id, entity_id, name, handle, logo, role_title, self? }` — on `feed` items, `refs/summary` entity summaries and `GET /organizations`) and draws the organization's `ORG_PROFILE > LOGO` image at `:size` px (18 on a feed card) as a rounded square, linking to `/organizations/:id` with `@click.stop`. No logo, or a private one (`logo.url === null`)? A **monogram plaque** stands in, tinted by the same golden-angle hue walk `EntityAvatar` uses, so two orgs never wear the same fallback. `show-name` adds the org's name beside the mark for list surfaces; the feed card leaves it in the `title` tooltip (with the member's `role_title`), since the row is already carrying a name. The mark has a `box-shadow` hairline rather than a border so a pale badge still has an edge on a pale card. |
| `entities/EntityPolls.vue` | The profile's **"My polls" band** (own profile only — `EntityProfilePage` renders it `v-if="isSelf"`, between the grid and EntityContributions): `GET /api/polls/mine` → responsive grid of `PollCard`s, each cell headed by a role chip (**you decide** purple / **your request** gray) + the poll's moment. Decider cells pass `allow-reverse`, so pending polls get Yes/No and decided ones the reverse button; any change reloads the list. |

### File tree viewer

`/files` renders the on-disk pathchain (`files/<owner>/…`) as a recursive tree. Backed by `services/file.service.js` → `/api/files`.

| Component | Role |
|-----------|------|
| `pages/FileTreePage.vue` | ⭐ Page shell: loads the tree, lineage breadcrumb, and builds the intra-tree **nav controller** (see below). |
| `components/files/FileTreeNode.vue` | Recursive row. Folders expand inline; each leaf carries a Mini-preview dropdown + a decoded/`.proto` dropdown. Consumes the nav controller to force-open + flash a revealed element. |
| `components/files/DecodedContentInline.vue` | The decoded panel: a **draggable vertical `q-splitter`** — decoded JSON leads at 70%, the `.proto` schema trails at 30% (stacks on phones). |
| `components/files/JsonView.vue`, `JsonNode.vue` | Collapsible pretty-printed JSON. `JsonNode` renders any reference-address string as a `RefChip`. |
| `components/files/RefChip.vue` | ⭐ A `MicroChip` whose click **reveals the target inside the tree** (`treeNav.reveal`) instead of routing away. |
| `components/files/FileContractBadge.vue` | Contract verdict chip for FILE nodes (mounted in `NodeDetailPage`'s body-kind row). Lazily calls `GET /api/uploads/verify/…` via `services/upload.service.js` and renders **contract verified** (teal carved chip, `.pioneer-gold` relief pattern) or **contract violated** (red) — the on-disk sealed container's word on whether the bytes still match the node. Hidden on transport errors. |
| `utils/treeNav.js` | The nav controller factory. Indexes every leaf by canonical `<prefix>/<hash>`, force-opens ancestor folders, scrolls + flashes the target, and auto-expands its decoded panel. |

**Intra-tree navigation flow:** open a leaf's decoded dropdown → every `kind/hash` reference field (author, register, tag, head, prev/next/target, …) is a `RefChip` → click it → `FileTreePage`'s provided `treeNav` resolves the address in its index, force-opens the ancestor folders, scrolls the target row into view, flashes it, and auto-expands *its* decoded panel. Targets outside the loaded subtree (e.g. a cross-owner reference on `/files/:ownerHash`) fall back to `/files?reveal=<address>`, which resolves on the whole-platform view. Plain-content fields (`text`) and empty fields stay inert text — only `parseRef`-matching addresses become chips.

## CSS design system

Three layered files under `src/css/` plus generated `quasar.variables.scss`:

```
_tokens.scss      → CSS custom properties (--ink, --paper, --aqua-*, --coral, --radius-*, --shadow-*)
_mixins.scss      → composable SCSS mixins (@glass, @carved, @inset-font, @neon-bg-surface, @chip, @scrollbar-ink)
_utilities.scss   → drop-in utility classes (.bg-surface, .nasalization, .inset-font, .rounded-md, .glass-light, …)
_components.scss  → shared component classes (.pathos-card, .maker-overlay, .maker-dialog, .interaction-bar, .nav-btn, .md-rendered)
app.scss          → thin: imports + the starfield page canvas + body + display-face rules
quasar.variables.scss → exposes $primary / $secondary / $accent for Quasar's own components
```

Re-theming is a single-file edit to `_tokens.scss`. All other styles consume `var(--…)` references.

Page canvas (2026-07): the body paints `assets/backgrounds/starfield-noise.svg`
(a black 700×700 specular-noise tile) repeated + fixed, dimmed by a subtle
opaque grey-3 (`rgba(238,238,238,0.16)`) glass wash so the stars stay quiet;
the rule targets `body, body.body--dark` because Quasar's dark mode otherwise
buries it under `--q-dark-page`. The Nasalization display face ships LOCALLY
at `src/css/fonts/nasalization.otf` (it is **not** a Google font — the old
remote `@import` silently fell back) and is applied globally to `.q-btn` and
`.text-h1…h6` on top of the existing `.nasalization` utility.

It walked `6px --light-cream` at 50% → `1px --grey-2` at 80% → here, and the first step is a TONE lesson, not a width one: a border in the COAT's own tone is capped by however far the wash has moved the coat, while one off the card's axis states its own value, so one pixel of a free tone says more than six of a capped one. Note this makes one token do two jobs on the card — the reading surfaces and the line that lights its edge. NodeMini takes it as its `--node-mini-coat` default. ⚠ The `--plaque-coat` trap applies unchanged: a `background` shorthand value, **not** a `<color>` — illegal in `border-color`, `color` and gradient stops; the composite for those is ~`#f2efeb` | cream under a 75% grey wash |
| `--red-13` | **The 2px BASE under the PIN TACK** (2026-08-22, user ask: take "the rounded one with the node icon inside" from the top-left corner of the node viewer quoted in the post cards and "use it on the bottom right's pin button", "instead of teal-13, use red-13"). Quasar red-13 = accent A400 `#ff1744`; a NEW token, two hand-declared lines in `_tokens.scss` (`$red-13` + `--red-13`) like every Quasar mirror in this file. It exists because `.node-mini__round-pill`'s MATERIAL travelled to a second object and only its one coloured line changed with it — the tack wears the pill's `--grey-3` fill, `--grey-5` ring + seam and `--grey-8` hairline-stroked glyph unaltered, and stated that it was not a node by carrying THIS under it instead of the pill's `--teal-12`. ⚠ **The pill itself was deleted the NEXT DAY** (2026-08-23, the NodeMini refit), so the tack is not a copy of anything any more — it IS the material, and the `tack-skin` witness was re-keyed off the two tack rules and a table of constants because the source it had been reading no longer exists. Same accent step, same job: the one line on a small object allowed to be a COLOUR rather than a tone. -11/-12 are the pale reds that read as pink on a light coat, losing the base's whole point; -14 `#d50000` reads as a hairline drawn in ink rather than as a mark. It doubles as the PINNED state — the base closes into a full 2px ring and the pushpin takes it as ink — and hover deliberately never borrows it (the face lifts to `--grey-1`), which is --teal-12/-13's rule one object further on. Carried by BOTH tack copies (`NavigationBar` + `PinsDrawer`) and nothing else on the platform | red, accent A400 |
| `--neon-mid-blue` / `--neon-mid-pink` | Mid neon triad exposure — both now free: `--neon-mid-blue` was the removed stack button's lit state, `--neon-mid-pink` was the tack's pinned state (now the round pill's `--grey-3` face with its base closed into a full `--red-13` ring + pushpin — a flat grey-3 face with a `--grey-9` rim until 2026-08-22, brown-1/brown-8 until 2026-08-18) | `#94daff` / `#ff94da` |
| `--surface-1..4` | Carved card surfaces | translucent paper tints |
| `--aqua-1..4` | Accent — cool/cyan scale | mint → deep teal |
| `--coral` / `--coral-deep` | Accent — warm | for emphasis chips, active state |
| `--light-cream` (`#FCF3E0`) | **THIRD ROLE 2026-08-17** (four user asks in one session): **the SHEET of all fixed chrome** — the lower layer of `--plaque-coat`, under a 30% `--grey-3` veil, worn by the nav bar, the left drawer, both right-edge widgets (panels and header rows) and the top rail, displacing the `--brown-1` the first four had worn since day one (the rail came from `--grey-4`) (the bar by way of one hour at flat `--grey-4`) — **and, since 2026-08-26, the SIX BOTTOM DOCKS too, through `--plaque-coat` rather than flat** (they spent 08-24 to 08-26 on the flat `#FCF3E0`, which left 1.03:1 across the one seam where it showed: the weld to the bar). First time this tone coats something that is **not a card**, and chrome is where the objection that minted it cannot apply: `--brown-1` read as *the crown strip's plaque borrowed* when a card wore it, but these surfaces **are** the platform, so here the tone says "the platform's own warm sheet". At four surfaces it also buys back what flat brown-1 had and the bar-alone-in-cream hour did not — the window's edges are ONE material again. **THE PUBLIC FEED'S POST CARD COAT** (2026-08-07, user ask) — and the first member of **CREAM, the only family in `_tokens.scss` that is NOT a Quasar ramp and the only one named by WORD instead of index**. Both are deliberate: this tone is hand-picked, has no ramp behind it, and members arrive one at a time as they are needed, so a new one is **`--<modifier>-cream`** and the modifier says where it sits — never `--cream-N`, because an index promises a ramp and inventing the missing steps to justify one is how a palette grows tones nothing wears. Its job: a WARM sheet on the `--grey-4` plate, one step above its bed and separating by HUE as well as by lightness, which is the arrangement the cards have had since 2026-08-06 with the tone under it moving three times — `--grey-3` → `--brown-1` → here. The hour on `--brown-1` is what earned the new token: brown-1 is the CROWN STRIP's plaque and already stands on the feed as the head box's inner posts' motif, so a card wearing it read as that plaque BORROWED rather than as a card with a coat of its own. This is yellower and lighter — a paper cream where brown-1 is a greyed one. Everything else on the card stays grey (eight `--grey-6` lines, `--grey-9` inks, the pit's `--grey-1` floor, `--grey-3` chips in a `--grey-4` tray), so the feed reads as four materials, one job each: grey plate, indigo plaques, near-white pit, cream sheet. |
| `--halo-yellow` / `--halo-yellow-rgb` | Return halo | luminous yellow "you came back from here" glow (`.nav-return-halo`) |
| `--dock-bar-h` | Height of the dock header bar (`.dock-bar`) **and** of the parked stack/pins head glyph (`.dock-side-head`) that stands in for it — equal by construction, so the list box keeps its offset across presentations | 30 px |
| `--side-item-h` / `--side-item-gap` | The stack/pins **item rhythm**, shared by both faces of `SidePanelItem` (parked icon key + expanded informative row) and by both lists' `gap` (2026-07-24) — the row's text is compacted and the chip grown to meet this height, so a list unravels **in place** instead of re-flowing | 36 / 4 px |
| `--radius-sm/md/lg` | Corner radii | 4 / 8 / 14 px |
| `--shadow-carved` | Inner shadow style | for `@carved` |
| `--shadow-side-edge` | **Left-edge cast of the right-edge column** (2026-07-24) — worn by `StackPanel` + `PinsDrawer` in **both** presentations; no vertical offset, so the right-edge column reads as one raised strip lying on top of the page. **The nav bar's `.tack-slot` and `.burger-slot` dropped it on 2026-08-02** — an inward cast is ink painted onto the bar's own plaque right where the block meets it, which both darkened the bar (it is meant to read as the same flat material as the left drawer) and made each 41px block look ~5px wider than the 42px column above it | `-5px 0 12px rgba(var(--ink-rgb-deep), 0.16)` |

Legacy purple-on-dark tokens (`#6C63FF`, `#00BCD4`, `#0D0D1A`) have been removed. Any remaining references to them are dead code awaiting cleanup.

### Common compound styles

- `.pathos-card` — translucent paper surface with `@glass` + soft border
- `.maker-overlay` + `.maker-dialog` — full-page overlay for any maker
- `.interaction-bar` — bottom bar on cards (vote / comment / fork buttons)
- `.nav-btn` — bottom-bar button, a Quasar `<q-btn push>` wearing a **light-green-1** (`#f1f8e9`) face; the push preset provides the 3D lip + press animation natively (no fake `--surface-4` ledge); Nasalization face. (Footer `q-btn-group` bundles use **light-blue-1** `#e1f5fe` instead.)
- `.dock-window` + `.dock-bar` (height `--dock-bar-h`) + `.traffic` + `.dock-tabs`/`.dock-tab` — shared chrome for the six bottom-docked windows (thin header — title centred and traffic lights pinned at the LEFT edge desktop+mobile since 2026-08-04, the dots running red-yellow-green close/min/max, the true Mac order — folder-tab strip; the glass shell + 12px backdrop blur it still declares is now only the STACK/PINS widgets' base — the six bottom docks replace it with an opaque `--dock-coat` plaque, which since 2026-08-26 is `--plaque-coat` for all six — the nav bar's own cream-under-veil, so a dock and the chrome it is welded to are one material; ⚠ the dial is a background LAYER LIST from that day, legal only in a `background` shorthand, and the `dock-coat` witness in `fsck --static` fails on the first consumer that treats it as a `<color>` or on either colorway re-declaring it) + the `dock-slide` transition + the `.is-split-left`/`.is-split-right` 50/50 tiling modifiers used during skeleton-builder element capture. `.dock-side-head` (the parked stack/pins head glyph) is `--dock-bar-h` tall too, standing in for the header — brown-8 ink, zero padding, 20px glyph since 2026-07-24 so it fills that band instead of floating in air (the box height is load-bearing for item alignment; trim the glyph, never the box); `.dock-bar--park` makes the whole header bar the park control on those two widgets, which carry **no traffic light** (2026-07-24 — the maker/uploader/builder/label/chat docks keep theirs) and paint their info box in one ink: `--brown-8` for the kind icon + title, `--brown-4` for the count meta. `.dock-bar__action` — a trailing control at the info box's very right end (20px, flat `--brown-8` glyph, no pebble or border, hover tint): today the stack/pins **history** clock button
- `.flyout-window` (2026-08-10) — the grey-3/brown flyout shell, extracted from the element flyout (then `SkeletonFlyout.vue`) the day the dashboard panel arrived so the two boxes would be one recipe rather than two copies of an argument that took a week to settle. **Since the 2026-08-17 fusion the element flyout no longer wears it** (that window is the media family's `--grey-4` metal now), so this block is the DASHBOARD DOCK's alone — it stays here as that shell's recipe and the tone argument's record. (The panel became a DOCK hours later, which leaves the shell itself with one consumer — but the extraction is what let the dock keep the family's COAT anyway, through `.dock-window--dashboard`'s dials and the two hoisted pieces below.) It carries the `--grey-3` plaque with its `--grey-4` border, `--radius-lg` and full drop; the head (`.dock-bar` restated in the plaque's own tone with no rule under it, `.traffic` pinned `left: 8px`, title centred, symmetric 56px side padding that clears the cluster at BOTH dot sizes, brown-8 glyph/title + brown-4 meta ink); `.flyout-window__frieze` (the `slim` FriezeBar on a `--grey-9` base, which inverts the motif so the brown waves read as light on a dark strip); and `.flyout-window__well` (the sunk `--grey-4` floor, bevelled `--grey-5` top/left and `--brown-1` bottom/right). ⚠ **those last two are TOP-LEVEL rules, not children of `.flyout-window`** — they were nested for the length of one pass and came out the moment the dashboard panel stopped being a flyout and became a dock, since `.dock-window--dashboard` wears the creation shell with these two pieces INSIDE it and a band that only existed as a child of the flyout plaque could not follow. Neither rule ever depended on the parent, which is what made the nesting a convenience and the hoist free. **GEOMETRY IS NOT IN IT** — every flyout is placed by its host (`.feed-flyout` on FeedPage, `.dashboard-flyout` on MainLayout), which is also who owns maximize; the shell is `height: 100%` on a flex column and fills what it is handed. The TONE prose stays in SkeletonFlyout's style block, which is the family's record. **Under 600px its traffic dots grow 13px → 17px on an 8px gap — ONE rule shared with `.dock-window--creation`** (2026-08-10, user ask "make sure the traffic lights look bigger on mobile"), so the two families can never disagree about what a light is worth under a thumb; a flyout is if anything the more pressing case, overlaying the full width down there with its two dots the only way out besides an Escape key a phone does not have.
- `.nav-return-halo` — luminous yellow double-pulse applied by `useStateHolder` to the `[data-nav-focus]` element matching the route the user just returned from (`MicroChip`, `InfoChip`, `MiniPanel`, `HashLink` all stamp `data-nav-focus` with their route)
- `.md-rendered` — carved-paper markdown body used by `PostViewerPage`, `NodeDetailPage`, `SkeletonPage` (docs viewer)

## Routing + auth

`router/routes.js` plus `router/index.js` (the guard). The guard:
- Allows unauthenticated visits to `/auth/*`.
- All other routes require `auth.store.token`. On entry it triggers `/api/identity/verify` (single check on app boot via `App.vue`). On 401 the api.js interceptor clears localStorage and routes to `/#/auth`.

## API connection

`src/services/api.js` reads `process.env.API_URL` (default: `http://localhost:3000/api`). Set via `quasar.config.js` `build.env.API_URL` for different environments.

## Keylogger stub

The typing session keylogger for NOTE nodes is stubbed at the data model:
- DB: `node` table has `content TEXT` and `ancestor_id` for versions.
- A `KEYSTROKES` skeleton kind is *planned* but no service writes to it (see [docs/concepts/skeletons.md](../../docs/concepts/skeletons.md) and [docs/gaps/contradictions.md](../../docs/gaps/contradictions.md) #10).
- UI: the makers' note editors (`MakerDock`, `UploaderDock`'s note section) do not yet track keystrokes.
